## Protocols

Every protocol the project ran, in the order it was used at the bench. Each one
opens to the full write-up: materials, safety classifications, the numbered
procedure, the notes the team added after a run, and the calculations behind
the numbers in the tables.

The tables that were left blank in our written protocols are **live on this
page** — change a value in a box and the shaded cells recalculate, so a run can
be planned without opening anything else. Each of those tables also names the
tab of our `Protocol_Calculators.xlsx` workbook it came from, for anyone
working from our own copy.

## A. Core Methods

The methods every workstream depends on. *P. vulgatus* is handled only inside
the anaerobic chamber, so the chamber rules are part of the culture protocol
rather than a separate page.

### Cell Culture

<ExpandableText title={<><em>E. coli</em> Aerobic Culture</>}>

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/e-coli-aerobic-culture-complete.pdf" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the routine aerobic handling of the *Escherichia coli*
strains used in the project: streaking from a cryostock, growing an overnight
culture, subculturing to a defined growth phase and measuring the optical
density. It is the shared basis for the cloning, transformation and conjugation
work, and is referred to from those protocols rather than repeated in them.

#### Materials

**Strains**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| *E. coli* cloning or donor strain | Cryostock or plate; DH5α and S17 λ pir were used |

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| LB broth, sterile | 3–5 mL per overnight culture (see the protocol "Preparing LB Broth and Agar Plates") |
| LB agar plates with the appropriate antibiotic | One per strain |
| Antibiotic stock solution | Appropriate to the plasmid: kanamycin 50 µg/mL or ampicillin 100 µg/mL |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | For disinfection of the work surface |
| Ice | For transport of the cryostocks |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Culture tubes | With a volume of at least four times the culture volume |
| Inoculation loops or sterile pipette tips | One per strain |
| Cuvettes | For the optical density |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | As required |
| Insulated box | For the cryostocks |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Shaking incubator | 37 °C, 180–250 rpm |
| Static incubator | 37 °C, for the plates |
| Spectrophotometer | Measurement at 600 nm |
| Freezer | −80 °C |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kanamycin sulfate | 25389-94-0 | Danger. H360D: may damage the unborn child (Repr. 1B). | Wear gloves, lab coat and safety glasses. Weigh powder in a fume hood. Not to be handled during pregnancy. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-c) |
| Ampicillin sodium salt | 69-52-3 | Danger. H317: may cause an allergic skin reaction. H334: may cause allergy or asthma symptoms if inhaled. | Avoid dust formation and inhalation. Not to be handled by persons with a penicillin allergy. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | 64-17-5 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Keep away from open flames and hot surfaces. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) SDS of absolute ethanol |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Streaking from a cryostock**

1. Take the cryostock from the −80 °C freezer in an insulated box with ice and
   keep it frozen.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The cryostock must not thaw. Repeated thawing reduces the viability of the stock.

</ProtocolNote>

2. Scrape a small amount from the frozen surface with a sterile inoculation
   loop or pipette tip and streak it onto an LB agar plate with the appropriate
   antibiotic.
3. Return the cryostock to the −80 °C freezer immediately.
4. Incubate the plate overnight at 37 °C and check the next day that the
   colonies are uniform in size, shape and colour.

<ProtocolNote>

The plate is kept until the work is finished, so that contamination can be
traced back if it appears later.

</ProtocolNote>

**Overnight culture**

5. Inoculate 3 to 5 mL of LB broth containing the appropriate antibiotic with a
   single colony.
6. Incubate overnight at 37 °C with shaking at 180 to 250 rpm.

<ProtocolNote>

A tube with a volume of at least four times the culture volume gives the
aeration the culture needs.

</ProtocolNote>

**Subculture and growth phase**

7. Dilute the overnight culture into fresh medium with antibiotic at the
   dilution required by the downstream protocol, and grow at 37 °C with shaking.

<ProtocolNote>

The dilutions used in this project are 1/100 for the conjugation donor, which
reaches an OD₆₀₀ of approximately 0.2 in about two hours, and 1/100 for the
preparation of competent cells, which is grown to an OD₆₀₀ of 0.4.

</ProtocolNote>

8. Follow the growth by measuring the OD₆₀₀ against uninoculated medium as the
   blank.

<ProtocolNote>

Readings above approximately 0.8 fall outside the linear range of most
spectrophotometers; a dense culture is diluted in medium and the reading
multiplied by the dilution factor.

</ProtocolNote>

9. Use the culture in the downstream protocol as soon as the required optical
   density is reached.

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The donor strains grew more slowly than expected at a 1/1000 dilution during
the conjugation work, which is why a 1/100 dilution is used (see the protocol
"Conjugation of pNBU2 Plasmids into *P. vulgatus*").

</ProtocolNote>

#### References

- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-a). *Ampicillin sodium salt* (Product No. A9518) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 6, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sial/a9518
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-b). *Ethanol absolute for analysis EMSURE* (Product No. 1.00983) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 6, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/mm/100983
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-c). *Kanamycin sulfate from Streptomyces kanamyceticus* (Product No. K1377) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 6, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/k1377

<ExpandableText title={<><em>P. vulgatus</em> Anaerobic Culture and Chamber Handling</>}>

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/p-vulgatus-anaerobic-culture-and-chamber-handling-1.pdf" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the routine anaerobic handling of *Phocaeicola
vulgatus* DSM1447 and the rules for working in the anaerobic chamber. The two
are combined because the handling of the organism and the handling of the
chamber cannot be separated in practice.

The chamber section records the safety training for the laboratory and the
practical arrangements for access, emergencies, waste and personal protective
equipment.

#### Materials

**Strains**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| *P. vulgatus* DSM1447 | Cryostock or plate |

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| mGAM broth and agar plates | See the protocol "Preparation of mGAM Broth and Agar Plates" |
| Minimal medium | See the protocol "Preparation of Minimal Medium for *P. vulgatus*" |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | For disinfection of the work surface and of material entering the chamber |
| Ice | For transport of the cryostocks |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Culture tubes | One per culture |
| Inoculation loops or needles, sterile | One per strain |
| Cuvettes | For the optical density |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | As required |
| Insulated box | For the cryostocks and for transport between chambers |
| Waste bag | Taken into the chamber with the material |
| Tissues | For disinfection |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Anaerobic chamber with airlock | 37 °C |
| Incubator inside the chamber | 37 °C |
| Spectrophotometer or plate reader | Measurement at 600 nm |
| Laminar airflow cabinet | For plate preparation, booked in advance |
| Autoclave | With a logbook that is filled in at every use |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Safety goggles | Recommended in the laboratory |
| Insulated gloves | For material stored at −80 °C |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

The chamber operates under an oxygen-free atmosphere and is entered only
through the airlock. Laboratory coats are worn closed and gloves are worn at
all times; a door is never opened while wearing gloves. The work takes place in
an L2 laboratory, although the organisms handled are risk group 1 and no
material of a higher containment level is brought in.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | 64-17-5 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Keep away from open flames and hot surfaces. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.) SDS of absolute ethanol |
| *P. vulgatus* DSM1447 and transconjugants | n/a | Biological agent, risk group 1. Transconjugants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Access and preparation**

1. Book the chamber in advance in the shared reservation calendar, and the
   laminar airflow cabinet separately if plates are to be poured.
2. Prepare and autoclave the medium before the session, and fill in the
   autoclave logbook.
3. Collect everything needed for the session before entering: sterile loops or
   needles, pipettes and tips, a waste bag, 70% ethanol and tissues.

<ProtocolNote>

Material is brought in through the airlock in one go, so that the airlock is
cycled as few times as possible.

</ProtocolNote>

4. Leave personal items outside the laboratory as instructed, and keep a
   laboratory coat for the anaerobic laboratory only.

<ProtocolNote>

Someone must be present in the laboratory at all times during the working hours
of the building. Headphones may be worn, provided that people around remain
audible.

</ProtocolNote>

**Streaking from a cryostock**

5. Collect the cryostock from the −80 °C freezer immediately before the session
   and keep it in an insulated box with ice.
6. Take the cryostock into the chamber through the airlock together with the
   rest of the material.
7. Scrape the frozen surface with a sterile inoculation loop and streak an mGAM
   agar plate, using a pattern that gives single colonies.
8. Return the cryostock to the −80 °C freezer immediately.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The cryostock must not thaw completely.

</ProtocolNote>

9. Incubate the plate at 37 °C inside the chamber for 24 to 48 hours.

<ProtocolNote>

The plate is checked after two days for purity: uniform morphology and colour,
and no contaminants. It is kept until the work is finished.

</ProtocolNote>

**Liquid culture**

10. Inoculate 2 to 5 mL of medium with two or three colonies and grow overnight
    at 37 °C inside the chamber.

<ProtocolNote>

Two or three colonies are used rather than one, because growth from a single
colony is slower and less reliable than for *E. coli*.

</ProtocolNote>

11. Subculture into fresh medium at the dilution required by the downstream
    protocol and grow to the required optical density.

<ProtocolNote>

For the conjugation work, a 1/50 dilution reaching an OD₆₀₀ of 0.2 to 0.5 was
used, which took three to six hours.

</ProtocolNote>

12. Shake or resuspend the culture immediately before every optical density
    measurement.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

*P. vulgatus* sediments quickly, so a reading taken from a settled culture
underestimates the density.

</ProtocolNote>

**Transfer between chambers**

13. Transfer plates and microtiter plates between chambers in a closed
    insulated container, so that the cultures are not exposed to air.

**Emergencies**

14. In an emergency, alert the responsible person in the laboratory, call the
    internal emergency number and then call an ambulance.
15. If the fire alarm sounds for more than ten seconds, stop the work: close
    experiments, bottles and the chamber, close the windows, leave the lights
    on and follow the exit signs.
16. In the event of a biological spill, leave the room for ten minutes, spray
    tissues with ethanol rather than spraying the spill itself, place the
    tissues on the spill and leave them for 30 minutes.
17. If something breaks, place it in a plastic bag, label the bag with its
    contents and hand it to the responsible person.

<ExpandableText title="Cryostock Generation">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/cryostock-generation-1.pdf" workbookTab="Cryostock" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the long-term storage of bacterial strains as glycerol
cryostocks at −80 °C. Equal volumes of an overnight culture and a 50% glycerol
solution are combined, which gives a final glycerol concentration of 25%.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Broth appropriate to the strain, with the appropriate antibiotic | 2 mL per cryostock; *P. vulgatus* DSM1447 is grown without antibiotic |
| Glycerol, 50% (v/v), sterile | 500 µL per cryovial |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | For disinfection of the work surface |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Cryovials, 2 mL | One per cryostock |
| Sterile tubes, 6–10 mL | One per overnight culture |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | 100–1000 µL |
| Cold box | Taken out of the freezer only at the last moment |
| Permanent marker | For labelling |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Shaking incubator | 37 °C, 200 rpm |
| Anaerobic chamber | For anaerobic strains |
| Freezer | −80 °C |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Insulated gloves | For handling material at −80 °C |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Glycerol | 56-81-5 | Not classified as hazardous. | Spills are slippery: clean up immediately. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | 64-17-5 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Keep away from open flames and hot surfaces. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) SDS of absolute ethanol |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Day 1: overnight culture**

1. Prepare an overnight culture in 2 mL of the medium appropriate to the
   strain, with the corresponding antibiotic.

<ProtocolNote>

No antibiotic is used for *P. vulgatus* DSM1447 without a construct.

</ProtocolNote>

2. Inoculate a single colony under sterile conditions.
3. Incubate overnight at 37 °C with shaking at 200 rpm, aerobically or
   anaerobically according to the strain.

**Day 2: freezing**

4. Label the cryovials with the initials of the person preparing them, the team
   and year, the date, the strain, the medium, the antibiotic and the plasmid
   where applicable.
5. Take the cold box out of the freezer only at the last moment.

<ProtocolNote>

Taking the cold box out late keeps the vials from being chilled slowly, which
reduces viability.

</ProtocolNote>

6. Combine 500 µL of the overnight culture and 500 µL of 50% glycerol in each
   cryovial (Table 2).

<ProtocolNote>

It is practical to remove all the caps first, taking care not to touch the
inside of a cap, so that the vials can be filled without interruption.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The culture is added first, and the vial is placed in the cold box immediately
after the glycerol is added.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Glycerol and culture volumes."
  workbookTab="Cryostock"
  rows={[
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Final volume per cryovial", value: 1000, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "cFinal", label: "Final glycerol concentration", value: 25, unit: "%", input: true },
    { key: "cStock", label: "Glycerol stock concentration", value: 50, unit: "%", input: true },
    { key: "vGlycerol", label: "Glycerol stock to add", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vTotal * (v.cFinal / v.cStock) },
    { key: "vCulture", label: "Overnight culture to add", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vTotal - v.vGlycerol },
    { key: "nVials", label: "Number of cryovials", value: 12, unit: "vials", input: true },
    { key: "glycerolTotal", label: "Glycerol stock required in total", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vGlycerol * v.nVials },
    { key: "cultureTotal", label: "Overnight culture required in total", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vCulture * v.nVials },
  ]}
/>

7. Store the cryovials at −80 °C in the box of the team.

<ProtocolNote>

The position of the box in the freezer is recorded in the team strain list, so
that stocks can be found without searching.

</ProtocolNote>

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="July 2026">

The procedure was set up on 22 July 2026 with the help of the host laboratory.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Glycerol and culture volumes**

*V*glycerol = *V*total × (*c*final / *c*stock)  and  *V*culture = *V*total − *V*glycerol

in which:

- *V*total = final volume per cryovial (µL)
- *c*final = required final glycerol concentration (%)
- *c*stock = concentration of the glycerol stock (%)
- *V*glycerol, *V*culture = volumes of glycerol stock and of culture (µL)

Mixing equal volumes of culture and a 50% glycerol stock gives a final glycerol
concentration of 25%, which is the standard for storage at −80 °C.

**Worked example.** For 1000 µL at 25% glycerol from a 50% stock:
1000 × (25 / 50) = 500 µL of glycerol stock and 500 µL of culture. For 12
cryovials, 6000 µL of each is required.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-a). *Ethanol absolute for analysis EMSURE* (Product No. 1.00983) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/mm/100983
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-b). *Glycerol* (Product No. G5516) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/g5516

</ExpandableText>

### Media Preparation

<ExpandableText title="Preparing LB Broth and Agar Plates">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/preparing-lb-broth-and-agar-plates-3.pdf" workbookTab="LB media" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the preparation of LB (Bertani, 1951) agar plates
supplemented with kanamycin and of sterile LB broth. The quantities given yield
approximately 20 plates (300 mL LB agar in 90 mm Petri dishes) and two flasks
of 250 mL LB broth.

Quantities for other volumes, plate numbers or antibiotics can be calculated
with Tables 2 and 3.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| LB broth with agar (Miller), powder | Sigma-Aldrich (Merck), St. Louis, MO, USA, cat. no. L3147; 12 g for 300 mL (40 g/L; Sigma-Aldrich, n.d.-c) |
| LB broth powder | VWR Chemicals; 6.25 g per 250 mL flask (25 g/L), 12.5 g in total |
| Antibiotic stock solution | Antibiotic of choice. Kanamycin: 50 mg/mL stock in water, filter-sterilized; 300 µL for 300 mL (final concentration 50 µg/mL) |
| Distilled or deionized water | 800 mL in total (300 mL for LB agar, 2 × 250 mL for LB broth) |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | For disinfection of the work surface |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Petri dishes, 90 mm, sterile | 20 |
| Autoclavable flasks or bottles, 500 mL | 3 (1 for LB agar, 2 for LB broth) |
| Measuring cylinder, 500 mL | 1 |
| Weighing boats and spatula | As required |
| Autoclave indicator tape | 1 strip per flask |
| Micropipette (100–1000 µL) and sterile tips | For addition of the antibiotic |
| Permanent marker | For labelling of the plates |
| Plastic bags | For storage of the plates |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Analytical or precision balance | Readability 0.01 g |
| Autoclave | Liquid cycle, 121 °C |
| Oven, incubator or water bath | Set to 50–55 °C, for cooling and holding the molten agar |
| Laminar airflow cabinet | For pouring of the plates |
| Refrigerator | 4 °C, for storage of the plates |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Heat-resistant (thermal) gloves | For handling flasks after autoclaving |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

Additional physical hazards: the autoclave operates at high temperature and
pressure and should only be opened once the cycle has ended and the pressure
has been released. Freshly autoclaved liquids can boil over when moved.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kanamycin sulfate | 25389-94-0 | Danger. H360D: may damage the unborn child (Repr. 1B). | Wear gloves, lab coat and safety glasses. Weigh powder in a fume hood. Not to be handled during pregnancy. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) |
| LB broth with agar (Miller) | Mixture | Not classified as hazardous. | Avoid inhaling dust when weighing. Molten agar causes burns. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-d) |
| LB broth powder | Mixture | Not classified as hazardous. | Avoid inhaling dust when weighing. | Supplier SDS (VWR Chemicals) |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | 64-17-5 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Keep away from open flames and hot surfaces. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) SDS of absolute ethanol |

#### Procedure

**Preparing the LB agar**

1. Add 300 mL of distilled or deionized water to a 500 mL flask.

<ProtocolNote>

It is recommended to add the water before the powder, as this prevents
clumping. The original version of this protocol used 500 mL of medium (20 g of
powder in a 1 L flask), which yielded more medium than required for 20 plates.
A volume of 300–350 mL is recommended for 20 plates.

</ProtocolNote>

2. Weigh 12 g of LB broth with agar powder and add it to the water (Table 2).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Medium powder and plate yield."
  workbookTab="LB media"
  rows={[
    { key: "vAgar", label: "LB agar volume", value: 300, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "cAgar", label: "LB-agar powder per litre (manufacturer: 40 g/L for Sigma L3147)", value: 40, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "mAgar", label: "LB-agar powder to weigh", unit: "g", compute: (v) => (v.cAgar * v.vAgar) / 1000 },
    { key: "vPerPlate", label: "Agar per 90 mm plate", value: 15, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "nPlates", label: "Approximate number of plates", unit: "plates", compute: (v) => Math.floor(v.vAgar / v.vPerPlate) },
    { key: "vBroth", label: "LB broth volume per flask", value: 250, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "cBroth", label: "LB broth powder per litre (LB Miller: 25 g/L)", value: 25, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "mBroth", label: "LB broth powder to weigh per flask", unit: "g", compute: (v) => (v.cBroth * v.vBroth) / 1000 },
  ]}
/>

3. Swirl or stir to mix and partially dissolve the powder.

<ProtocolNote>

Mixing before autoclaving prevents clumping. The agar dissolves completely only
during autoclaving.

</ProtocolNote>

**Preparing the LB broth**

4. Add 250 mL of distilled or deionized water to a 500 mL flask.
5. Weigh 6.25 g of LB broth powder and add it to the water.
6. Swirl or stir to mix and partially dissolve the powder.
7. Repeat steps 4 to 6 for the second flask of LB broth.

<ProtocolNote>

It is recommended to autoclave the LB agar and both flasks of LB broth together
in a single cycle.

</ProtocolNote>

**Autoclaving**

8. Loosen the cap of each flask slightly to allow airflow.

<ProtocolNote>

A loosened cap prevents the flask from bursting as a result of the heat and
pressure.

</ProtocolNote>

9. Attach a strip of autoclave tape to each flask, folding one end over so that
   the tape can be removed easily afterwards.

<ProtocolNote>

The tape shows black stripes once it has been autoclaved.

</ProtocolNote>

10. Check the water level of the autoclave. The level should lie between the
    black marks on the outside of the machine and should reach the bottom of
    the basket on the inside. If the level is too low, add water until the
    marks are reached.
11. Place the flasks in the basket, close the lid by turning it clockwise and
    attach the magnetic pin to the machine.
12. Switch on the power and press start without changing any other setting. The
    medium is sterilized at 121 °C.

<ProtocolNote>

The complete cycle, including heating and cooling, takes 2 to 3 hours.

</ProtocolNote>

13. When the cycle has ended, remove the flasks using thermal gloves. Allow the
    LB broth to cool to room temperature, tighten the caps and store until use.

**Cooling the agar and adding the antibiotic**

14. Cool the LB agar to approximately 50–55 °C using one of the following
    options:
    - Place the flask in the laboratory oven set to 50–55 °C.
    - Place the flask in a water bath at 50–55 °C for 20 to 30 minutes.
    - Leave the flask on the bench for approximately 10 to 20 minutes.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The agar should be warm but still liquid, and comfortable to hold briefly
against the hand or wrist. Antibiotics are degraded when added to agar that is
too hot (above 60 °C), whereas the agar solidifies if the temperature drops too
low. It is recommended to check the temperature of the flask with thermal
gloves before testing it with a bare hand.

</ProtocolNote>

15. Add the antibiotic stock to the required final concentration (Table 3). For
    kanamycin, add 300 µL of a 50 mg/mL stock to 300 mL of LB agar to obtain a
    final concentration of 50 µg/mL (Addgene, n.d.).

<ProtocolNote>

The volume is calculated as V1 = (C2 × V2) / C1, in which C1 is the stock
concentration, C2 the final concentration and V2 the volume of medium. Example:
(0.05 mg/mL × 300 mL) / 50 mg/mL = 0.3 mL = 300 µL.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Antibiotic stock volume (C1 × V1 = C2 × V2)."
  workbookTab="LB media"
  rows={[
    { key: "c1", label: "Antibiotic stock concentration (C1)", value: 50, unit: "mg/mL", input: true },
    { key: "c2", label: "Final concentration in medium (C2)", value: 50, unit: "µg/mL", input: true },
    { key: "v2", label: "Medium volume (V2)", value: 300, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "v1uL", label: "Stock volume to add (V1)", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => (v.c2 * v.v2) / v.c1 },
    { key: "v1mL", label: "Stock volume to add (V1)", unit: "mL", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => v.v1uL / 1000 },
  ]}
/>

16. Swirl gently to mix, avoiding the formation of bubbles.

**Pouring the plates**

17. Transfer the flask and the Petri dishes to a laminar airflow cabinet to
    maintain sterility.
18. Pour agar into each plate until approximately two thirds of the bottom is
    covered, replace the lid and swirl the plate so that the entire bottom is
    covered.

<ProtocolNote>

Several plates can be poured and swirled at the same time.

</ProtocolNote>

19. Open each lid for a few seconds to let the steam escape, then close the
    plate.
20. Leave the plates undisturbed in the laminar airflow cabinet at room
    temperature until fully solidified (30 to 60 minutes).

**Labelling and storage**

21. Label each plate on the bottom, around the edge, with the medium and
    antibiotic, the date and the name of the operator.

<ProtocolNote>

Plates should be labelled on the bottom and not on the lid, because lids can be
exchanged accidentally.

</ProtocolNote>

22. Store the plates upside down in a plastic bag at 4 °C. The plates can be
    kept for 2 to 3 weeks.

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Mass of medium powder**

*m* = (*c* × *V*) / 1000

in which:

- *m* = mass of powder to weigh (g)
- *c* = concentration specified by the manufacturer (g/L)
- *V* = volume of medium (mL)

The factor 1000 converts millilitres to litres.

**Worked example.** For 300 mL of LB agar at 40 g/L: *m* = (40 × 300) / 1000 =
12.00 g. For 250 mL of LB broth at 25 g/L: *m* = (25 × 250) / 1000 = 6.25 g.

**Number of plates**

*n* = *V* / *V*p

in which:

- *n* = number of plates
- *V* = volume of medium (mL)
- *V*p = volume of medium per plate (mL)

The result is rounded down, because a partly filled plate is not usable.

**Worked example.** For 300 mL of agar at 15 mL per 90 mm plate: *n* = 300 / 15
= 20 plates.

**Volume of antibiotic stock**

C1 × V1 = C2 × V2 → V1 = (C2 × V2) / C1

in which:

- C1 = concentration of the stock solution
- V1 = volume of stock solution to add
- C2 = required final concentration
- V2 = final volume of the solution

C1 and C2 must be expressed in the same unit before the values are entered;
1 mg/mL equals 1000 µg/mL.

**Worked example.** For kanamycin at 50 µg/mL in 300 mL of medium, from a
50 mg/mL (50,000 µg/mL) stock: V1 = (50 × 300) / 50,000 = 0.3 mL = 300 µL.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- Addgene. (n.d.). *Pouring LB agar plates*. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.addgene.org/protocols/pouring-lb-agar-plates/
- Bertani, G. (1951). Studies on lysogenesis. I. The mode of phage liberation by lysogenic *Escherichia coli*. *Journal of Bacteriology, 62*(3), 293–300. https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC386127
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-a). *Ethanol absolute for analysis EMSURE* (Product No. 1.00983) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/mm/100983
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-b). *Kanamycin sulfate from Streptomyces kanamyceticus* (Product No. K1377) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/k1377
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-c). *LB broth with agar (Miller)* (Product No. L3147) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/sigma/l3147
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-d). *LB broth with agar (Miller)* (Product No. L3147) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/l3147

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Preparation of mGAM Broth and Agar Plates">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/preparation-of-mgam-broth-and-agar-plates-1.pdf" workbookTab="Media preparation" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the preparation of modified Gifu Anaerobic Medium
(mGAM) as broth and as agar plates. mGAM is the rich medium used for the
routine growth of *Phocaeicola vulgatus* DSM1447 and for the selective plates
used after conjugation.

The medium is prepared from a dehydrated powder according to the instructions
of the manufacturer, autoclaved, and then reduced in the anaerobic chamber
before use.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| mGAM dehydrated powder | 41.7 g per litre according to the manufacturer; the exact value on the label of the batch in use applies (DSMZ, n.d.) |
| Agar | 15 g/L for plates; omitted for broth |
| Distilled or deionized water | As required |
| Antibiotic stock solutions | Added after autoclaving for selective plates |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Autoclavable flasks or bottles | Of at least twice the volume of medium |
| Measuring cylinder | Matching the volume |
| Weighing boats and spatula | As required |
| Petri dishes, 90 mm, sterile | One per 15 mL of agar |
| Autoclave indicator tape | One strip per flask |
| Permanent marker | For labelling |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Precision balance | Readability 0.01 g |
| Autoclave | Liquid cycle |
| Magnetic stirrer with heating, or a water bath | For dissolving the powder |
| Oven or water bath | 50–55 °C, for holding the molten agar |
| Laminar airflow cabinet | For pouring the plates |
| Anaerobic chamber | For reduction and storage of the medium |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Heat-resistant (thermal) gloves | For handling flasks after autoclaving |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

Additional physical hazards: the autoclave operates at high temperature and
pressure; freshly autoclaved liquids can boil over when moved; molten agar
causes burns.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| mGAM dehydrated powder | Mixture | The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Avoid inhaling dust when weighing. | Supplier SDS |
| Agar | 9002-18-0 | Not classified as hazardous. | Molten agar causes burns. | Supplier SDS |
| Kanamycin sulfate | 25389-94-0 | Danger. H360D: may damage the unborn child (Repr. 1B). | Wear gloves, lab coat and safety glasses. Weigh powder in a fume hood. Not to be handled during pregnancy. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.) |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Preparing the medium**

1. Add the required volume of distilled or deionized water to the flask
   (Table 2).
2. Weigh the mGAM powder and add it to the water. For plates, weigh the agar as
   well and add it.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The amount of powder per litre is taken from the label of the batch in use. The
value of 41.7 g/L is the figure given for the modified formulation (DSMZ, n.d.)
and differs between suppliers and formulations.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="mGAM broth and agar."
  workbookTab="Media preparation"
  rows={[
    { key: "vMedium", label: "Volume of medium", value: 1000, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "cPowder", label: "mGAM powder per litre (manufacturer)", value: 41.7, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cAgar", label: "Agar for plates (0 for broth)", value: 15, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "mPowder", label: "mGAM powder to weigh", unit: "g", compute: (v) => (v.cPowder * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mAgar", label: "Agar to weigh", unit: "g", compute: (v) => (v.cAgar * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "vPerPlate", label: "Agar per 90 mm plate", value: 15, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "nPlates", label: "Approximate number of plates", unit: "plates", compute: (v) => Math.floor(v.vMedium / v.vPerPlate) },
  ]}
/>

3. Heat the suspension while stirring until the powder has dissolved.

<ProtocolNote>

The medium is heated to dissolve the powder; the agar dissolves completely only
during autoclaving.

</ProtocolNote>

**Autoclaving**

4. Loosen the cap of the flask, attach a strip of autoclave indicator tape and
   autoclave the medium on a liquid cycle.

<ProtocolNote>

The manufacturer specifies 115 °C for 15 minutes for the modified formulation
(DSMZ, n.d.); the cycle stated on the label of the batch in use applies.

</ProtocolNote>

**Pouring the plates**

5. For plates, cool the agar to approximately 50–55 °C in an oven or water bath.
6. Add the antibiotic stock for selective plates and swirl gently to mix,
   avoiding bubbles.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The agar must be below 60 °C when the antibiotic is added, otherwise the
antibiotic is degraded; above approximately 45 °C it stays liquid. The volumes
for the antibiotics used after conjugation are in the protocol "Conjugation of
pNBU2 Plasmids into *P. vulgatus*".

</ProtocolNote>

7. Pour the plates in a laminar airflow cabinet, at approximately 15 mL per
   90 mm plate, and leave them undisturbed until fully solidified.
8. Label the plates on the bottom with the medium, the antibiotic, the date and
   the name of the operator.

**Reduction and storage**

9. Transfer the broth and the plates into the anaerobic chamber and leave them
   to reduce before use.

<ProtocolNote>

Medium that has been exposed to air carries dissolved oxygen, which delays or
prevents the growth of *P. vulgatus*. Plates and broth are therefore brought
into the chamber in advance rather than immediately before use.

</ProtocolNote>

10. Store the plates upside down at 4 °C in a sealed bag, and check them for
    contamination before use.

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The supplier and catalogue number of the mGAM powder used by the team, and the
mass per litre on that label, still need to be recorded here.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Mass of dehydrated medium and agar**

*m* = (*c* × *V*) / 1000

in which:

- *m* = mass to weigh (g)
- *c* = concentration specified by the manufacturer (g/L)
- *V* = volume of medium (mL)

Agar is added at 15 g/L for plates and omitted for broth. The factor 1000
converts millilitres to litres.

**Worked example.** For 1000 mL of mGAM at 41.7 g/L: *m* = (41.7 × 1000) / 1000
= 41.70 g, plus 15.00 g of agar for plates, which gives about 66 plates of
15 mL.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- DSMZ. (n.d.). *Medium 1715: GAM broth, modified (Gifu Anaerobic Medium Broth, modified)*. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://bacmedia.dsmz.de/medium/1715
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.). *Kanamycin sulfate from Streptomyces kanamyceticus* (Product No. K1377) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/k1377

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Preparation of Wilkins-Chalgren Broth and Agar Plates">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/preparation-of-wilkins-chalgren-broth-and-agar-plates-1.pdf" workbookTab="Media preparation" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the preparation of Wilkins-Chalgren Anaerobe medium as
broth and as agar plates. Wilkins-Chalgren and supplemented brain heart
infusion can be used interchangeably, and the original conjugation protocol is
written for them.

L-cysteine is added as a reducing agent to lower the redox potential of the
medium, and resazurin can be included as a redox indicator: it is colourless
when the medium is reduced and pink when it is not.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Wilkins-Chalgren Anaerobe Broth, dehydrated | 33 g per litre (DSMZ, n.d.) |
| L-cysteine hydrochloride | 0.3 g per litre (DSMZ, n.d.) |
| Resazurin solution, 0.1% (w/v), optional | 0.5 mL per litre, as a redox indicator (DSMZ, n.d.) |
| Agar | 15 g/L for plates; omitted for broth |
| Distilled or deionized water | As required |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Autoclavable flasks or bottles | Of at least twice the volume of medium |
| Measuring cylinder, weighing boats and spatula | As required |
| Petri dishes, 90 mm, sterile | One per 15 mL of agar |
| Autoclave indicator tape | One strip per flask |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Precision balance | Readability 0.001 g for the cysteine |
| Autoclave | Liquid cycle |
| Magnetic stirrer with heating | For dissolving the powder |
| pH meter | Target pH 6.8–7.0 |
| Laminar airflow cabinet | For pouring the plates |
| Anaerobic chamber | For reduction and storage |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Heat-resistant (thermal) gloves | For handling flasks after autoclaving |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

Additional physical hazards: the autoclave operates at high temperature and
pressure; molten agar causes burns.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Wilkins-Chalgren Anaerobe Broth, dehydrated | Mixture | Not classified as hazardous; the SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Avoid inhaling dust when weighing. | Thermo Fisher Scientific (n.d.) |
| L-Cysteine hydrochloride, anhydrous | 52-89-1 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Avoid dust formation when weighing. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) |
| Resazurin, sodium salt | 62758-13-8 | Warning. H315: causes skin irritation. H319: causes serious eye irritation. H335: may cause respiratory irritation. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. Avoid dust formation when weighing. The dye stains. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-c) |
| Agar | 9002-18-0 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Molten agar causes burns. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Preparing the medium**

1. Dissolve the dehydrated medium in distilled or deionized water (Table 2),
   together with the resazurin solution if a redox indicator is wanted. For
   plates, add the agar as well.

<ProtocolNote>

The manufacturer specifies 33 g of powder per litre of warm distilled water
(Thermo Fisher Scientific, 2024). The medium already contains haemin at
0.005 g/L and menadione at 0.0005 g/L, alongside tryptone, gelatine peptone,
yeast extract, glucose, sodium chloride, L-arginine and sodium pyruvate (Thermo
Fisher Scientific, 2024); no separate haemin or vitamin K supplement is
therefore needed.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Wilkins-Chalgren broth and agar."
  workbookTab="Media preparation"
  rows={[
    { key: "vMedium", label: "Volume of medium", value: 1000, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "cPowder", label: "WC powder per litre (manufacturer)", value: 33, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cCys", label: "L-cysteine HCl", value: 0.3, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cAgar", label: "Agar for plates (0 for broth)", value: 15, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "mPowder", label: "WC powder to weigh", unit: "g", compute: (v) => (v.cPowder * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mCys", label: "L-cysteine HCl to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.cCys * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mAgar", label: "Agar to weigh", unit: "g", compute: (v) => (v.cAgar * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
  ]}
/>

2. Add the L-cysteine hydrochloride.

<ProtocolNote>

L-cysteine is the reducing agent that lowers the redox potential of the medium.
It also supplies sulfur, but that is not the reason it is added here.

</ProtocolNote>

**Autoclaving and pouring**

3. Loosen the cap, attach a strip of autoclave indicator tape and autoclave the
   medium at 121 °C for 15 minutes (Thermo Fisher Scientific, 2024).
4. Check the pH after autoclaving and adjust it to 6.8–7.0 if necessary (DSMZ,
   n.d.).

<ProtocolNote>

The pH is measured on a small aliquot rather than with the probe in the bottle
of medium, so that the bottle is not contaminated.

</ProtocolNote>

5. For plates, cool the agar to approximately 50–55 °C, add any antibiotic,
   pour the plates in a laminar airflow cabinet and let them solidify.
6. Label the plates on the bottom with the medium, the antibiotic, the date and
   the name of the operator.

**Reduction and storage**

7. Transfer the broth and the plates into the anaerobic chamber and leave them
   to reduce before use.

<ProtocolNote>

If resazurin was included, the medium is reduced when it has lost its pink
colour.

</ProtocolNote>

8. Store the plates upside down at 4 °C in a sealed bag, and check them for
   contamination before use.

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The team used mGAM rather than Wilkins-Chalgren throughout the conjugation
work. This protocol is written so that the original conjugation protocol can be
followed as published if the medium is ever needed.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The manufacturer formula shows that this medium already contains haemin and
menadione, which is worth knowing when comparing it with the minimal medium,
where both have to be supplied separately.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

CAS 52-89-1 is the anhydrous L-cysteine hydrochloride. If the bottle in the
laboratory is the monohydrate, the CAS number is 7048-04-6 and the mass per
litre differs by about 10%.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Mass of dehydrated medium, cysteine and agar**

*m* = (*c* × *V*) / 1000

in which:

- *m* = mass to weigh (g)
- *c* = concentration specified by the manufacturer or the recipe (g/L)
- *V* = volume of medium (mL)

**Worked example.** For 1000 mL of Wilkins-Chalgren at 33 g/L: 33.00 g of
powder, 0.300 g of L-cysteine HCl and, for plates, 15.00 g of agar.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- DSMZ. (n.d.). *Medium 339: Wilkins-Chalgren Anaerobe Broth*. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://mediadive.dsmz.de/medium/339
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-a). *Agar* (Product No. A1296) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/a1296
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-b). *L-Cysteine hydrochloride, anhydrous* (Product No. C1276) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/c1276
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-c). *Resazurin sodium salt* (Product No. R7017) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/r7017
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (n.d.). *Wilkins-Chalgren Anaerobe Broth, dehydrated* (Cat. No. CM0643B) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.fishersci.ie/store/msds?partNumber=10689744&countryCode=IE&language=en
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (2024). *Wilkins Chalgren Anaerobe Broth, dehydrated* (Cat. No. CM0643B) (Document No. MBD_BT_IFU-0559, Version 2.0) [Instructions for use]. https://assets.fishersci.com/TFS-Assets/MBD/Instructions/MBD-BT-IFU-0559%20CM0643R%20WILKINS%20CHALGREN%20ANAEROBE%20BROTH

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title={<>Preparation of Minimal Medium for <em>P. vulgatus</em> (anaerobic, with hemin)</>}>

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/preparation-of-minimal-medium-for-p-vulgatus-2.pdf" workbookTab="Media preparation" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the preparation of the anaerobic minimal medium used to
grow *Phocaeicola vulgatus* DSM1447, as broth and as agar plates. It is a
simplified medium: the LB component is added at half the concentration normally
recommended, which keeps the medium minimal while still supporting growth.

The project uses two different minimal media, and they are not interchangeable.
This one is the anaerobic medium for *P. vulgatus* and contains hemin; it is
used for the growth curves and the medium comparison. The other is the aerobic
minimal medium for *E. coli* used in the liquid bile salt assay, which has the
same LB, sodium bicarbonate, glucose and L-cysteine content but no hemin,
because *E. coli* makes its own heme (see the protocol "Liquid Bile Salt
Assay"). Hemin is the component that decides whether *P. vulgatus* grows at
all, so the *E. coli* medium cannot be used in its place.

Each component has a defined role. LB, being tryptone, yeast extract and sodium
chloride, supplies peptides, amino acids and B vitamins, and is on its own an
adequate carbon and nitrogen source, because *P. vulgatus* is not dependent on
glucose for peptide catabolism. Glucose adds a rapidly fermentable sugar on top
of that, which supports faster growth and the production of acid and succinate.
Hemin is the component that decides whether the organism grows at all:
*Bacteroides* and *Phocaeicola* species cannot synthesize heme and need it from
the medium for their cytochromes and other heme-dependent enzymes, so growth is
poor or absent without it. L-cysteine is present as a reducing agent that
lowers the redox potential of the medium, which anaerobes require, rather than
as a nutrient. Sodium bicarbonate buffers the medium against the organic acids
produced during growth and supplies the carbon dioxide and bicarbonate used in
carboxylation reactions.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| LB powder | 10 g per litre, which is half the 20 g/L normally recommended |
| Sodium bicarbonate | 1.25 g per litre |
| Glucose | 0.2 g per litre |
| L-cysteine | 0.5 g per litre |
| Hemin | 0.01 g per litre, added separately after autoclaving |
| Sodium hydroxide solution | For dissolving the hemin and for adjusting the pH |
| Agar | 15 g/L (1.5%) for plates; omitted for broth |
| Distilled or deionized water | As required |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Autoclavable bottles or flasks | Of at least twice the volume of medium |
| Measuring cylinder, weighing boats and spatula | As required |
| Tubes for the pH aliquots | One per batch |
| Syringe filters, 0.22 µm, with syringes | For the hemin stock |
| Petri dishes, 90 mm, sterile | One per 15 mL of agar |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Analytical balance | Readability 0.1 mg for the hemin |
| Autoclave | Liquid cycle |
| pH meter | Target pH 6.5 |
| Magnetic stirrer | For dissolving the components |
| Laminar airflow cabinet | For pouring the plates |
| Anaerobic chamber | For reduction and storage |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Heat-resistant (thermal) gloves | For handling bottles after autoclaving |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

Sodium hydroxide is corrosive and is handled with gloves and eye protection.
Additional physical hazards: the autoclave operates at high temperature and
pressure; molten agar causes burns.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sodium hydroxide solution | 1310-73-2 | Danger. H314: causes severe skin burns and eye damage. H290: may be corrosive to metals. | Wear gloves and eye protection. Add base to water, never the reverse. | Supplier SDS |
| Hemin | 16009-13-5 | The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Avoid dust formation when weighing. Light sensitive. | Supplier SDS |
| Sodium bicarbonate | 144-55-8 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Standard laboratory practice. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-c) Product page |
| D-(+)-Glucose | 50-99-7 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Standard laboratory practice. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) Product page |
| L-Cysteine | 52-90-4 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Standard laboratory practice. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) Product page |
| LB powder | Mixture | Not classified as hazardous. | Avoid inhaling dust when weighing. | Supplier SDS |
| Agar | 9002-18-0 | Not classified as hazardous. | Molten agar causes burns. | Supplier SDS |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Preparing the base medium**

1. Weigh the LB powder, glucose, sodium bicarbonate and L-cysteine for the
   intended volume (Table 2) and dissolve them in distilled or deionized water.

<ProtocolNote>

The LB component is added at 10 g/L, which is half the 20 g/L normally
recommended. *P. vulgatus* grows well on this, and the lower concentration
keeps the medium minimal.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title={<>Minimal medium for <em>P. vulgatus</em>.</>}
  workbookTab="Media preparation"
  rows={[
    { key: "vMedium", label: "Volume of medium", value: 1000, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "cLB", label: "LB powder (half the recommended 20 g/L)", value: 10, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cBicarb", label: "Sodium bicarbonate", value: 1.25, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cGlucose", label: "Glucose", value: 0.2, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cCys", label: "L-cysteine", value: 0.5, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cHemin", label: "Hemin (added separately after autoclaving)", value: 0.01, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cAgar", label: "Agar for plates (0 for broth)", value: 15, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "mLB", label: "LB powder to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.cLB * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mBicarb", label: "Sodium bicarbonate to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.cBicarb * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mGlucose", label: "Glucose to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.cGlucose * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mCys", label: "L-cysteine to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.cCys * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mHemin", label: "Hemin to weigh", unit: "mg", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.cHemin * v.vMedium },
    { key: "mAgar", label: "Agar to weigh", unit: "g", compute: (v) => (v.cAgar * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
  ]}
/>

2. For plates, add the agar at 15 g/L (1.5%) at this stage.
3. Autoclave the medium on a liquid cycle.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The hemin is not added before autoclaving; it is prepared separately and added
afterwards.

</ProtocolNote>

**Adjusting the pH**

4. After autoclaving, adjust the pH to 6.5 with sodium hydroxide.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The pH is measured on a small aliquot taken from the batch, never with the
probe in the bottle of medium itself. A value close to 6.5 is acceptable, but
the pH must not fall below 5.5, because *P. vulgatus* is very sensitive to a
low pH.

</ProtocolNote>

**Preparing and adding the hemin**

5. Dissolve the hemin in sodium hydroxide to make a stock solution, and
   filter-sterilize it (Table 3).

<ProtocolNote>

Hemin does not dissolve in water, which is why it is dissolved in sodium
hydroxide first. The stock is light sensitive and is kept dark.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Hemin stock solution."
  workbookTab="Media preparation"
  rows={[
    { key: "cStock", label: "Stock concentration", value: 1, unit: "mg/mL", input: true },
    { key: "vStock", label: "Stock volume", value: 10, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "mHemin", label: "Hemin to weigh", unit: "mg", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.cStock * v.vStock },
    { key: "cFinal", label: "Final concentration in the medium", value: 10, unit: "mg/L", input: true },
    { key: "vMedium", label: "Volume of medium", value: 1000, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "vAdd", label: "Stock to add to the medium", unit: "mL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.cFinal * (v.vMedium / 1000)) / v.cStock },
  ]}
/>

6. Add the hemin stock to the autoclaved medium to a final concentration of
   0.01 g/L and mix.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

Without hemin the medium does not support growth: *P. vulgatus* cannot
synthesize heme and depends on an external supply.

</ProtocolNote>

**Pouring plates and storage**

7. For plates, pour the medium in a laminar airflow cabinet at approximately
   15 mL per 90 mm plate once the agar has cooled to 50–55 °C and the hemin has
   been added, and let the plates solidify.
8. Label the plates on the bottom with the medium, the date and the name of the
   operator.
9. Transfer the broth and the plates into the anaerobic chamber and leave them
   to reduce before use.
10. Store the plates upside down at 4 °C in a sealed bag, and check them for
    contamination before use.

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The composition in this protocol is the simplified medium used by the team,
with LB at half the normally recommended concentration. *P. vulgatus* DSM1447
grows well on it.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

A batch prepared on 9 September 2026 was brought to the correct pH but was not
left to reduce overnight in the chamber and was used nonetheless. Medium that
has not been reduced should be expected to delay growth.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The minimal medium in the liquid bile salt assay is a different medium, for
*E. coli* and without hemin. The similarity of the two recipes has caused
confusion before; the medium is named by its organism everywhere it is
mentioned, for that reason.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Mass of each component of the minimal medium**

*m* = (*c* × *V*) / 1000

in which:

- *m* = mass to weigh (g)
- *c* = concentration of the component (g/L)
- *V* = volume of medium (mL)

Hemin is weighed in milligrams because its concentration is a hundredfold lower
than that of the other components: *m*(mg) = *c*(g/L) × *V*(mL). It is
dissolved separately in sodium hydroxide and added after autoclaving.

**Worked example.** For 1000 mL: LB 10.000 g, sodium bicarbonate 1.250 g,
glucose 0.200 g, L-cysteine 0.500 g and hemin 10.0 mg, plus 15.00 g of agar for
plates.

**Hemin stock and the volume to add**

*m* = *c* × *V*  and  *V*stock = (*c*final × *V*medium) / *c*stock

in which:

- *m* = hemin to weigh (mg)
- *c* = stock concentration (mg/mL)
- *V* = stock volume (mL)
- *c*final = required concentration in the medium (mg/L)
- *V*medium = volume of medium (L)
- *V*stock = volume of stock to add (mL)

Hemin does not dissolve in water and is first dissolved in sodium hydroxide.

**Worked example.** For 10 mL of a 1 mg/mL stock: 10.0 mg of hemin. To reach
10 mg/L in 1000 mL of medium: (10 × 1) / 1 = 10.0 mL of stock.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-a). *D-(+)-Glucose* (Product No. G7021) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/sigma/g7021
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-b). *L-Cysteine* (Product No. 168149) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/aldrich/168149
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-c). *Sodium bicarbonate* (Product No. S5761) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/sigma/s5761

</ExpandableText>

## B. Bile Salt Hydrolase Characterization

The route from a BSH coding sequence to a measured activity: assembling the
construct, verifying it by colony PCR, gel and sequencing, and then measuring
deconjugation in the liquid bile salt assay. Several of these protocols are
used again in the toolkit workstream, where they are cross-referenced rather
than repeated.

### Construct Assembly

<ExpandableText title="Plasmid Miniprep: Monarch Kit">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/monarch-plasmid-miniprep-kit-protocol.pdf" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the isolation of plasmid DNA from overnight bacterial
cultures with the Monarch Spin Plasmid Miniprep Kit (New England Biolabs,
n.d.-a). The kit is supplied with a detailed protocol card; this document
serves as preparation before the work is started.

#### Attributions

This protocol is a rewritten version of the manufacturer's protocol (New
England Biolabs, n.d.-b), which is available online: Plasmid Miniprep Protocol
using Centrifugation (NEB #T1110).

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol for the isolation of BSH
plasmids from *E. coli* DH5α for sequencing, for the extraction of the pNBU2
plasmid from *E. coli* S17 for downstream promoter library generation, and for
sequencing validation of the promoter library constructs.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Overnight bacterial cultures | 1–5 mL per preparation (New England Biolabs, n.d.-b) |
| Monarch Spin Plasmid Miniprep Kit | New England Biolabs, cat. no. T1110 |
| Monarch Buffer B1 (pink, resuspension) | 200 µL per preparation; stored in the refrigerator |
| Monarch Buffer B2 (blue, lysis) | 200 µL per preparation |
| Monarch Buffer B3 (yellow, neutralization) | 400 µL per preparation |
| Monarch Buffer BZ (wash 1) | 200 µL per preparation |
| Monarch Buffer WZ (wash 2) | 400 µL per preparation |
| Monarch Buffer EY (elution) | 30 µL per preparation |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Monarch Spin Columns and Collection Tubes | 1 per preparation (supplied with the kit) |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL | 2 per preparation |
| Micropipettes and tips | 20–200 µL and 100–1000 µL |
| Timer | 1 |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Microcentrifuge | 16,000 × g |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

The compositions of the kit buffers are proprietary; the hazardous components
named below are those disclosed by the manufacturer (New England Biolabs,
n.d.-c).

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Monarch Buffer B2 | Mixture | Contains sodium hydroxide and SDS. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. Avoid contact with skin and eyes. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| Monarch Buffers B3 and BZ | Mixture | Contain guanidine hydrochloride (B3) and guanidine with isopropanol (BZ). | Wear gloves and safety glasses. Flow-through should not be mixed with bleach. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| Monarch Buffer WZ | Mixture | Contains ethanol. | Keep away from ignition sources. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

#### Procedure

**Harvesting the cells**

1. Pellet the overnight culture by centrifugation at 16,000 × g for 30 seconds
   and discard the supernatant.

<ProtocolNote>

With older centrifuges, centrifugation at 8000 × g for 8 minutes is sufficient.

</ProtocolNote>

**Lysis and neutralization**

2. Resuspend the pellet in 200 µL of Monarch Buffer B1 (pink) and transfer the
   suspension to a 1.5 mL microcentrifuge tube.

<ProtocolNote>

Buffer B1 should be kept in the refrigerator before and after use.

</ProtocolNote>

3. Add 200 µL of Monarch Buffer B2 (blue), gently invert the tube 4 to 5 times
   and incubate for 1 minute at room temperature.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The tube should not be vortexed. The colour of the suspension should change to
dark pink. The incubation should not exceed 1 minute (New England Biolabs,
n.d.-b).

</ProtocolNote>

4. Add 400 µL of Monarch Buffer B3 (yellow), gently invert the tube until the
   suspension is neutralized and incubate for 2 minutes.

<ProtocolNote>

The suspension is neutralized when it is uniformly yellow.

</ProtocolNote>

5. Centrifuge the lysate at 16,000 × g for 5 minutes.

**Binding and washing**

6. Transfer the supernatant to a Monarch Spin Column and centrifuge at
   16,000 × g for 1 minute. Discard the flow-through.

<ProtocolNote>

The plasmid DNA is bound to the column membrane at this stage.

</ProtocolNote>

7. Re-insert the column into the collection tube, add 200 µL of Monarch Buffer
   BZ (wash 1) and centrifuge for 1 minute.
8. Add 400 µL of Monarch Buffer WZ (wash 2) and centrifuge at 16,000 × g for
   1 minute.

**Elution**

9. Transfer the column to a clean 1.5 mL microcentrifuge tube.
10. Add 30 µL of Monarch Buffer EY to the centre of the membrane, wait for
    1 minute and centrifuge for 1 minute to elute the DNA.

#### References

- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-a). *Monarch Spin Plasmid Miniprep Kit* (NEB #T1110) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/products/t1110-monarch-spin-plasmid-miniprep-kit
- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-b). *Plasmid miniprep protocol using centrifugation* (NEB #T1110). Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/protocols/plasmid-miniprep-protocol-using-centrifugation-t1110
- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-c). *What is the composition of each buffer provided with the Monarch Spin Plasmid Miniprep Kit (NEB #T1110)?* Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/faqs/what-is-the-composition-of-each-buffer-provided-with-the-monarch-spin-plasmid-miniprep-kit-neb-t1110

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Plasmid Miniprep: Thermo Fisher GeneJET Kit">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/thermo-fisher-genejet-plasmid-miniprep-kit-2.pdf" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the isolation of plasmid DNA from overnight bacterial
cultures with the GeneJET Plasmid Miniprep Kit, using the centrifugation
procedure (Thermo Fisher Scientific, 2025). The notes in this protocol are
taken from the user guide of the manufacturer.

#### Attributions

This protocol is a rewritten version of the user guide of the manufacturer
(Thermo Fisher Scientific, 2025), which is available online: GeneJET Plasmid
Miniprep Kit User Guide (MAN0012655).

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol for the isolation of BSH
plasmids from *E. coli* DH5α for sequencing, for the extraction of the pNBU2
plasmid from *E. coli* S17 for downstream promoter library generation, and for
sequencing validation of the promoter library constructs.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| GeneJET Plasmid Miniprep Kit | Thermo Fisher Scientific, cat. no. K0502 (50 preparations) or K0503 (250 preparations) |
| Resuspension Solution with RNase A | 250 µL per preparation; stored at 4 °C after addition of RNase A |
| Lysis Solution | 250 µL per preparation |
| Neutralization Solution | 350 µL per preparation |
| Wash Solution (with ethanol added) | 2 × 500 µL per preparation |
| Elution Buffer | 50 µL per preparation |
| Ethanol, 96–100%, molecular biology grade | 35 mL (K0502) or 170 mL (K0503), added once to the Wash Solution |
| LB medium with the appropriate antibiotic | 1–5 mL per culture |
| Freshly streaked selective plate | One colony per culture |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| GeneJET Spin Columns with collection tubes | 1 per preparation (supplied with the kit) |
| Culture tubes | Volume at least 4 times the culture volume |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL | 2 per preparation |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | 20–200 µL and 100–1000 µL |
| Timer | 1 |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Shaking incubator | 37 °C, 200–250 rpm |
| Microcentrifuge | At least 12,000 × g |
| Vortex mixer | 1 |
| Freezer | −20 °C, for storage of the plasmid DNA |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lysis Solution and Neutralization Solution | Mixtures | Contain irritants and are harmful if contacted with skin, inhaled or swallowed (Thermo Fisher Scientific, 2025). | Wear gloves and safety glasses. Bleach should not be added to the flow-through. | Thermo Fisher Scientific (2025) User guide; SDS on the product page |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | 64-17-5 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Keep away from open flames and hot surfaces. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.) SDS of absolute ethanol |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

#### Procedure

**Preparing the kit solutions**

1. Add the RNase A solution provided to the Resuspension Solution and mix
   thoroughly.

<ProtocolNote>

After the addition of RNase A, the Resuspension Solution is stable for 6 months
when stored at 4 °C.

</ProtocolNote>

2. Before the first use of the kit, add ethanol (96–100%) to the concentrated
   Wash Solution: 35 mL to 20 mL (K0502) or 170 mL to 100 mL (K0503). Mark the
   checkbox on the bottle cap.

**Growing the bacterial culture**

3. Pick a single colony from a freshly streaked selective plate and inoculate 1
   to 5 mL of LB medium supplemented with the appropriate antibiotic.
4. Incubate for approximately 12 to 16 hours at 37 °C with shaking at 200 to
   250 rpm.

<ProtocolNote>

A tube or flask with a volume of at least 4 times the culture volume should be
used.

</ProtocolNote>

5. Harvest the cells by centrifugation at 6800 × g for 2 minutes at room
   temperature. Decant the supernatant and remove all remaining medium.

**Lysing the cells**

6. Resuspend the pelleted cells in 250 µL of Resuspension Solution, transfer
   the suspension to a microcentrifuge tube and resuspend completely by
   vortexing or pipetting up and down until no cell clumps remain.

<ProtocolNote>

RNase A should have been added to the Resuspension Solution (step 1).

</ProtocolNote>

7. Add 250 µL of Lysis Solution and mix gently by inverting the tube 4 to 6
   times, until the solution becomes viscous and slightly clear.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The tube should not be vortexed, to avoid shearing of chromosomal DNA. The
incubation should not exceed 5 minutes, to avoid denaturation of supercoiled
plasmid DNA.

</ProtocolNote>

**Clearing the lysate**

8. Add 350 µL of Neutralization Solution and mix immediately by inverting the
   tube 4 to 6 times.

<ProtocolNote>

It is important to mix thoroughly and gently after the addition of the
Neutralization Solution, to avoid localized precipitation of bacterial cell
debris. The neutralized lysate should become cloudy.

</ProtocolNote>

9. Centrifuge at 12,000 × g or more for 5 minutes to pellet the cell debris and
   chromosomal DNA.

**Binding the DNA**

10. Transfer the supernatant to a GeneJET spin column by decanting or pipetting.

<ProtocolNote>

The white precipitate should not be disturbed or transferred.

</ProtocolNote>

11. Centrifuge at 12,000 × g or more for 1 minute. Discard the flow-through and
    place the column back into the same collection tube.

<ProtocolNote>

Bleach should not be added to the flow-through.

</ProtocolNote>

**Washing the DNA**

12. Add 500 µL of Wash Solution (with ethanol added) to the spin column.

<ProtocolNote>

For EndA+ strains only, which have a high level of nuclease activity, a
preliminary wash with 500 µL of Wash Solution I (supplemented with isopropanol,
centrifugation for 30 to 60 seconds) is performed before this step.

</ProtocolNote>

13. Centrifuge at 12,000 × g or more for 30 to 60 seconds. Discard the
    flow-through and place the column back into the same collection tube.
14. Repeat steps 12 and 13 once.
15. Discard the flow-through and centrifuge at 12,000 × g or more for 1 minute
    to remove residual Wash Solution.

<ProtocolNote>

This step is essential to avoid residual ethanol in the plasmid preparation.

</ProtocolNote>

**Eluting the DNA**

16. Transfer the spin column into a fresh 1.5 mL microcentrifuge tube.
17. Add 50 µL of Elution Buffer to the centre of the spin column membrane,
    without touching the membrane with the pipette tip.
18. Incubate for 2 minutes at room temperature, then centrifuge at 12,000 × g
    or more for 2 minutes.
19. Store the purified plasmid DNA at −20 °C.

#### References
