- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.). *Ethanol absolute for analysis EMSURE* (Product No. 1.00983) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/mm/100983
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (2025). *GeneJET plasmid miniprep kit user guide* (Publication No. MAN0012655, Rev. B). https://documents.thermofisher.com/TFS-Assets/LSG/manuals/MAN0012655_GeneJET_Plasmid_Miniprep_UG.pdf

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="DNA Quantification with the LVis Plate">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/dna-quantification-with-the-lvis-plate-1.pdf" workbookTab="Nanodrop" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the measurement of DNA concentration and purity by
absorbance on a low-volume plate read in the microplate reader. The plate holds
sixteen sample positions, takes 1 to 2 µL per position and is reusable, so that
a batch of minipreps or cleaned PCR products can be quantified in one read (BMG
Labtech, n.d.).

The concentration follows from the absorbance at 260 nm. Two ratios are read
alongside it: A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀ reports protein carry-over and A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ reports salts
and organic compounds.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| DNA samples | 1–2 µL per measurement |
| Elution buffer or MQ water matching the samples | For the blank |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v), and lint-free tissue | For cleaning the plate |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Low-volume quantification plate, 16 positions | Reusable |
| Micropipette, 0.5–2 µL, and tips | For loading the positions |
| Lint-free tissue | For cleaning between reads |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Microplate reader with absorbance spectrum capability | CLARIOstar Plus |

**Software**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Reader control software | For the quantification protocol and the pathlength correction |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

The samples are purified DNA in buffer. Ethanol used for cleaning is flammable.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | 64-17-5 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Keep away from open flames and hot surfaces. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.) SDS of absolute ethanol |
| Purified DNA in elution buffer or water | n/a | Not classified as hazardous. | Standard laboratory practice. | No SDS required |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Preparing the plate**

1. Clean each sample position with 70% ethanol on a lint-free tissue and let it
   dry completely.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

Residue from a previous sample on a position carries directly into the next
reading. The positions are cleaned before and after every use.

</ProtocolNote>

2. Open the quantification protocol for double-stranded DNA in the reader
   software and check that the pathlength correction is switched on.

**Measuring**

3. Apply 1 to 2 µL of the blank to the first position: the same elution buffer
   or water the samples are in.

<ProtocolNote>

The blank must match the buffer of the samples. Water used as a blank for
samples in elution buffer gives a shifted A₂₃₀ and therefore a wrong
A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio.

</ProtocolNote>

4. Apply 1 to 2 µL of each sample to its own position, keeping a record of
   which sample is on which position.

<ProtocolNote>

The positions are arranged for an eight-channel pipette, so a row of samples
can be loaded in one movement.

</ProtocolNote>

5. Close the reader and run the measurement.

**Reading the result**

6. Record the concentration and both purity ratios for each sample (Table 2).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Concentration and purity."
  workbookTab="Nanodrop"
  rows={[
    { key: "a260", label: "A₂₆₀ (pathlength corrected to 10 mm)", value: 0.25, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "factor", label: "Factor for double-stranded DNA", value: 50, unit: "ng/µL per A₂₆₀", input: true },
    { key: "dilution", label: "Dilution factor of the measured sample", value: 1, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "a280", label: "A₂₈₀ (pathlength corrected)", value: 0.132, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "a230", label: "A₂₃₀ (pathlength corrected)", value: 0.119, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "conc", label: "DNA concentration", unit: "ng/µL", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.a260 * v.factor * v.dilution },
    { key: "r280", label: "A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀ ratio (target 1.7–1.9)", unit: "–", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.a260 / v.a280 },
    { key: "r230", label: "A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio (target 2.0–2.2)", unit: "–", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.a260 / v.a230 },
  ]}
/>

<ProtocolNote>

The concentration is the absorbance at 260 nm multiplied by 50 ng/µL per
absorbance unit for double-stranded DNA; the factor is 40 for RNA and 33 for
single-stranded DNA.

</ProtocolNote>

7. Check the ratios: A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀ should be 1.7 to 1.9 and A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ should be
   2.0 to 2.2.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

A low A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio indicates carry-over of salts or organic compounds from
the purification, even when the A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀ ratio looks acceptable. A preparation
with a low A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio can fail in sequencing and in enzymatic reactions.

</ProtocolNote>

8. Calculate the volume needed for the mass of DNA the next step requires
   (Table 3).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Volume required for a given mass of DNA."
  workbookTab="Nanodrop"
  rows={[
    { key: "mass", label: "Mass of DNA required", value: 200, unit: "ng", input: true },
    { key: "conc", label: "Measured concentration", value: 120, unit: "ng/µL", input: true },
    { key: "volume", label: "Volume to use", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.mass / v.conc },
  ]}
/>

**After the measurement**

9. Clean every used position with 70% ethanol on a lint-free tissue and let the
   plate dry before storing it.
10. Record the concentration and ratios in the team records, with the date and
    the sample identity.

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

A pNBU2 miniprep measured on 12 August 2026 gave 73.37 ng/µL with an
A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀ ratio of 1.89 and an A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio of 0.56. Only one of the two
recommended washes had been performed, which is the likely cause of the low
A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

A gel-extracted backbone measured 0.09 ng/µL, which is too low for the assembly
and meant the amplification had to be repeated.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Concentration and purity from the absorbance**

*c* = A₂₆₀ × *f* × *d*

in which:

- *c* = concentration (ng/µL)
- A₂₆₀ = absorbance at 260 nm, corrected to a 10 mm pathlength
- *f* = 50 ng/µL per absorbance unit for double-stranded DNA (40 for RNA, 33 for single-stranded DNA)
- *d* = dilution factor of the measured sample

The reader corrects the short pathlength of the plate to the 10 mm equivalent,
which is what the factor *f* is defined for. The purity ratios are A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀,
which should be 1.7 to 1.9 for DNA, and A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀, which should be 2.0 to 2.2;
a low A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ indicates carry-over of salts or organic compounds.

**Worked example.** For A₂₆₀ = 0.250 undiluted: *c* = 0.250 × 50 × 1 =
12.5 ng/µL. With A₂₈₀ = 0.132 and A₂₃₀ = 0.119 the ratios are 1.89 and 2.10,
both within the target range.

**Volume for a given mass of DNA**

*V* = *m* / *c*

in which:

- *V* = volume to use (µL)
- *m* = mass of DNA required (ng)
- *c* = measured concentration (ng/µL)

**Worked example.** For 200 ng from a sample at 120 ng/µL: *V* = 200 / 120 =
1.67 µL.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- BMG Labtech. (n.d.). *LVis Plate* [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://store.bmglabtech.com/product/lvis-plate/
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.). *Ethanol absolute for analysis EMSURE* (Product No. 1.00983) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/mm/100983

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Gibson Assembly">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/gibson-assembly-2.pdf" workbookTab="Gibson assembly" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the assembly of a linearized vector with one or more
synthetic fragments in a single isothermal reaction, using a commercial HiFi
assembly master mix. The fragments carry overlapping ends, which the reaction
joins without a separate ligation step.

The assembled product is transformed directly into competent cells (see the
protocol "Chemical Transformation Protocol").

#### Attributions

The reaction follows the instructions of the manufacturer of the assembly
master mix (New England Biolabs, n.d.-b), which are available online: NEBuilder
HiFi DNA Assembly Master Mix (NEB #E2621). The reaction volumes were planned
with the assembly calculator of the manufacturer (New England Biolabs, n.d.-a).

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol to assemble the
promoter-reporter constructs of the promoter library into the amplified pNBU2
backbone.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| NEBuilder HiFi DNA Assembly Master Mix (2×) | New England Biolabs, cat. no. E2621; half the reaction volume |
| Linearized vector | See the protocol "Backbone Amplification PCR" |
| Synthetic DNA fragments (gBlocks), lyophilised | One or more per construct |
| Positive control supplied with the kit | For the first use and when troubleshooting |
| MQ water (Milli-Q), sterile and autoclaved | For resuspension and for the reaction |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| PCR tubes, thin-walled | One per assembly |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL, sterile | One per resuspended fragment |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | 0.5–10 µL and 2–20 µL |
| Ice bucket | The reaction is assembled on ice |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Thermocycler | 50 °C |
| Microplate reader or spectrophotometer | For quantification of the fragments |
| Vortex mixer and microcentrifuge | For resuspension and collection of the liquid |
| Freezer | −20 °C |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NEBuilder HiFi DNA Assembly Master Mix | Mixture | The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. Keep on ice and thaw on ice. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-b) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| Synthetic DNA fragments and purified vector | n/a | Not classified as hazardous. | Standard laboratory practice. | No SDS required |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Preparing the fragments**

1. Prepare a bottle of sterile MQ water in advance.

<ProtocolNote>

It is recommended to prepare and autoclave the water the day before, so that no
time is lost waiting for it.

</ProtocolNote>

2. Resuspend each lyophilised fragment in MQ water to the intended
   concentration (Table 2).

<ProtocolNote>

The volume in µL is the amount on the tube in fmol divided by the intended
concentration in nM, because 1 nM is 1 fmol/µL.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Resuspension of lyophilised gBlocks."
  workbookTab="Gibson assembly"
  rows={[
    { key: "fmol", label: "Amount stated on the tube", value: 1000, unit: "fmol", input: true },
    { key: "cTarget", label: "Target concentration", value: 10, unit: "nM", input: true },
    { key: "vResuspend", label: "MQ water for resuspension", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.fmol / v.cTarget },
    { key: "length", label: "Fragment length", value: 1000, unit: "bp", input: true },
    { key: "cNg", label: "Measured concentration after resuspension", value: 15.7, unit: "ng/µL", input: true },
    { key: "cNM", label: "Concentration in nM", unit: "nM", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.cNg * 1e6) / (650 * v.length) },
  ]}
/>

3. Measure the concentration of each resuspended fragment (see the protocol
   "DNA Quantification with the LVis Plate"), using 1 µL per measurement.

<ProtocolNote>

As little sample as possible is used, because the whole volume is needed for
the assemblies.

</ProtocolNote>

4. Convert each measured concentration from ng/µL to nM, using the length of
   the fragment.

**Setting up the reaction**

5. Thaw the assembly master mix on ice and keep it on ice throughout.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The master mix is kept on ice at all times. When it has thawed, it is pipetted
up and down very slowly the first time with a sterile tip.

</ProtocolNote>

6. Vortex the DNA fragments briefly and collect the liquid at the bottom of the
   tubes.
7. Assemble the reaction on ice according to Table 3: half the reaction volume
   is master mix, and the fragments are added in the intended molar amounts.

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Assembly reaction."
  workbookTab="Gibson assembly"
  rows={[
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Total reaction volume", value: 10, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vMasterMix", label: "NEBuilder HiFi Master Mix (2×)", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vTotal / 2 },
    { key: "nVector", label: "Vector, amount in the reaction", value: 0.05, unit: "pmol", input: true },
    { key: "ratio", label: "Insert to vector molar ratio", value: 2, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "nInsert", label: "Insert, amount in the reaction", unit: "pmol", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => v.nVector * v.ratio },
    { key: "cVector", label: "Vector concentration", value: 20, unit: "nM", input: true },
    { key: "cInsert", label: "Insert concentration", value: 50, unit: "nM", input: true },
    { key: "vVector", label: "Vector volume to add", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => (v.nVector * 1000) / v.cVector },
    { key: "vInsert", label: "Insert volume to add", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => (v.nInsert * 1000) / v.cInsert },
    { key: "vWater", label: "Nuclease-free water to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.vTotal - v.vMasterMix - v.vVector - v.vInsert },
  ]}
/>

<ProtocolNote>

The manufacturer recommends 0.03 to 0.2 pmol of fragments in total for an
assembly of two or three fragments, with a two-fold molar excess of each insert
over the vector, and an overlap of 15 to 20 bp between fragments (New England
Biolabs, n.d.-b). For cloning efficiency, 50 to 100 ng of vector is a practical
amount.

</ProtocolNote>

**Incubation**

8. Incubate the reaction at 50 °C for 30 minutes in a thermocycler.

<ProtocolNote>

An incubation of up to 60 minutes can improve the assembly efficiency in some
cases (New England Biolabs, n.d.-b). The team used 43 minutes.

</ProtocolNote>

9. Place the reaction on ice, or store it at −20 °C until the transformation.

**Transformation**

10. Transform the chilled assembly product into competent cells, using
    approximately 10% of the reaction volume (see the protocol "Chemical
    Transformation Protocol").

<ProtocolNote>

For the promoter library, 2.0 µL of a 10 µL assembly was transformed into
chemically competent *E. coli* S17 λ pir.

</ProtocolNote>

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

The assemblies of 3 September 2026 used twenty constructs, combining five
promoters with four reporters, each as a single fragment assembled into the
amplified pNBU2 backbone.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

The fragments were supplied lyophilised and were resuspended to 10 nM, then
measured individually, because the concentration after resuspension varied
between fragments.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Resuspension of lyophilised gBlocks and conversion to nM**

*V* = *n* / *c*  and  *c*nM = (*c*ng × 10⁶) / (650 × *L*)

in which:

- *V* = volume of MQ water for resuspension (µL)
- *n* = amount stated on the tube (fmol)
- *c* = target concentration (nM, equal to fmol/µL)
- *c*nM = concentration in nM
- *c*ng = measured concentration (ng/µL)
- *L* = fragment length (bp)
- 650 = average molar mass of one base pair of double-stranded DNA (g/mol)

A concentration in nM equals fmol/µL, which is why the amount in fmol divided
by the target concentration gives the volume directly. The conversion from
ng/µL to nM is needed because the assembly is set up on a molar basis.

**Worked example.** For a tube containing 1000 fmol at 10 nM: *V* = 1000 / 10 =
100 µL. A 1000 bp fragment measured at 15.7 ng/µL is (15.7 × 10⁶) /
(650 × 1000) = 24.2 nM.

**Assembly reaction**

*n*insert = *n*vector × *R*  and  *V* = (*n* × 1000) / *c*

in which:

- *n*vector, *n*insert = amount of vector and insert in the reaction (pmol)
- *R* = insert to vector molar ratio
- *V* = volume to add (µL)
- *c* = concentration of that fragment (nM)
- 1000 = conversion from pmol to fmol, since 1 nM is 1 fmol/µL

The master mix is half the reaction volume. The manufacturer recommends 0.03 to
0.2 pmol of fragments in total for a two-fragment assembly, with a two-fold
molar excess of insert over vector, and an overlap of 15 to 20 bp between
fragments. Nuclease-free water makes up the remainder.

**Worked example.** For a 10 µL reaction: 5.0 µL of master mix. With 0.05 pmol
of a 20 nM vector and a 2:1 ratio, the insert is 0.100 pmol; the volumes are
(0.05 × 1000) / 20 = 2.50 µL of vector and (0.1 × 1000) / 50 = 2.00 µL of a
50 nM insert, leaving 0.50 µL of water.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-a). *NEBuilder assembly tool*. Retrieved October 6, 2026, from https://nebuildercalculator.neb.com/
- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-b). *NEBuilder HiFi DNA Assembly Master Mix* (NEB #E2621) [Product information]. Retrieved October 6, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/products/e2621-nebuilder-hifi-dna-assembly-master-mix

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Chemical Transformation">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/chemical-transformation-protocol.pdf" workbookTab="Transformation" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the introduction of plasmid DNA into chemically
competent *Escherichia coli* cells by heat shock (Hanahan, 1983; Chang et al.,
2017). After a recovery period in antibiotic-free medium, the cells are spread
on selective LB agar plates using sterile glass beads, and transformants are
obtained as colonies after overnight incubation.

#### Attributions

This protocol has been adapted from the Benchling workspace of the KU Leuven
iGEM 2024 team.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Cloning reaction mix | 5 µL per transformation |
| Control plasmid (positive control) | Amount to be confirmed with the supervising researcher (Table 2) |
| Chemically competent *E. coli* cells | 1 aliquot per transformation; kept on ice (see the protocol "Making Chemically Competent Cells") |
| LB outgrowth medium (rich medium without antibiotic) | 450 µL per transformation; pre-warmed to 37 °C |
| LB agar plates with the appropriate antibiotic | 1 per transformation; pre-warmed to 37 °C (see the protocol "Preparing LB Broth and Agar Plates") |
| Ice | For thawing and incubation of the cells |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | For disinfection of the work surface |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL, sterile | 1 per transformation (if the cells are not already aliquoted) |
| Sterile glass plating beads | Several beads per plate |
| Micropipettes (0.5–10 µL, 20–200 µL and 100–1000 µL) and sterile tips | For the DNA, plating and outgrowth medium |
| Ice bucket and floating tube rack | 1 each |
| Timer | 1 |
| Permanent marker | For labelling of the plates |
| Container for used beads and biological waste container | 1 each |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Water bath or heat block | 42 °C, for the heat shock |
| Shaking incubator or thermomixer | 37 °C, for the outgrowth |
| Microcentrifuge | 3000 × g |
| Incubator | 37 °C, for the plates |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

The antibiotic rows below apply to the antibiotic contained in the selective
plates and should be read according to the antibiotic in use.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kanamycin sulfate | 25389-94-0 | Danger. H360D: may damage the unborn child (Repr. 1B). | Wear gloves, lab coat and safety glasses. Weigh powder in a fume hood. Not to be handled during pregnancy. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-c) |
| Ampicillin sodium salt | 69-52-3 | Danger. H317: may cause an allergic skin reaction. H334: may cause allergy or asthma symptoms if inhaled. | Avoid dust formation and inhalation. Not to be handled by persons with a penicillin allergy. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | 64-17-5 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Keep away from open flames and hot surfaces. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) SDS of absolute ethanol |
| *E. coli* laboratory strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agent, risk group 1. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

#### Procedure

**Preparation**

1. Pre-warm the LB outgrowth medium and the LB-antibiotic plates to 37 °C.
2. Thaw the competent cells on ice.

<ProtocolNote>

Competent cells should be kept on ice at all times until the heat shock.

</ProtocolNote>

**Transformation**

3. Add 5 µL of the cloning reaction mix to the competent cells and mix gently.

<ProtocolNote>

For the control transformation with plasmid DNA, it is recommended to confirm
the amount of DNA to be added with the supervising PhD researchers. The volume
required for a given DNA mass can be calculated with Table 2.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="DNA volume to add."
  workbookTab="Transformation"
  rows={[
    { key: "conc", label: "DNA concentration", value: 0.1, unit: "ng/µL", input: true },
    { key: "mass", label: "DNA mass to transform", value: 0.1, unit: "ng", input: true },
    { key: "vCells", label: "Competent cell volume", value: 50, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vDNA", label: "DNA volume to add", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.mass / v.conc },
    { key: "vMax", label: "Maximum recommended DNA volume (10% of cell volume)", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => 0.1 * v.vCells },
    { key: "check", label: "Volume check", unit: "–", computeText: (v) => (v.vDNA <= v.vMax ? "Within the maximum" : "Too much DNA solution — concentrate it or use less") },
  ]}
/>

4. Keep the cells on ice for 20 to 30 minutes, until the DNA is homogeneously
   distributed.
5. Heat-shock the cells at 42 °C for 30 seconds.
6. Immediately chill the cells on ice for 5 minutes.
7. Add 450 µL of the pre-warmed outgrowth medium.
8. Shake at 37 °C for 20 to 60 minutes.

<ProtocolNote>

A minimum of 20 minutes is required, and an outgrowth of 45 to 90 minutes is
recommended. In this project, samples were incubated for approximately 45 to
50 minutes. For ampicillin resistance, the outgrowth can be skipped entirely
(Addgene, n.d.), because ampicillin is bacteriostatic for slow-growing cells
and bactericidal only for fast-growing cells; this applies in particular when
the plates are cold or when the cells are grown at 30 °C.

</ProtocolNote>

**Plating**

9. Centrifuge the tubes at 3000 × g for 3 minutes and discard the supernatant,
   leaving approximately 50 µL in which the pellet is gently resuspended.
10. Add several sterile glass beads to each LB-antibiotic plate and pipette
    50 µL of the cell suspension onto the beads.
11. Close the lid and shake the plate back and forth, rotate the plate and
    shake again. Repeat the rotation and shaking a few times to spread the
    cells evenly.
12. Remove the beads into the designated container and incubate the plates
    upside down overnight at 37 °C.

**Evaluation**

13. On the next day, check the plates for colonies. If required, calculate the
    transformation efficiency (Table 3).

<ProtocolNote>

Depending on the strain, construct and antibiotic, colonies can take up to
36 hours to form.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Transformation efficiency."
  workbookTab="Transformation"
  rows={[
    { key: "colonies", label: "Colonies counted on plate", value: 150, unit: "CFU", input: true },
    { key: "mass", label: "DNA mass transformed", value: 0.1, unit: "ng", input: true },
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Total cell suspension volume before plating", value: 50, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vPlated", label: "Volume plated", value: 50, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "fraction", label: "Fraction of transformation plated", unit: "–", compute: (v) => v.vPlated / v.vTotal },
    { key: "efficiency", label: "Transformation efficiency", unit: "CFU/µg DNA", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.colonies / ((v.mass / 1000) * v.fraction) },
  ]}
/>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Volume of DNA to transform**

*V* = *m* / *c*

in which:

- *V* = volume of DNA solution to add (µL)
- *m* = mass of DNA to transform (ng)
- *c* = concentration of the DNA solution (ng/µL)

The added volume should not exceed 10% of the volume of competent cells,
because the buffer of the DNA solution otherwise dilutes the transformation
mixture too much.

**Worked example.** For 0.1 ng of DNA at 0.1 ng/µL: *V* = 0.1 / 0.1 = 1.00 µL,
which is within the maximum of 0.1 × 50 = 5.0 µL for a 50 µL aliquot of cells.

**Transformation efficiency**

*E* = *N* / (*m* × *f*)  with  *f* = *V*plated / *V*total

in which:

- *E* = transformation efficiency (CFU per µg of DNA)
- *N* = number of colonies counted on the plate (CFU)
- *m* = mass of DNA transformed (µg)
- *f* = fraction of the transformation that was plated
- *V*plated = volume plated (µL)
- *V*total = total volume of the cell suspension before plating (µL)

The mass of DNA is converted from ng to µg by dividing by 1000. Only the plated
fraction of the transformation gave rise to the counted colonies, which is why
*N* is divided by *f*.

**Worked example.** For 150 colonies from 0.1 ng of DNA with the whole 50 µL
suspension plated: *f* = 50 / 50 = 1, and *E* = 150 / (0.0001 × 1) =
1.50 × 10⁶ CFU/µg.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- Addgene. (n.d.). *Bacterial transformation*. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.addgene.org/protocols/bacterial-transformation/
- Chang, A. Y., Chau, V. W. Y., Landas, J. A., & Pang, Y. (2017). Preparation of calcium competent *Escherichia coli* and heat-shock transformation. *JEMI Methods, 1*, 22–25. https://ujemi.microbiology.ubc.ca/sites/default/files/Chang%20et%20al%20JEMI-methods%20Vol%201%20pg%2022-25.pdf
- Hanahan, D. (1983). Studies on transformation of *Escherichia coli* with plasmids. *Journal of Molecular Biology, 166*(4), 557–580. https://doi.org/10.1016/S0022-2836(83)80284-8
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-a). *Ampicillin sodium salt* (Product No. A9518) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sial/a9518
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-b). *Ethanol absolute for analysis EMSURE* (Product No. 1.00983) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/mm/100983
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-c). *Kanamycin sulfate from Streptomyces kanamyceticus* (Product No. K1377) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/k1377

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Making Chemically Competent Cells">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/making-chemically-competent-cells.pdf" workbookTab="Competent cells" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the preparation of chemically competent *Escherichia
coli* cells by sequential washing with ice-cold magnesium chloride and calcium
chloride. Exposure to divalent cations at low temperature makes the cells
permeable to plasmid DNA during a subsequent heat shock (Mandel & Higa, 1970;
Hanahan, 1983). The cells are harvested at an OD₆₀₀ of 0.4 (Chang et al., 2017)
and stored as single-use aliquots at −80 °C in calcium chloride containing
15–20% glycerol.

The quantities given are for a 200 mL culture, which yields approximately 40
aliquots of 50 µL.

#### Attributions

Adapted from a protocol provided by Mathew Herdman, KU Leuven iGEM 2026
Advisor.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| *E. coli* strain of interest | Glycerol stock or fresh colony |
| Growth medium (LB broth), sterile | 5 mL for the overnight culture and 200 mL for the main culture |
| Calcium chloride (CaCl₂), 100 mM, sterile | 60 mL per 200 mL culture, plus 17 mL for the storage solution; pre-chilled on ice |
| Magnesium chloride (MgCl₂), 100 mM, sterile | 50 mL per 200 mL culture; pre-chilled on ice |
| CaCl₂-glycerol storage solution (85 mM CaCl₂, 15% v/v glycerol), sterile | 2 mL per 200 mL culture (20 mL prepared); pre-chilled on ice |
| Glycerol, 100% | 3 mL for 20 mL of storage solution |
| MQ water (Milli-Q) | For preparation of the salt solutions |
| Ice and dry ice | For chilling of the cells and snap-freezing of the aliquots |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | For disinfection of the work surface |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Culture tube, 14 mL, sterile | 1, for the overnight culture |
| Baffled or conical flask, 1 L, sterile | 1, for the 200 mL culture |
| Conical centrifuge tubes, 50 mL, sterile | 6; pre-chilled |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL, sterile | Approximately 40; pre-chilled |
| Syringe filters or bottle-top filters, 0.22 µm, with syringes | For filter sterilization of the salt solutions |
| Bottles, autoclavable, 100 mL to 1 L | For the salt solutions |
| Serological pipettes (10 and 25 mL) and pipette controller | For transfer of the culture and the wash solutions |
| Micropipettes (20–200 µL and 100–1000 µL) and sterile tips | For resuspension and aliquoting |
| Cuvettes | For measurement of the OD₆₀₀ |
| Ice bucket and insulated box for dry ice | 1 each |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Precision balance | Readability 0.001 g |
| Shaking incubator | 37 °C |
| Spectrophotometer | Measurement at 600 nm |
| Refrigerated centrifuge with rotor for 50 mL tubes | Pre-cooled to 4 °C; 4000 × g |
| Autoclave | For sterilization of the storage solution, medium and glassware |
| Freezer | −80 °C, for storage of the aliquots |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Insulated (cryogenic) gloves | For handling dry ice and items stored at −80 °C |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Calcium chloride | 10043-52-4 (anhydrous), 10035-04-8 (dihydrate) | Warning. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Wear safety glasses and gloves. Dissolution of the anhydrous salt releases heat. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) SDS of the dihydrate |
| Magnesium chloride hexahydrate | 7791-18-6 | Not classified as hazardous. | Standard laboratory practice. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-d) |
| Glycerol | 56-81-5 | Not classified as hazardous. | Spills are slippery: clean up immediately. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-c) |
| Dry ice (solid carbon dioxide) | 124-38-9 | Not classified under CLP. Cold burns (−78.5 °C). Asphyxiation risk in confined spaces. | Handle with insulated gloves. Use in a ventilated room. Never store in a sealed container. | Oy Linde Gas Ab (2023) |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | 64-17-5 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Keep away from open flames and hot surfaces. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) SDS of absolute ethanol |
| *E. coli* laboratory strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agent, risk group 1. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

#### Procedure

**Preparing the solutions**

1. Prepare 100 mM CaCl₂: dissolve 11.098 g of anhydrous CaCl₂ (molar mass
   110.98 g/mol) in MQ water to a final volume of 1000 mL (1.1098 g for
   100 mL). Filter-sterilize the solution (0.22 µm).
2. Prepare 100 mM MgCl₂: dissolve 20.33 g of MgCl₂ hexahydrate (molar mass
   203.30 g/mol) in MQ water to a final volume of 1000 mL. Filter-sterilize the
   solution (0.22 µm).

<ProtocolNote>

If anhydrous MgCl₂ (molar mass 95.21 g/mol) is used instead, 9.521 g is
required for 1000 mL (0.9521 g for 100 mL). The molar mass printed on the
reagent bottle should be checked against Table 2, because the hydrated and
anhydrous forms require different masses.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalcGrid
  number={2}
  title="Salt solutions: mass to weigh."
  workbookTab="Competent cells"
  columns={[
    { key: "salt", label: "Salt", type: "text" },
    { key: "M", label: "Molar mass (g/mol)", type: "input" },
    { key: "c", label: "Concentration (mM)", type: "input" },
    { key: "V", label: "Volume (mL)", type: "input" },
    { key: "m", label: "Mass to weigh (g)", type: "computed", decimals: 3, compute: (r) => (r.c / 1000) * (r.V / 1000) * r.M },
  ]}
  rows={[
    { salt: "CaCl₂ (anhydrous)", M: 110.98, c: 100, V: 1000 },
    { salt: "CaCl₂·2H₂O (dihydrate)", M: 147.01, c: 100, V: 1000 },
    { salt: "MgCl₂ (anhydrous)", M: 95.21, c: 100, V: 1000 },
    { salt: "MgCl₂·6H₂O (hexahydrate)", M: 203.3, c: 100, V: 1000 },
  ]}
/>

3. Prepare 20 mL of CaCl₂-glycerol storage solution (85 mM CaCl₂, 15%
   glycerol): combine 17 mL of 100 mM CaCl₂ with 3 mL of 100% glycerol and
   sterilize by autoclaving.

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="CaCl₂-glycerol storage solution."
  workbookTab="Competent cells"
  rows={[
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Total volume", value: 20, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "pGlycerol", label: "Glycerol (final, v/v)", value: 15, unit: "%", input: true },
    { key: "cStock", label: "CaCl₂ stock concentration", value: 100, unit: "mM", input: true },
    { key: "vGlycerol", label: "100% glycerol to add", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => v.vTotal * (v.pGlycerol / 100) },
    { key: "vCaCl2", label: "CaCl₂ stock to add", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => v.vTotal - v.vGlycerol },
    { key: "cFinal", label: "Final CaCl₂ concentration", unit: "mM", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.cStock * (v.vCaCl2 / v.vTotal) },
  ]}
/>

4. Chill all solutions, the 50 mL tubes and the 1.5 mL tubes on ice or at 4 °C
   before use.

**Day 1: overnight culture**

5. Inoculate 5 mL of growth medium with the required strain and grow overnight
   at 37 °C with shaking.

**Day 2: preparing the competent cells**

6. Add 1/100 volume of the overnight culture to fresh growth medium: 2 mL of
   overnight culture into 200 mL of fresh medium (Table 4).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={4}
  title="Culture scaling."
  workbookTab="Competent cells"
  rows={[
    { key: "vCulture", label: "Culture volume", value: 200, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "dilution", label: "Dilution of overnight culture (1 in …)", value: 100, unit: "fold", input: true },
    { key: "nTubes", label: "Number of 50 mL tubes", value: 6, unit: "tubes", input: true },
    { key: "vAliquot", label: "Aliquot volume", value: 50, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vInoculum", label: "Overnight culture to inoculate", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => v.vCulture / v.dilution },
    { key: "vPerTube", label: "Culture per tube", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => v.vCulture / v.nTubes },
    { key: "mgTotal", label: "Cold 100 mM MgCl₂, total (0.25 × culture volume)", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => 0.25 * v.vCulture },
    { key: "mgPerTube", label: "Cold 100 mM MgCl₂ per tube", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => v.mgTotal / v.nTubes },
    { key: "ca1Total", label: "Cold 100 mM CaCl₂, first resuspension, total (0.25 × culture volume)", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => 0.25 * v.vCulture },
    { key: "ca1PerTube", label: "Cold 100 mM CaCl₂, first resuspension, per tube", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => v.ca1Total / v.nTubes },
    { key: "ca2Total", label: "Cold 100 mM CaCl₂, second resuspension, total (0.05 × culture volume)", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => 0.05 * v.vCulture },
    { key: "ca2PerTube", label: "Cold 100 mM CaCl₂, second resuspension, per tube", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => v.ca2Total / v.nTubes },
    { key: "vStorage", label: "CaCl₂-glycerol storage solution (0.01 × culture volume)", unit: "mL", compute: (v) => 0.01 * v.vCulture },
    { key: "nAliquots", label: "Number of aliquots", unit: "aliquots", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => (v.vStorage * 1000) / v.vAliquot },
  ]}
/>

7. Grow the culture at 37 °C with shaking to an OD₆₀₀ of 0.4.

<ProtocolNote>

It is recommended to pre-cool the centrifuge to 4 °C in advance, so that it is
ready by the time the culture is expected to reach the required density.

</ProtocolNote>

8. Divide the culture over 6 conical tubes (33.33 mL each) and pellet the cells
   at 4000 × g for 15 minutes at 4 °C. Discard the supernatant.
9. Resuspend the pellets in a total of 50 mL of cold 100 mM MgCl₂ (8.33 mL in
   each of the 6 tubes) by gently tapping the tubes on the palm of the hand.

<ProtocolNote>

The cells should not be pipetted up and down at this stage, because they are
fragile.

</ProtocolNote>

10. Centrifuge at 4000 × g for 15 minutes at 4 °C. Discard the supernatant.
11. Resuspend the pellets in a total of 50 mL of cold 100 mM CaCl₂ (8.33 mL in
    each of the 6 tubes) by gently tapping the tubes on the palm of the hand.

<ProtocolNote>

The cells should not be pipetted up and down at this stage.

</ProtocolNote>

12. Place the tubes on ice for 30 minutes.
13. Centrifuge at 4000 × g for 15 minutes at 4 °C. Discard the supernatant.
14. Resuspend the pellets in a total of 10 mL of cold 100 mM CaCl₂ (1.67 mL in
    each of the 6 tubes) by pipetting gently up and down, and combine the
    suspensions.
15. Centrifuge at 2000 × g for 15 minutes at 4 °C. Discard the supernatant.
16. Resuspend the pellet in 2 mL of cold CaCl₂-glycerol storage solution
    (85 mM CaCl₂, 15% glycerol) by pipetting gently up and down.
17. Dispense 50 µL aliquots into sterile, pre-chilled 1.5 mL microcentrifuge
    tubes kept on dry ice.
18. Freeze the aliquots and store them at −80 °C.

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Mass of salt for the stock solutions**

*m* = *c* × *V* × *M*

in which:

- *m* = mass to weigh (g)
- *c* = required concentration (mol/L)
- *V* = final volume (L)
- *M* = molar mass of the compound (g/mol)

The molar mass of a hydrated salt differs from that of the anhydrous form, so
the form stated on the bottle must be used.

**Worked example.** For 1000 mL of 100 mM CaCl₂ (anhydrous, *M* =
110.98 g/mol): *m* = 0.100 × 1.000 × 110.98 = 11.098 g. For the dihydrate (*M*
= 147.01 g/mol) the same solution requires 14.701 g.

**Composition of the CaCl₂-glycerol storage solution**

*V*g = *V* × (*p*g / 100)  and  *V*c = *V* − *V*g  and  *c*f = *c*s × (*V*c / *V*)

in which:

- *V*g = volume of 100% glycerol (mL)
- *V* = total volume of the solution (mL)
- *p*g = required glycerol content (% v/v)
- *V*c = volume of CaCl₂ stock (mL)
- *c*f = final CaCl₂ concentration (mM)
- *c*s = concentration of the CaCl₂ stock (mM)

The glycerol dilutes the CaCl₂ stock, so the final CaCl₂ concentration is lower
than the stock concentration.

**Worked example.** For 20 mL with 15% glycerol from a 100 mM stock: *V*g =
20 × 0.15 = 3.00 mL, *V*c = 20 − 3 = 17.00 mL, *c*f = 100 × (17 / 20) =
85.0 mM.

**Scaling of the culture and the wash solutions**

*V*wash = *f* × *V*culture  and  *V*tube = *V*wash / *n*

in which:

- *V*wash = total volume of wash solution (mL)
- *f* = ratio of wash volume to culture volume
- *V*culture = culture volume (mL)
- *V*tube = volume per centrifuge tube (mL)
- *n* = number of tubes

The ratios used in this protocol are 0.25 for the MgCl₂ wash and the first
CaCl₂ wash, 0.05 for the second CaCl₂ resuspension and 0.01 for the storage
solution. The inoculum for the main culture is *V*culture divided by the
dilution factor.

**Worked example.** For a 200 mL culture in 6 tubes: the MgCl₂ wash is
0.25 × 200 = 50.00 mL in total and 50 / 6 = 8.33 mL per tube; the storage
solution is 0.01 × 200 = 2.00 mL, which gives 2000 / 50 = 40 aliquots of 50 µL.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- Chang, A. Y., Chau, V. W. Y., Landas, J. A., & Pang, Y. (2017). Preparation of calcium competent *Escherichia coli* and heat-shock transformation. *JEMI Methods, 1*, 22–25. https://ujemi.microbiology.ubc.ca/sites/default/files/Chang%20et%20al%20JEMI-methods%20Vol%201%20pg%2022-25.pdf
- Hanahan, D. (1983). Studies on transformation of *Escherichia coli* with plasmids. *Journal of Molecular Biology, 166*(4), 557–580. https://doi.org/10.1016/S0022-2836(83)80284-8
- Mandel, M., & Higa, A. (1970). Calcium-dependent bacteriophage DNA infection. *Journal of Molecular Biology, 53*(1), 159–162. https://doi.org/10.1016/0022-2836(70)90051-3
- Oy Linde Gas Ab. (2023). *Carbon dioxide, solid (dry ice)* [Safety data sheet]. https://static.prd.echannel.linde.com/wcsstore/FI_REN_Industrial_Gas_Store/pdf/SDS_EN/Carbon%20dioxide_solid_Dry%20ice_.pdf
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-a). *Calcium chloride dihydrate* (Product No. C3306) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/c3306
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-b). *Ethanol absolute for analysis EMSURE* (Product No. 1.00983) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/mm/100983
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-c). *Glycerol* (Product No. G5516) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/g5516
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-d). *Magnesium chloride hexahydrate* (Product No. M2670) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sial/m2670

</ExpandableText>

### Verification

<ExpandableText title="Agarose Gel Electrophoresis (Lithium Acetate)">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/agarose-gel-electrophoresis-lithium-acetate.pdf" workbookTab="Agarose gel" />

#### Introduction

Agarose gel electrophoresis is one of the most traditional molecular techniques
for the separation and visualization of double-stranded DNA (Lee et al., 2012).
It can also be used to detect single-stranded DNA (with some adaptation and a
loss of resolution), to investigate changes in DNA topology and to investigate
protein-DNA interactions (the basis of electrophoretic mobility shift assays,
EMSA).

This protocol uses 10 mM lithium acetate as gel and running buffer.
Low-molarity conductive media of this kind permit higher voltages and shorter
run times than conventional Tris-based buffers (Brody & Kern, 2004). The DNA is
stained with SYBR Safe, which is visualized with blue light instead of UV light
(Thermo Fisher Scientific, n.d.-a). The principles and detailed comments on the
protocol are given in the entry "02_Agarose gel electrophoresis".

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lithium acetate (LiOAc), 10 mM in deionized water | Sigma-Aldrich, cat. no. 920320; gel volume plus sufficient buffer to cover the gel (Table 2) |
| Low-melting-temperature agarose, molecular biology grade | Thermo Fisher Scientific, cat. no. 16520050; 0.5 g for a 50 mL, 1% gel |
| SYBR Safe DNA gel stain, 10,000× | Thermo Fisher Scientific, cat. no. S33102; 3 µL for a 50 mL gel |
| DNA loading buffer, 6× | 5 µL per 25 µL of sample (Addgene, n.d.) |
| DNA size ladder | Appropriate for the expected fragment sizes; 1 lane per gel |
| DNA samples | As required |
| Deionized water | For preparation of the 10 mM lithium acetate buffer |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Beaker or conical flask, heat-resistant | At least 2 to 3 times the gel volume |
| Measuring cylinder | Matching the gel volume |
| Weighing boats and spatula | As required |
| Micropipettes (0.5–10 µL and 2–20 µL or 20–200 µL) and tips | For the stain, loading buffer and samples |
| Microcentrifuge tubes or PCR tubes | 1 per sample |
| Gel casting tray, comb and dams (or casting box) | Matching the electrophoresis apparatus |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Precision balance | Readability 0.01 g |
| Microwave oven | For dissolving the agarose |
| Horizontal electrophoresis apparatus | Tank with lid and leads |
| Power supply | Capable of 140 V |
| Blue-light transilluminator with orange filter | For visualization of SYBR Safe-stained DNA |
| Camera or gel documentation system | For documentation of the gel |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

Additional physical hazards: molten agarose can superheat and boil over
suddenly; the power supply delivers a hazardous voltage and should be switched
off before the lid of the tank is removed.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lithium acetate | 546-89-4 | Warning. H302: harmful if swallowed. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. Avoid dust formation when weighing. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.) |
| Low-melting-point agarose | 9012-36-6 | Not classified as hazardous. | Molten agarose can superheat and boil over: wear thermal gloves and safety glasses. | Thermo Fisher Scientific (n.d.-b) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| SYBR Safe DNA gel stain, 10,000× in DMSO | Mixture | DNA-binding dye: to be treated as a potential mutagen. DMSO promotes skin absorption. | Wear nitrile gloves. Dispose of gels and buffer as stain-contaminated waste. | Thermo Fisher Scientific (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |

#### Procedure

**Preparing the running buffer**

1. Prepare 10 mM lithium acetate by dissolving the required mass of lithium
   acetate in deionized water (Table 2).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="10 mM lithium acetate running buffer."
  workbookTab="Agarose gel"
  rows={[
    { key: "conc", label: "Buffer concentration", value: 10, unit: "mM", input: true },
    { key: "volume", label: "Buffer volume", value: 1000, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "molarMass", label: "Molar mass (anhydrous 65.99; dihydrate 102.02)", value: 65.99, unit: "g/mol", input: true },
    { key: "mass", label: "Lithium acetate to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.conc / 1000) * (v.volume / 1000) * v.molarMass },
  ]}
/>

**Planning the gel**

2. Determine the gel volume and gel percentage, and calculate the required
   amounts of agarose and SYBR Safe (Table 3).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Planning the gel."
  workbookTab="Agarose gel"
  rows={[
    { key: "vGel", label: "Gel volume", value: 50, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "pGel", label: "Gel percentage (w/v)", value: 1, unit: "%", input: true },
    { key: "mAgarose", label: "Agarose to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => (v.pGel * v.vGel) / 100 },
    { key: "fStock", label: "SYBR Safe stock concentration", value: 10000, unit: "×", input: true },
    { key: "fFinal", label: "SYBR Safe final concentration in gel (original protocol: 0.6×; manufacturer: 1×)", value: 0.6, unit: "×", input: true },
    { key: "vStain", label: "SYBR Safe stock to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vGel * 1000 * (v.fFinal / v.fStock) },
  ]}
/>

**Making the gel**

3. Prepare the gel tray and seal the ends (apparatus dependent).
4. Weigh the agarose in a beaker.
5. Add the planned gel volume of 10 mM lithium acetate and gently resuspend the
   agarose powder.
6. Heat the suspension in the microwave at full power until all agarose is
   thoroughly dissolved.

<ProtocolNote>

The beaker and its contents will be hot. Thermal gloves and protective eyewear
should be worn.

</ProtocolNote>

7. Add the SYBR Safe stain (10,000× stock) and mix by gently swirling the
   agarose solution.

<ProtocolNote>

The stain must not be added before the agarose has been dissolved, because it
is sufficiently volatile to be lost during heating.

</ProtocolNote>

8. Pour the gel into the prepared gel tray, insert a suitable comb and let the
   gel set (approximately 20 minutes).

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

This step is time critical. Particularly at higher gel densities, the gel may
begin to set during handling between the microwave and the tray, which can
interfere with migration if the gel is not fully submerged.

</ProtocolNote>

9. Remove the comb, taking care not to disrupt the wells. Remove the upper and
   lower dams (or the casting box), or turn the casting tray by 90 degrees to
   expose the upper and lower sides of the gel.

<ProtocolNote>

The method of removal depends on the equipment used.

</ProtocolNote>

**Preparing the samples**

10. Add at least the recommended amount of loading buffer to each sample
    (Table 4). Mix thoroughly by pipetting up and down and set the samples
    aside until loading (step 13).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={4}
  title="Preparing the samples."
  workbookTab="Agarose gel"
  rows={[
    { key: "vSample", label: "Sample volume", value: 25, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "xBuffer", label: "Loading buffer concentration", value: 6, unit: "×-fold", input: true },
    { key: "vBuffer", label: "Loading buffer to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vSample / (v.xBuffer - 1) },
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Total volume per sample", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vSample + v.vBuffer },
  ]}
/>

**Running the gel**

11. Place the gel (with the casting tray) in the electrophoresis apparatus with
    the wells facing the negative electrode (usually black), as shown in
    Figure 1.

<ProtocolNote>

DNA is negatively charged and migrates towards the positive electrode (red).

</ProtocolNote>

<ZoomableImage src="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/figures/gelorientationinelectrophoresis.avif" alt="Top view of a horizontal electrophoresis tank: the wells sit at the black cathode (negative) end and the DNA migrates towards the red anode (positive) end, with the gel submerged in 10 mM lithium acetate running buffer." />

*Figure 1: Orientation of the gel in the electrophoresis apparatus (top view).
The wells are placed at the cathode (−, black) and the DNA migrates towards the
anode (+, red), with the gel submerged in 10 mM lithium acetate running buffer.*

12. Pour enough 10 mM lithium acetate into the tank to cover the gel.
13. Carefully load the DNA ladder and the samples into the wells.
14. Close the lid, connect the leads to the power supply and start the run.

<ProtocolNote>

With the current power supplies, 50 mL gels (approximately 10 cm) are often run
at 140 V for 20 minutes.

</ProtocolNote>

**Visualizing and documenting the gel**

15. Switch off the power supply, remove the gel and place it between the
    blue-light source and the orange filter. Document the gel using the camera
    provided.
16. Save the image file in the personal folder using the file name structure
    INITIALS_YYYYMMDD_description (e.g. VBP_20220307_pGDR_BsaI_digest).

**Gel and buffer disposal**

17. Dispose of the gel in a bin containing a yellow bag (for incineration).
18. Collect the used running buffer in the designated container for
    stain-contaminated liquid waste (ethidium bromide and SYBR Safe
    contaminated waste).

<ProtocolNote>

Liquid running buffer requires specialist disposal and must not be poured down
the drain.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Mass of lithium acetate for the running buffer**

*m* = *c* × *V* × *M*

in which:

- *m* = mass to weigh (g)
- *c* = required concentration (mol/L)
- *V* = final volume (L)
- *M* = molar mass of the compound (g/mol)

The molar mass of a hydrated salt differs from that of the anhydrous form, so
the form stated on the bottle must be used.

**Worked example.** For 1000 mL of 10 mM lithium acetate (anhydrous, *M* =
65.99 g/mol): *m* = 0.010 × 1.000 × 65.99 = 0.660 g.

**Mass of agarose**

*m* = (*p* × *V*) / 100

in which:

- *m* = mass of agarose to weigh (g)
- *p* = gel percentage (% w/v)
- *V* = gel volume (mL)

A percentage w/v is grams per 100 mL, which is why the percentage is divided by
100.

**Worked example.** For a 50 mL gel at 1.0%: *m* = (1.0 × 50) / 100 = 0.50 g.

**Volume of SYBR Safe stain**

V1 = V2 × (F2 / F1)

in which:

- V1 = volume of stain stock to add (µL)
- V2 = gel volume (µL)
- F2 = required concentration in the gel (as a multiple of the working strength)
- F1 = concentration factor of the stock

This is the dilution equation written with concentration factors (×) instead of
mass concentrations.

**Worked example.** For a 50 mL (50,000 µL) gel at 0.6× from a 10,000× stock:
V1 = 50,000 × (0.6 / 10,000) = 3.0 µL.

**Volume of loading buffer**

*V*b = *V*s / (*X* − 1)

in which:

- *V*b = volume of loading buffer to add (µL)
- *V*s = sample volume (µL)
- *X* = concentration factor of the loading buffer (×-fold)

The buffer is diluted *X*-fold in the final mixture, so it must make up one
part in *X* of the total volume: *V*b / (*V*s + *V*b) = 1 / *X*, which
rearranges to the equation above.

**Worked example.** For 25 µL of sample and a 6× buffer: *V*b = 25 / (6 − 1) =
5.0 µL, giving 30.0 µL in total.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- Addgene. (n.d.). *How to run an agarose gel*. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.addgene.org/protocols/gel-electrophoresis/
- Brody, J. R., & Kern, S. E. (2004). History and principles of conductive media for standard DNA electrophoresis. *Analytical Biochemistry, 333*(1), 1–13. https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ab.2004.05.054
- Lee, P. Y., Costumbrado, J., Hsu, C.-Y., & Kim, Y. H. (2012). Agarose gel electrophoresis for the separation of DNA fragments. *Journal of Visualized Experiments*, (62), Article e3923. https://doi.org/10.3791/3923
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.). *Lithium acetate* (Product No. 920320) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/aldrich/920320
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (n.d.-a). *SYBR Safe DNA gel stain* (Catalog No. S33102) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.thermofisher.com/order/catalog/product/S33102
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (n.d.-b). *UltraPure low melting point agarose* (Catalog No. 16520050) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.thermofisher.com/order/catalog/product/16520050

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Colony PCR">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/colony-pcr-2.pdf" workbookTab="Colony PCR" />

#### Introduction

Colony PCR is used to check a transformation: the target region is amplified
directly from a colony, and the size of the amplified fragment is verified on
an agarose gel. The procedure takes one day.

The reaction is set up with OneTaq 2X Master Mix (New England Biolabs, n.d.-a).
Quantities for several reactions and the extension time can be calculated with
Tables 3 and 5.

#### Attributions

This protocol is a rewritten version of the standard operating procedure
"BSB.008 Colony PCR", prepared by Caroline Thyssen Petersen and reviewed by
Ruben Vazquez-Uribe. The BSB series is the set of Bacterial Synthetic Biology
standard operating procedures that Ruben Vazquez-Uribe, principal investigator
of the iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team, wrote for his students in Denmark.

The manufacturer's protocol for the polymerase master mix (New England Biolabs,
n.d.-b) is available online: Protocol for OneTaq 2X Master Mix with Standard
Buffer (NEB #M0482).

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol for screening colonies of both
*E. coli* and *Phocaeicola vulgatus*.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Bacterial colonies on agar plates | One colony per reaction |
| Sterile LB broth or MQ water (Milli-Q) | 30 µL per colony, plus MQ water for the reaction mix |
| OneTaq 2X Master Mix with Standard Buffer | New England Biolabs, cat. no. M0482; 12.5 µL per reaction |
| Forward and reverse primer, 10 µM | 0.5 µL of each per reaction |
| DMSO, 100% (optional) | 0.25–2.5 µL per reaction |
| Agarose | For a small 1% gel |
| TAE buffer | For the gel, the running buffer and sample loading |
| SYBR Safe DNA gel stain | 30 µL per small gel |
| GeneRuler 1 kb DNA ladder | 3.5 µL per gel |
| DNA loading dye, 6× | 1 µL per sample |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| PCR tubes | 1 per colony and 1 per reaction |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | Small tips for picking colonies |
| Parafilm | For mixing samples with loading dye |
| Small gel tray and comb with small wells | 1 |
| Beaker or conical flask, heat-resistant | For dissolving the agarose |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Thermocycler | 1 |
| Precision balance | Readability 0.01 g |
| Microwave oven | For dissolving the agarose |
| Horizontal electrophoresis apparatus and power supply | 100 V |
| Blue-light transilluminator or gel documentation system | For visualization of the gel |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Heat-resistant (thermal) gloves | For handling the hot agarose solution |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

Additional physical hazards: molten agarose can superheat and boil over
suddenly; the power supply delivers a hazardous voltage and should be switched
off before the lid of the tank is removed.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dimethyl sulfoxide (DMSO) | 67-68-5 | H227: combustible liquid. Rapidly absorbed through the skin. | Wear nitrile gloves. Dissolved substances can be carried through the skin. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.) |
| SYBR Safe DNA gel stain, 10,000× in DMSO | Mixture | DNA-binding dye: to be treated as a potential mutagen. DMSO promotes skin absorption. | Wear nitrile gloves. Dispose of gels and buffer as stain-contaminated waste. | Thermo Fisher Scientific (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| Low-melting-point agarose | 9012-36-6 | Not classified as hazardous. | Molten agarose can superheat and boil over: wear thermal gloves and safety glasses. | Thermo Fisher Scientific (n.d.-b) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| OneTaq 2X Master Mix, primers, DNA ladder, loading dye and TAE buffer | Mixtures | The SDS of each product in use should be consulted. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. | Supplier SDS |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

#### Procedure

**Preparing the template DNA**

1. Pick a single colony with a small pipette tip and resuspend it in 30 µL of
   sterile LB broth or MQ water in a PCR tube.

**Setting up the PCR**

2. Mix the reagents listed in Table 2. For several reactions, prepare a master
   mix without template (Table 3).

**Table 2.** Composition of one colony PCR reaction (total volume 25 µL).

| Reagent | Stock concentration | Volume (µL) | Final concentration |
|---|---|---|---|
| MQ water | – | To 25 µL (9.0 µL without DMSO) | – |
| DMSO (optional) | 100% | 0.25–2.5 | 1–10% |
| OneTaq 2X Master Mix | 2× | 12.5 | 1× |
| Forward primer | 10 µM | 0.5 | 0.2 µM |
| Reverse primer | 10 µM | 0.5 | 0.2 µM |
| Template DNA | – | 2.5 | – |

<ProtocolNote>

According to the principal investigator of the team, both OneTaq and Phusion
polymerase are suitable. OneTaq is recommended whenever possible, because the
Phusion master mix is more expensive. If Phusion polymerase is used, the
reaction and cycling parameters have to be adjusted.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote>

The addition of DMSO can be an advantage, because it prevents the reannealing
of single-stranded DNA. Between 1% and 10% can be added depending on the
reaction, with the volume of MQ water reduced accordingly so that the total
volume is unchanged. For the reactions of the team, DMSO was not necessary and
was replaced with MQ water.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote>

If the concentration of the template DNA is too high (approximately 20 ng is
sufficient), less template and more MQ water can be used so that the total
volume is unchanged.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="PCR master mix for multiple reactions."
  workbookTab="Colony PCR"
  rows={[
    { key: "n", label: "Number of reactions", value: 8, unit: "reactions", input: true },
    { key: "excess", label: "Excess for pipetting loss", value: 10, unit: "%", input: true },
    { key: "vRxn", label: "Reaction volume", value: 25, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "cPrimerStock", label: "Primer stock concentration", value: 10, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "cPrimerFinal", label: "Primer final concentration", value: 0.2, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "vTemplate", label: "Template per reaction", value: 2.5, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "pDMSO", label: "DMSO final concentration (0 if not used)", value: 0, unit: "%", input: true },
    { key: "vMix", label: "OneTaq 2× Master Mix per reaction", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vRxn / 2 },
    { key: "vPrimer", label: "Each primer per reaction", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => (v.vRxn * v.cPrimerFinal) / v.cPrimerStock },
    { key: "vDMSO", label: "DMSO per reaction", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => (v.vRxn * v.pDMSO) / 100 },
    { key: "vWater", label: "MQ water per reaction", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vRxn - v.vMix - 2 * v.vPrimer - v.vDMSO - v.vTemplate },
    { key: "vMixTotal", label: "OneTaq 2× Master Mix, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vMix * v.n * (1 + v.excess / 100) },
    { key: "vPrimerTotal", label: "Each primer, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vPrimer * v.n * (1 + v.excess / 100) },
    { key: "vDMSOTotal", label: "DMSO, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vDMSO * v.n * (1 + v.excess / 100) },
    { key: "vWaterTotal", label: "MQ water, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vWater * v.n * (1 + v.excess / 100) },
    { key: "vPerTube", label: "Master mix to dispense per PCR tube (before template)", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vRxn - v.vTemplate },
  ]}
/>

3. Run the PCR program given in Table 4.

**Table 4.** OneTaq colony PCR program.

| Step | Temperature (°C) | Time | Cycles |
|---|---|---|---|
| Initial denaturation | 95 | 5 min | 1 |
| Denaturation | 95 | 20 s | 30 |
| Annealing | 55 | 30 s | 30 |
| Extension | 68 | 1 min per kb | 30 |
| Final extension | 68 | 10 min | 1 |
| Cooling | 10 | Hold | – |

<ProtocolNote>

The annealing temperature should be set to the optimal annealing temperature of
the primers. The extension time is 1 minute per kb of the expected fragment
(Table 5; New England Biolabs, n.d.-b). For colony PCR, an initial denaturation
of 5 minutes is recommended by the manufacturer to lyse the cells (New England
Biolabs, n.d.-b).

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={5}
  title="Extension time."
  workbookTab="Colony PCR"
  rows={[
    { key: "length", label: "Expected amplicon length", value: 1.5, unit: "kb", input: true },
    { key: "rate", label: "Extension rate", value: 1, unit: "min/kb", input: true },
    { key: "time", label: "Extension time", unit: "min", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.length * v.rate },
  ]}
/>

**Gel electrophoresis**

4. Prepare a small gel tray with a comb with small wells.
5. Spread 30 µL of SYBR Safe stain over the bottom of the small tray.
6. Pour 1% agarose gel into the tray until the gel stands approximately 2 mm
   above the marks of the comb.
7. Let the gel set for approximately 20 minutes.
8. Remove the comb.
9. Transfer the gel in the small tray to the electrophoresis apparatus.
10. Load 3.5 µL of GeneRuler 1 kb DNA ladder into the first lane.
11. Mix 1 µL of 6× loading dye, 2 µL of TAE buffer and 3.5 µL of amplified DNA
    on a piece of parafilm, and load the sample.

<ProtocolNote>

When OneTaq Quick-Load 2X Master Mix with Standard Buffer is used, 2 µL of
amplified DNA can be loaded directly, because this master mix already contains
loading dye.

</ProtocolNote>

12. Run the gel at 100 V for 25 minutes.

<ProtocolNote>

The run time depends on the length of the DNA fragments: 20 minutes for smaller
fragments, and 30 minutes for larger fragments or when more separation of the
bands is required.

</ProtocolNote>

13. Visualize the gel and check that the size of the DNA fragments is correct.

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**PCR master mix for several reactions**

*V*component, total = *V*component × *n* × (1 + *e* / 100)

in which:

- *V*component = volume of the component in one reaction (µL)
- *n* = number of reactions
- *e* = excess for pipetting loss (%)

The master mix contains every component except the template, so the volume
dispensed into each tube is the reaction volume minus the template volume. The
water volume per reaction is the reaction volume minus all other components.

**Worked example.** For 8 reactions of 25 µL with 10% excess: the master mix
needs 12.5 × 8 × 1.1 = 110.0 µL of OneTaq 2X Master Mix, 0.5 × 8 × 1.1 =
4.4 µL of each primer and 9.0 × 8 × 1.1 = 79.2 µL of MQ water. Each tube
receives 25 − 2.5 = 22.5 µL of master mix before the template is added.

**Extension time**

*t* = *L* × *r*

in which:

- *t* = extension time (min)
- *L* = expected length of the amplicon (kb)
- *r* = extension rate of the polymerase (min/kb)

**Worked example.** For a 1.5 kb amplicon at 1 min/kb: *t* = 1.5 × 1 = 1.5 min.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-a). *OneTaq 2X Master Mix with Standard Buffer* (NEB #M0482) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/products/m0482-onetaq-2x-master-mix-with-standard-buffer
- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-b). *Protocol for OneTaq 2X Master Mix with Standard Buffer* (NEB #M0482). Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en-us/protocols/protocol-for-onetaq-2x-master-mix-with-standard-buffer-m0482
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.). *Dimethyl sulfoxide* (Product No. D8418) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/d8418
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (n.d.-a). *SYBR Safe DNA gel stain* (Catalog No. S33102) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.thermofisher.com/order/catalog/product/S33102
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (n.d.-b). *UltraPure low melting point agarose* (Catalog No. 16520050) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.thermofisher.com/order/catalog/product/16520050

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="PCR Clean-Up">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/pcr-clean-up.pdf" workbookTab="PCR clean-up" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the purification of a PCR product with the Monarch Spin
PCR & DNA Cleanup Kit, which removes polymerase, primers, nucleotides and salts
from a completed reaction. It is the step between a verified PCR product and
sequencing, and is also used to clean a product before further enzymatic work.

The kit binds up to 5 µg of DNA between 50 bp and 25 kb with the standard
protocol, and recovery is typically 70 to 90% (New England Biolabs, n.d.-a).

#### Attributions

This protocol is a rewritten version of the manufacturer's protocol (New
England Biolabs, n.d.-b), which is available online: Monarch Spin PCR & DNA
Cleanup Kit (NEB #T1130).

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol to clean colony PCR products
before submitting them for Sanger sequencing.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Completed PCR reaction | 20–100 µL per sample is recommended |
| Monarch Spin PCR & DNA Cleanup Kit (5 µg) | New England Biolabs, cat. no. T1130 |
| Monarch Buffer BZ (binding) | 5 volumes per volume of sample |
| Monarch Buffer WZ (wash) | 2 × 200 µL per sample |
| Monarch Buffer EY (elution) | 5–20 µL per sample |
| TE buffer or nuclease-free water | To make up samples smaller than 20 µL |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Monarch Spin Columns S1A and Collection Tubes | One per sample (supplied with the kit) |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL | One per sample |
| Micropipettes and tips | 2–20 µL, 20–200 µL and 100–1000 µL |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Microcentrifuge | At least 12,000 × g |
| Heating block (optional) | 50 °C, for elution of large fragments |
| Freezer | −20 °C, for storage of the purified DNA |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

The compositions of the kit buffers are proprietary; the hazardous components
named below are those disclosed by the manufacturer for the Monarch buffers of
the same names.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Monarch Buffer BZ | Mixture | Contains guanidine and isopropanol. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. Flow-through should not be mixed with bleach. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| Monarch Buffer WZ | Mixture | Contains ethanol. | Keep away from ignition sources. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Binding**

1. Add 5 volumes of Monarch Buffer BZ to 1 volume of sample and mix by
   pipetting up and down or by flicking the tube (Table 2).

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The sample must not be vortexed.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote>

A sample volume of 20 to 100 µL is recommended. A sample below 20 µL is made up
to that range with TE buffer or nuclease-free water.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Binding buffer and expected yield."
  workbookTab="PCR clean-up"
  rows={[
    { key: "vSample", label: "Sample volume", value: 50, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "ratio", label: "Volumes of Monarch Buffer BZ per volume of sample", value: 5, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "vBZ", label: "Monarch Buffer BZ to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vSample * v.ratio },
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Total volume to load", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vSample + v.vBZ },
    { key: "loads", label: "Loads of 800 µL required", unit: "loads", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => Math.ceil(v.vTotal / 800) },
    { key: "cIn", label: "DNA concentration before clean-up", value: 40, unit: "ng/µL", input: true },
    { key: "recovery", label: "Expected recovery", value: 80, unit: "%", input: true },
    { key: "vElution", label: "Elution volume", value: 20, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "cOut", label: "Expected concentration after clean-up", unit: "ng/µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.cIn * v.vSample * (v.recovery / 100)) / v.vElution },
  ]}
/>

2. Insert a Monarch Spin Column S1A into a collection tube, load the sample
   onto the column and spin for 1 minute. Discard the flow-through.

<ProtocolNote>

The column holds 800 µL per spin. A larger total volume is loaded in successive
spins, discarding the flow-through between them.

</ProtocolNote>

**Washing**

3. Re-insert the column into the collection tube, add 200 µL of Monarch Buffer
   WZ and spin for 1 minute.
4. Repeat the wash with a further 200 µL of Monarch Buffer WZ and spin for
   1 minute.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

Both washes are performed. Omitting the second wash leaves salts behind, which
shows up as a low A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio on quantification.

</ProtocolNote>

**Elution**

5. Transfer the column to a clean 1.5 mL microcentrifuge tube, taking care that
   the tip of the column does not touch the flow-through.

<ProtocolNote>

If the tip may have touched the flow-through, spin the column again for
1 minute before transferring it.

</ProtocolNote>

6. Add 5 to 20 µL of Monarch Buffer EY to the centre of the matrix, wait
   1 minute and spin for 1 minute.

<ProtocolNote>

Nuclease-free water can be used instead of Buffer EY. A larger elution volume
gives a slightly higher yield but a lower concentration; the volume is chosen
from what the next step needs. For fragments of 15 kb and larger, incubate for
5 minutes at room temperature or warm the elution buffer to 50 °C.

</ProtocolNote>

7. Quantify the purified DNA (see the protocol "DNA Quantification with the
   LVis Plate") and store it at −20 °C.

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

This kit was used on 9 September 2026 to clean the colony PCR products of the
promoter library constructs before sequencing.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

A preparation cleaned with only one of the two washes gave an A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀ ratio
of 1.89 but an A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio of 0.56, which is far below the target range of
2.0 to 2.2 and indicates carry-over of salts or organic compounds. Both washes
are needed.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>
