This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Binding buffer and expected recovery**

*V*BZ = *V*sample × *r*  and  *c*out = (*c*in × *V*sample × *R* / 100) / *V*elution

in which:

- *V*BZ = volume of Monarch Buffer BZ (µL)
- *V*sample = sample volume (µL)
- *r* = volumes of buffer per volume of sample
- *c*in, *c*out = concentration before and after clean-up (ng/µL)
- *R* = recovery (%)
- *V*elution = elution volume (µL)

The column takes 800 µL at a time, so a larger total volume is loaded in
successive spins. Eluting in a smaller volume gives a higher concentration but
a slightly lower total yield.

**Worked example.** For 50 µL of sample: 50 × 5 = 250 µL of Buffer BZ, giving
300 µL in total, which fits one load. From 40 ng/µL at 80% recovery eluted in
20 µL: (40 × 50 × 0.8) / 20 = 80.0 ng/µL.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-a). *Monarch Spin PCR & DNA Cleanup Kit, 5 µg* (NEB #T1130) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/products/t1130-monarch-spin-pcr-and-dna-cleanup-kit-5-ug
- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-b). *Standard cleanup protocol using the Monarch Spin PCR & DNA Cleanup Kit and centrifugation* (NEB #T1130). Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en-us/protocols/standard-cleanup-protocol-using-the-monarch-spin-pcr-and-dna-cleanup-kit-and-centrifugation

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Sanger Sequencing Sample Preparation">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/sanger-sequencing-sample-preparation-3.pdf" workbookTab="Sequencing prep" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the preparation and submission of purified DNA for
Sanger sequencing by an external service. The sequencing itself is performed by
the provider; this protocol covers everything up to handing the samples over.

Two submission formats are available. In the first, template and primer are
supplied in separate tubes; in the second, they are premixed in one tube. The
requirements below are those of the provider used by the team (Eurofins
Genomics, n.d.-b; Eurofins Genomics, n.d.-a) and should be checked against the
current instructions before each submission, because they change.

#### Attributions

The requirements for the submission are those of the sequencing provider, which
are available online: TubeSeq sample submission guide and Mix2Seq kit.

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol to sequence-validate the BSH
constructs and the promoter library constructs.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Purified plasmid DNA or purified PCR product | See the concentration requirements in Table 2 |
| Sequencing primer, 10 pmol/µL | One per reaction |
| MQ water (Milli-Q), sterile | For adjusting the concentration |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Tubes supplied or specified by the provider | One per sample and, for the separate format, one per primer |
| Barcode labels supplied by the provider | One per tube |
| Micropipettes and tips | 0.5–10 µL and 2–20 µL |
| Permanent marker | For the barcode number on the side of the tube |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Microplate reader or spectrophotometer | For quantification before submission |
| Microcentrifuge | For collecting the liquid at the bottom of the tube |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

The samples themselves are purified DNA in buffer and present no particular
hazard. No cells are submitted.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Purified DNA in elution buffer or water | n/a | Not classified as hazardous. | Standard laboratory practice. | No SDS required |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

<Callout title="Check before each submission">

The submission requirements were read from the provider documentation in
October 2026. They change over time, so check them against the current version
before the next submission.

</Callout>

#### Procedure

**Preparing the DNA**

1. Purify the DNA before submission: a plasmid preparation from a miniprep, or
   a PCR product cleaned with the protocol "PCR Clean-Up".

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

An unpurified PCR reaction cannot be submitted: residual primers, nucleotides
and polymerase interfere with the sequencing reaction.

</ProtocolNote>

2. Quantify the DNA and check the purity ratios (see the protocol "DNA
   Quantification with the LVis Plate").

<ProtocolNote>

An A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀ ratio of 1.7 to 1.9 and an A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio of 2.0 to 2.2 indicate
a preparation clean enough to sequence.

</ProtocolNote>

3. Check the required concentration for the sample type and length (Table 2)
   and adjust the sample with MQ water if it is too concentrated.

**Table 2.** Concentration required by the provider, by sample type (Eurofins
Genomics, n.d.-b; Eurofins Genomics, n.d.-a).

| Sample type | Concentration | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Plasmid DNA, up to 30 kbp | 50–100 ng/µL | Purified preparation |
| PCR product, 150–300 bp | 1 ng/µL | Purified product |
| PCR product, 300–1000 bp | 5 ng/µL | Purified product |
| PCR product, 1000–3000 bp | 10 ng/µL | Purified product |
| Sequencing primer | 10 pmol/µL | Exactly this concentration is required |

**Preparing the primers**

4. Prepare each sequencing primer at exactly 10 pmol/µL (Table 3).

<ProtocolNote>

A concentration of 10 pmol/µL is the same as 10 µM. Primers for sequencing are
16 to 25 bases long, with a melting temperature of 50 to 62 °C and a GC content
of 35 to 60%, and carry no phosphorylation or fluorescent label (Eurofins
Genomics, n.d.-a).

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Primer working solution at 10 pmol/µL."
  workbookTab="Sequencing prep"
  rows={[
    { key: "nmol", label: "Amount of primer stated on the tube", value: 24.8, unit: "nmol", input: true },
    { key: "cStock", label: "Stock concentration after resuspension", value: 100, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "vResuspend", label: "MQ water for resuspension", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => (v.nmol / v.cStock) * 1000 },
    { key: "vWorking", label: "Working solution volume", value: 100, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "cWorking", label: "Working concentration required", value: 10, unit: "pmol/µL", input: true },
    { key: "vStockAdd", label: "Stock to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vWorking * (v.cWorking / v.cStock) },
    { key: "vWaterAdd", label: "MQ water to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vWorking - v.vStockAdd },
  ]}
/>

**Separate tubes format**

5. Pipette the required volume of template into a tube: 15 µL for one to four
   reactions, or 20 µL for five to eight reactions (Eurofins Genomics, n.d.-b).
6. Pipette 15 µL of each primer into its own tube, adding 5 µL for every
   reaction beyond the first that uses that primer (Eurofins Genomics, n.d.-b).

**Premixed format**

7. Combine the template and the primer in one tube to the total volume required
   by the format in use (Table 4).

<ProtocolNote>

The premixed TubeSeq format takes a total of 17 µL (Eurofins Genomics, n.d.-b).
The Mix2Seq format takes 5 µL of purified template and 5 µL of primer at
5 pmol/µL, with a minimum total of 10 µL (Eurofins Genomics, n.d.-a). The
format and its volumes are confirmed with the current instructions of the
provider before mixing.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={4}
  title="Premixed sample for TubeSeq (17 µL total)."
  workbookTab="Sequencing prep"
  rows={[
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Total premixed volume required", value: 17, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vPrimer", label: "Primer volume", value: 2, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "cPrimer", label: "Primer concentration required", value: 10, unit: "pmol/µL", input: true },
    { key: "cRequired", label: "Required template concentration", value: 50, unit: "ng/µL", input: true },
    { key: "cMeasured", label: "Measured DNA concentration", value: 120, unit: "ng/µL", input: true },
    { key: "vTemplate", label: "Template volume at the required concentration", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vTotal - v.vPrimer },
    { key: "mass", label: "DNA needed", unit: "ng", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.vTemplate * v.cRequired },
    { key: "vSample", label: "Volume of the measured sample to use", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.mass / v.cMeasured },
    { key: "vWater", label: "MQ water to add", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vTemplate - v.vSample },
  ]}
/>

**Labelling and submission**

8. Stick a barcode label on each tube and write the barcode number on the side
   of the tube.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The tubes are not taped or wrapped in parafilm (Eurofins Genomics, n.d.-b).

</ProtocolNote>

9. Spin the tubes briefly so that all the liquid is at the bottom, and close
   them with the cap supplied for the service in use.
10. Enter the order online, assigning each barcode to its sample and primer, and
    record in the team strain list which construct each barcode corresponds to.
11. Submit the samples by the collection or shipping route of the provider.

**After the result**

12. Compare the returned trace with the expected sequence of the construct, and
    check the quality of the trace over the region of interest before accepting
    a construct as validated.

<ProtocolNote>

The beginning of a Sanger read is unreliable for the first several dozen bases,
so a primer is chosen to bind far enough upstream of the region that has to be
read.

</ProtocolNote>

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

The first samples were submitted on 9 September 2026, after cleaning the colony
PCR products with the Monarch kit.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The exact service used, the format of the tubes and the current volume
requirements should be recorded here, because the requirements of the provider
change over time.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Primer working solution at 10 pmol/µL**

*V* = (*n* / *c*) × 1000  and  *V*stock = *V*working × (*c*working / *c*stock)

in which:

- *V* = MQ water for resuspension (µL)
- *n* = amount stated on the tube (nmol)
- *c* = stock concentration (µM)
- *c*working = working concentration (pmol/µL)
- *c*stock = stock concentration (pmol/µL)

1 µM equals 1 pmol/µL, so a 10 µM solution is a 10 pmol/µL solution, which is
the concentration the sequencing service requires.

**Worked example.** For 24.8 nmol resuspended to 100 µM: 248 µL of MQ water.
For 100 µL at 10 pmol/µL: 10.0 µL of stock and 90.0 µL of MQ water.

**Premixed sequencing sample**

*V*template = *V*total − *V*primer · *m* = *V*template × *c*required · *V*sample = *m* / *c*measured

in which:

- *V*total = total premixed volume required by the service (µL)
- *V*primer = primer volume (µL)
- *V*template = volume available for the template (µL)
- *c*required = template concentration required by the service (ng/µL)
- *m* = mass of DNA needed (ng)
- *c*measured = measured concentration of the sample (ng/µL)
- *V*sample = volume of the sample to use (µL)

The remainder, *V*template − *V*sample, is made up with MQ water so that the
premix reaches exactly the required total volume.

**Worked example.** For a 17 µL premix with 2 µL of primer, the template
occupies 15.0 µL. At a required 50 ng/µL that is 750 ng; from a sample measured
at 120 ng/µL this is 6.25 µL of sample and 8.75 µL of MQ water.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- Eurofins Genomics. (n.d.-a). *Mix2Seq kit*. Retrieved October 6, 2026, from https://eurofinsgenomics.eu/en/custom-dna-sequencing/mix2seq/
- Eurofins Genomics. (n.d.-b). *TubeSeq service: sample submission guide*. Retrieved October 6, 2026, from https://eurofinsgenomics.eu/media/1587670/web_tubeseq_samplesubmissionguide_dinlang_final.pdf

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Backbone Amplification PCR">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/backbone-amplification-pcr-1.pdf" workbookTab="Backbone PCR" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the amplification of the pNBU2 backbone by PCR, the
verification of the product on an agarose gel, and its recovery from the gel by
extraction. The purified linear backbone is the vector used in the Gibson
assembly of the promoter library.

The annealing temperature is established with a temperature gradient the first
time the primer pair is used, and the amplicon is gel-extracted rather than
column-cleaned, so that the circular plasmid template is removed along with it.

#### Attributions

The reaction follows the instructions of the manufacturer of the polymerase
master mix (New England Biolabs, n.d.-c), which are available online: Protocol
for Taq 2X Master Mix (NEB #M0270). The gel extraction follows the protocol of
the extraction kit (New England Biolabs, n.d.-a).

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol to linearize the pNBU2 backbone
for the assembly of the promoter library constructs.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| pNBU2 plasmid preparation | Template; from the miniprep of *E. coli* S17 λ pir |
| OneTaq 2X Master Mix with Standard Buffer | New England Biolabs, cat. no. M0482; half the reaction volume |
| Backbone forward and reverse primers, 10 µM | 0.5 µL of each per 25 µL reaction |
| MQ water (Milli-Q), sterile | For the reaction |
| Agarose, lithium acetate buffer, DNA stain and DNA ladder | See the protocol "Agarose Gel Electrophoresis (Lithium Acetate)" |
| Monarch Spin DNA Gel Extraction Kit | New England Biolabs, cat. no. T1120 |
| Monarch Buffer BY (gel dissolving) | 4 volumes per volume of gel slice |
| Monarch Buffer WZ (wash) and Buffer EY (elution) | 2 × 200 µL and 5–20 µL per sample |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| PCR tubes or strips | One per gradient position |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL | For the gel slices and the eluates |
| Scalpel or clean blade | For excising the band |
| Monarch Spin Columns S1A and Collection Tubes | One per sample |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | As required |
| Gel tray with thick combs | For the preparative gel |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Thermocycler with gradient function | For the annealing temperature gradient |
| Horizontal electrophoresis apparatus and power supply | For the gels |
| Gel documentation system | For the images |
| Precision balance | For weighing the gel slices |
| Heating block or water bath | 50 °C, for dissolving the gel slices |
| Microcentrifuge | At least 12,000 × g |
| Microplate reader or spectrophotometer | For quantification of the eluate |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Heat-resistant (thermal) gloves | For the molten agarose |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

Additional physical hazards: molten agarose can superheat and boil over; the
power supply delivers a hazardous voltage. If a transilluminator with
ultraviolet light is used to excise the band, the skin and eyes are protected
with a face shield and the exposure of the DNA is kept short.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OneTaq 2X Master Mix with Standard Buffer | Mixture | The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-b) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| Monarch Buffer BY (gel dissolving) | Mixture | Contains a chaotropic salt. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. Flow-through should not be mixed with bleach. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| Monarch Buffer WZ | Mixture | Contains ethanol. | Keep away from ignition sources. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| Low-melting-point agarose | 9012-36-6 | Not classified as hazardous. | Molten agarose can superheat and boil over: wear thermal gloves and safety glasses. | Thermo Fisher Scientific (n.d.-b) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| SYBR Safe DNA gel stain, 10,000× in DMSO | Mixture | DNA-binding dye: to be treated as a potential mutagen. DMSO promotes skin absorption. | Wear nitrile gloves. Dispose of gels and buffer as stain-contaminated waste. | Thermo Fisher Scientific (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

<Callout title="Open question: gel visualization">

This protocol was drafted with visualization on a UV transilluminator with SYBR
Green, whereas the team gel protocol "Agarose Gel Electrophoresis (Lithium
Acetate)" uses blue light with SYBR Safe. Which applies to the backbone work
still has to be confirmed.

</Callout>

#### Procedure

**Preparing the template**

1. Isolate the pNBU2 plasmid from *E. coli* S17 λ pir (see the miniprep
   protocols) and quantify it.
2. Calculate the volume of template that gives the intended mass per reaction
   (Table 3).

<ProtocolNote>

The manufacturer recommends up to 10 ng of plasmid template per reaction. When
the calculated volume is too small to pipette accurately, a diluted template is
used rather than pipetting a fraction of a microlitre.

</ProtocolNote>

**Setting up the gradient PCR**

3. Assemble the master mix on ice for the number of gradient positions plus at
   least one spare (Table 2).

<ProtocolNote>

The components are added in the order water, primers, master mix, then mixed
briefly, aliquoted into the tubes, and the template added last.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Master mix for a temperature gradient."
  workbookTab="Backbone PCR"
  rows={[
    { key: "n", label: "Number of reactions (gradient positions plus one spare)", value: 11, unit: "reactions", input: true },
    { key: "vRxn", label: "Reaction volume", value: 25, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "cPrimerStock", label: "Primer stock concentration", value: 10, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "cPrimerFinal", label: "Primer final concentration", value: 0.2, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "vTemplate", label: "Template per reaction", value: 0.5, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vMix", label: "OneTaq 2× Master Mix per reaction", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vRxn / 2 },
    { key: "vPrimer", label: "Each primer per reaction", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => (v.vRxn * v.cPrimerFinal) / v.cPrimerStock },
    { key: "vWater", label: "MQ water per reaction", unit: "µL", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.vRxn - v.vMix - 2 * v.vPrimer - v.vTemplate },
    { key: "vMixTotal", label: "OneTaq 2× Master Mix, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vMix * v.n },
    { key: "vPrimerTotal", label: "Each primer, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vPrimer * v.n },
    { key: "vTemplateTotal", label: "Template, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vTemplate * v.n },
    { key: "vWaterTotal", label: "MQ water, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vWater * v.n },
  ]}
/>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Template volume and extension time."
  workbookTab="Backbone PCR"
  rows={[
    { key: "conc", label: "Plasmid concentration", value: 73.37, unit: "ng/µL", input: true },
    { key: "mass", label: "Template mass per reaction", value: 10, unit: "ng", input: true },
    { key: "vTemplate", label: "Template volume", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.mass / v.conc },
    { key: "length", label: "Amplicon length", value: 5.562, unit: "kb", input: true },
    { key: "rate", label: "Extension rate", value: 1, unit: "min/kb", input: true },
    { key: "time", label: "Extension time", unit: "min", compute: (v) => v.length * v.rate },
  ]}
/>

4. Run the program of Table 4, with the annealing step set as a gradient across
   the range of the primer pair.

**Table 4.** Cycling program for the backbone amplification.

| Step | Temperature (°C) | Time | Cycles |
|---|---|---|---|
| Initial denaturation | 95 | 30 s | 1 |
| Denaturation | 95 | 20 s | 30 |
| Annealing | Gradient, 52–61 | 1 min | 30 |
| Extension | 68 | 1 min per kb | 30 |
| Final extension | 68 | 15 min | 1 |
| Cooling | 16 | Hold | – |

**Screening gel**

5. Run a small volume of each gradient reaction on an analytical gel and
   identify the positions that give a single band of the expected size (see the
   protocol "Agarose Gel Electrophoresis (Lithium Acetate)").

<ProtocolNote>

The screening gel is run only to choose the annealing temperatures; the bulk of
each reaction is kept for the preparative gel.

</ProtocolNote>

**Preparative gel and extraction**

6. Pour a thicker gel with thick combs and load the remaining volume of the
   selected reactions.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The gel is extracted to remove the circular plasmid template. Without this, the
transformation after assembly yields a mixture of colonies carrying the new
construct and colonies carrying the original vector, which has to be resolved
by screening many colonies.

</ProtocolNote>

7. Excise the band with a clean blade, trimming away excess agarose, transfer
   it to a microcentrifuge tube and weigh the slice.

<ProtocolNote>

Trimming the agarose around the band reduces the amount of dissolving buffer
needed and shortens the dissolving time. Exposure to ultraviolet light is kept
to a minimum, because it damages the DNA.

</ProtocolNote>

8. Add 4 volumes of Monarch Buffer BY per volume of gel slice, taking 100 mg of
   agarose as 100 µL (Table 5).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={5}
  title="Gel extraction: dissolving buffer."
  workbookTab="Backbone PCR"
  rows={[
    { key: "mass", label: "Mass of the excised gel slice", value: 307, unit: "mg", input: true },
    { key: "ratio", label: "Volumes of Monarch Buffer BY per volume of gel", value: 4, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "vBY", label: "Monarch Buffer BY to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.mass * v.ratio },
    { key: "loads", label: "Loads of 800 µL required", unit: "loads", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => Math.ceil((v.mass + v.vBY) / 800) },
  ]}
/>

<ProtocolNote>

A large slice is split over several tubes, or loaded on the column in
successive spins of 800 µL.

</ProtocolNote>

9. Incubate at 50 °C, vortexing periodically, until the slice has completely
   dissolved, which takes 5 to 10 minutes.
10. Load the dissolved sample onto a Monarch Spin Column S1A, spin for 1 minute
    and discard the flow-through.
11. Wash with 200 µL of Monarch Buffer WZ and spin for 1 minute; repeat the
    wash once.
12. Transfer the column to a clean 1.5 mL tube, add 5 to 20 µL of Monarch
    Buffer EY to the centre of the matrix, wait 1 minute and spin for 1 minute.
13. Quantify the purified backbone and store it at −20 °C.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The concentration of the eluate decides whether the assembly can be set up. A
very low concentration means the amplification or the extraction has to be
repeated.

</ProtocolNote>

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

The amplification was run as a gradient from 52 to 61 °C on 1 and 2 September
2026. The amplicon is 5.562 kb, which at 1 min/kb needs an extension of about
6 minutes.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

The first gel extraction gave 0.09 ng/µL, which was too low to use, and the
amplification had to be repeated the following day.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

The gel slices were too large for the amount of dissolving buffer that fits one
tube and were split over five tubes weighing 307, 339, 271, 260 and 254 mg.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

The plasmid preparation used as template measured 73.37 ng/µL with an
A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀ ratio of 1.89 and an A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio of 0.56, because only one of the
two recommended washes had been performed.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The draft records 0.5 µL of template per reaction, whereas 10 ng from a
73.37 ng/µL preparation is 0.14 µL. The larger volume was used because it can
be pipetted accurately; this puts roughly 37 ng of template in the reaction.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Master mix for a temperature gradient**

*V*total = *V*component × *n*

in which:

- *V*component = volume of the component in one reaction (µL)
- *n* = number of reactions, being the gradient positions plus at least one spare

Because every reaction of a gradient has the same composition and differs only
in annealing temperature, the master mix can contain the template as well,
which removes a pipetting step per tube. The water volume per reaction is the
reaction volume minus every other component.

**Worked example.** For 11 reactions of 25 µL: 12.5 × 11 = 137.5 µL of OneTaq
2x Master Mix, 0.50 × 11 = 5.5 µL of each primer, 0.5 × 11 = 5.5 µL of template
and 11.00 × 11 = 121.0 µL of MQ water.

**Template volume and extension time**

*V* = *m* / *c*  and  *t* = *L* × *r*

in which:

- *V* = template volume (µL)
- *m* = template mass per reaction (ng)
- *c* = plasmid concentration (ng/µL)
- *t* = extension time (min)
- *L* = amplicon length (kb)
- *r* = extension rate (min/kb)

The manufacturer recommends up to 10 ng of plasmid template per reaction. When
the calculated volume is below what can be pipetted accurately, a larger volume
of a diluted template is used rather than pipetting a fraction of a microlitre.

**Worked example.** From a plasmid at 73.37 ng/µL, 10 ng corresponds to
0.14 µL. At 1 min/kb a 5.562 kb amplicon needs 5.56 min, rounded up to 6 min.

**Gel extraction: dissolving buffer**

*V*BY = *w* × *r*

in which:

- *V*BY = volume of Monarch Buffer BY (µL)
- *w* = mass of the excised gel slice (mg)
- *r* = volumes of buffer per volume of gel, taking 100 mg of agarose as 100 µL

The column takes 800 µL per spin, so a large slice is split over several tubes
or loaded in successive spins.

**Worked example.** For a slice of 307 mg: *V*BY = 307 × 4 = 1228 µL, which
with the gel itself needs 2 loads.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-a). *Monarch Spin DNA Gel Extraction Kit* (NEB #T1120) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/products/t1120-monarch-spin-dna-gel-extraction-kit
- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-b). *OneTaq 2X Master Mix with Standard Buffer* (NEB #M0482) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/products/m0482-onetaq-2x-master-mix-with-standard-buffer
- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-c). *Protocol for Taq 2X Master Mix* (NEB #M0270). Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/protocols/protocol-for-taq-2x-master-mix-m0270
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (n.d.-a). *SYBR Safe DNA gel stain* (Catalog No. S33102) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.thermofisher.com/order/catalog/product/S33102
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (n.d.-b). *UltraPure low melting point agarose* (Catalog No. 16520050) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.thermofisher.com/order/catalog/product/16520050

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="Miniprep of pNBU2 (Jena Bioscience Kit)">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/miniprep-jena-bioscience-kit.pdf" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the isolation of the pNBU2 plasmid from *Escherichia
coli* S17 λ pir with a column-based plasmid miniprep kit from Jena Bioscience.
It is one of three miniprep kits used by the team; the other two are covered by
their own protocols.

The kit differs from the other two in that the column is activated before the
sample is loaded, and that the neutralization buffer contains the RNase.

#### Attributions

This protocol is a rewritten version of the protocol supplied with the kit; the
manufacturer's current protocol should be consulted for the catalogue number in
use.

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol to isolate the pNBU2 plasmid
from *E. coli* S17 λ pir as the template for the backbone amplification.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Overnight culture of *E. coli* S17 λ pir carrying pNBU2 | 1–3 mL per preparation |
| Plasmid miniprep kit, column based | Jena Bioscience; the catalogue number of the kit in use applies |
| Lysis Buffer | 300 µL per preparation |
| Neutralization Buffer with RNase A | 300 µL per preparation |
| Activation Buffer | 100 µL per preparation |
| Washing Buffer, containing ethanol | 500 µL, and a further 700 µL for the optional second wash |
| Elution Buffer or distilled water | 30–50 µL per preparation |
| Sodium acetate, 3 M, pH 5.0 | For correcting the pH of the binding mixture if required |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Binding columns and 2 mL collection tubes | One each per preparation (supplied with the kit) |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL | One per preparation; not supplied with the kit |
| Micropipettes and tips | 20–200 µL and 100–1000 µL |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Microcentrifuge | 10,000 × g |
| Vortex mixer | For resuspension |
| Freezer | −20 °C |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

The compositions of the kit buffers are proprietary. The lysis and
neutralization buffers of plasmid kits of this type contain irritants, and the
washing buffer contains ethanol.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lysis Buffer and Neutralization Buffer | Mixtures | Contain irritants; the SDS of the kit in use should be consulted. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. Avoid contact with skin and eyes. | Supplier SDS |
| Washing Buffer | Mixture | Contains ethanol. | Keep away from ignition sources. | Supplier SDS |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Harvesting and lysis**

1. Harvest 1 to 3 mL of the bacterial culture by centrifugation and discard the
   supernatant.
2. Resuspend the pellet in 300 µL of Lysis Buffer by pipetting or vortexing for
   1 minute.

**Neutralization**

3. Add 300 µL of Neutralization Buffer, which contains the RNase A, and mix
   gently by inverting the tube 4 to 6 times.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The tube must not be vortexed at this step.

</ProtocolNote>

4. Centrifuge at 10,000 × g for 5 minutes at room temperature.

<ProtocolNote>

The binding mixture should turn bright yellow, which indicates the pH of 7.5
needed for the DNA to bind. An orange or violet colour indicates a pH above 7.5
and poor binding; in that case the pH is corrected with a small volume of 3 M
sodium acetate at pH 5.0 before continuing.

</ProtocolNote>

**Activating the column**

5. Place a binding column into a 2 mL collection tube.
6. Add 100 µL of Activation Buffer to the column and centrifuge at 10,000 × g
   for 30 seconds.

<ProtocolNote>

This activation step is particular to this kit; the other miniprep kits used by
the team load the sample directly onto the column.

</ProtocolNote>

**Loading and washing**

7. Apply the supernatant from the neutralization step to the activated column
   by decanting or pipetting.
8. Centrifuge at 10,000 × g for 30 seconds and discard the flow-through.
9. Place the column back into the used collection tube, add 500 µL of Washing
   Buffer and centrifuge at 10,000 × g for 30 seconds. Discard the flow-through.
10. For DNA larger than 200 bp that has to be highly pure, for example for
    sequencing, add a further 700 µL of Washing Buffer and centrifuge at
    10,000 × g for 30 seconds. Discard the flow-through.
11. Centrifuge the empty column for a further 2 minutes to remove residual
    Washing Buffer.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

This dry spin is essential: residual ethanol carried into the eluate interferes
with downstream enzymatic reactions and with sequencing.

</ProtocolNote>

**Elution**

12. Place the column into a clean 1.5 mL microcentrifuge tube.
13. Add 30 to 50 µL of Elution Buffer or distilled water to the centre of the
    column membrane.
14. Incubate for 1 minute at room temperature and centrifuge at 10,000 × g for
    1 minute.
15. Quantify the plasmid (see the protocol "DNA Quantification with the LVis
    Plate") and store it at −20 °C.

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

This kit was used on 12 August 2026 to prepare the pNBU2 template for the
backbone amplification. The preparation measured 73.37 ng/µL with an
A₂₆₀/A₂₈₀ ratio of 1.89 and an A₂₆₀/A₂₃₀ ratio of 0.56, because only one wash
was performed where two are recommended.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The team now uses three miniprep kits. Which kit is used for which purpose
should be recorded, so that the quantification results stay comparable.

</ProtocolNote>

<Callout title="Still to be added">

The catalogue number of the kit was not given in the draft and still needs to
be added, together with a link to the manufacturer protocol for that catalogue
number.

</Callout>

</ExpandableText>

### Activity Assay

<ExpandableText title="Liquid Bile Salt Assay (LC-MS readout)">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/liquid-bile-salt-assay-5.pdf" workbookTab="Bile salt assay" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes a liquid culture assay for bile salt hydrolase (BSH)
activity. *E. coli* strains expressing the BSH genes are grown for 24 hours in
medium containing a bile salt; the proteins are then precipitated from the
culture supernatant with methanol, and the samples are analysed by LC-MS.

Ideally, the experiment is performed in minimal medium for *E. coli*: this
reduces the background from other compounds, and *E. coli* grows well in this
medium. LB can also be used, but background levels of bile acids and other
compounds will then be detected. Kanamycin is added to maintain selection for
the plasmid and to reduce the risk of contamination.

The minimal medium prepared here is the aerobic formulation for *E. coli*, and
it is not the same medium as the one used for *P. vulgatus*. The two share the
LB, sodium bicarbonate, glucose and L-cysteine content, but the *E. coli*
medium contains no hemin, because *E. coli* synthesizes its own heme and does
not depend on an external supply. The anaerobic medium for *P. vulgatus*, which
does contain hemin, is a separate protocol (see "Preparation of Minimal Medium
for *P. vulgatus*") and is not interchangeable with this one.

The protocol was executed with GUDCA and TUDCA at 0.1% and 0.25% (w/v): 16
strains, 2 concentrations, 2 bile salts and 2 replicates, giving 128 samples.
The assay was run twice; the procedure below is that of the second run, and the
differences of the first run are given in the notes.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Glycerol stocks of the sequence-validated BSH *E. coli* strains | 16 strains, including the sfGFP control strain (H2) |
| LB agar plates with kanamycin (50 µg/mL) | 16 |
| LB | 10 g/L of minimal medium for *E. coli* |
| Sodium bicarbonate | 1.25 g/L of minimal medium for *E. coli* |
| Glucose | 0.2 g/L of minimal medium for *E. coli* |
| L-cysteine | 0.5 g/L of minimal medium for *E. coli* |
| Kanamycin stock, 50 mg/mL | 2 µL per 2 mL culture |
| GUDCA | 102.5 mg per mL of stock |
| TUDCA | 102.5 mg per mL of stock |
| DMSO, anhydrous | 1 mL per bile salt stock |
| Methanol, ice-cold (4 °C) | 400 µL per sample |
| Methanol, 50% in water | 100 or 250 µL per sample |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Culture tubes | 1 per overnight culture, sample and control |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL | 2 per sample |
| Inoculation loops | For streaking and picking colonies |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | As required |
| Bottle, 500 mL | For the minimal medium for *E. coli* |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Precision balance | Readability 0.1 mg for the bile salts |
| pH meter | For adjusting the medium to pH 6.5–7 |
| Incubator and shaking incubator | 37 °C |
| Vortex mixer and sonicator | For dissolving the bile salts |
| Centrifuge | 6000 × g and 14,000 × g |
| Freezer | −20 °C |
| SpeedVac vacuum concentrator | For evaporation of the methanol |
| LC-MS system | For analysis of the samples |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Methanol | 67-56-1 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H301 + H311 + H331: toxic if swallowed, in contact with skin or if inhaled. H370: causes damage to organs. | Handle in a fume hood. Wear gloves and safety glasses. Keep away from ignition sources. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-f) Product page; SDS under "Documentation" |
| Dimethyl sulfoxide (DMSO) | 67-68-5 | H227: combustible liquid. Rapidly absorbed through the skin. | Wear nitrile gloves. Dissolved substances can be carried through the skin. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) |
| Kanamycin sulfate | 25389-94-0 | Danger. H360D: may damage the unborn child (Repr. 1B). | Wear gloves, lab coat and safety glasses. Weigh powder in a fume hood. Not to be handled during pregnancy. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-d) |
| Glycoursodeoxycholic acid (GUDCA) | 64480-66-6 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Avoid dust formation. The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-c) Product page |
| Sodium tauroursodeoxycholate (TUDCA) | 35807-85-3 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Avoid dust formation. The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-h) Product page |
| Sodium bicarbonate | 144-55-8 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Standard laboratory practice. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-g) Product page |
| D-(+)-Glucose | 50-99-7 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Standard laboratory practice. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) Product page |
| L-Cysteine | 52-90-4 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Standard laboratory practice. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-e) Product page |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

#### Procedure

**Planning the experiment**

1. Determine the number of samples and the volume of minimal medium for
   *E. coli* required (Table 2).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Number of samples and medium required."
  workbookTab="Bile salt assay"
  rows={[
    { key: "strains", label: "Number of strains (including the sfGFP control strain)", value: 16, unit: "strains", input: true },
    { key: "concs", label: "Number of bile salt concentrations", value: 2, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "salts", label: "Number of bile salts", value: 2, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "reps", label: "Replicates", value: 2, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "vTube", label: "Culture volume per tube", value: 2, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "sampleTubes", label: "Sample tubes", unit: "tubes", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.strains * v.concs * v.salts * v.reps },
    { key: "vSamples", label: "Medium for the sample tubes", unit: "mL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.sampleTubes * v.vTube },
    { key: "overnightTubes", label: "Overnight cultures (strains × replicates)", unit: "tubes", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.strains * v.reps },
    { key: "vOvernight", label: "Medium for the overnight cultures", unit: "mL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.overnightTubes * v.vTube },
    { key: "negTubes", label: "Negative control tubes (medium, kanamycin and bile salt)", unit: "tubes", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.concs * v.salts * v.reps },
    { key: "vNeg", label: "Medium for the negative controls", unit: "mL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.negTubes * v.vTube },
    { key: "vAll", label: "Total medium (excluding the medium-only control)", unit: "mL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.vSamples + v.vOvernight + v.vNeg },
  ]}
/>

**Day 0: minimal medium for *E. coli***

2. Prepare 400 mL of minimal medium for *E. coli*, containing 10 g/L LB,
   1.25 g/L sodium bicarbonate, 0.2 g/L glucose and 0.5 g/L L-cysteine
   (Table 3).

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

No hemin is added. This is the aerobic medium for *E. coli* and is a different
medium from the anaerobic minimal medium for *P. vulgatus*, which contains
hemin at 0.01 g/L. The two must not be substituted for each other.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Minimal medium components."
  workbookTab="Bile salt assay"
  rows={[
    { key: "vMedium", label: "Medium volume", value: 400, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "cLB", label: "LB", value: 10, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cBicarb", label: "Sodium bicarbonate", value: 1.25, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cGlucose", label: "Glucose", value: 0.2, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "cCys", label: "L-cysteine", value: 0.5, unit: "g/L", input: true },
    { key: "mLB", label: "LB to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.cLB * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mBicarb", label: "Sodium bicarbonate to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.cBicarb * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mGlucose", label: "Glucose to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.cGlucose * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
    { key: "mCys", label: "L-cysteine to weigh", unit: "g", decimals: 3, compute: (v) => (v.cCys * v.vMedium) / 1000 },
  ]}
/>

3. Adjust the pH to 6.5 to 7.

**Day 1: plating from the cryostocks**

4. Streak the 16 sequence-validated BSH *E. coli* strains from the glycerol
   stocks onto LB agar plates containing kanamycin (50 µg/mL) and let them grow
   overnight.

**Day 2: overnight cultures**

5. Pick a single colony of each strain, inoculate it into 2 mL of minimal
   medium containing kanamycin (2 µL of the 50 mg/mL stock) and let it grow
   overnight.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

At this stage, the medium should not contain bile acids.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote>

It is recommended to inoculate the overnight cultures in replicates, to ensure
that grown cultures are available on the next day (16 strains × 2 mL × 2
replicates = 64 mL of minimal medium).

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote>

In the first run, overnight cultures of 5 mL were used (5 µL of kanamycin stock
per culture, 80 mL of minimal medium in total).

</ProtocolNote>

**Day 3: main experiment**

6. Prepare the GUDCA stock: weigh 102.5 mg of GUDCA and add 1 mL of anhydrous
   DMSO. Vortex, then sonicate if needed, until the bile salt is fully
   dissolved and the solution is clear (102.5 mg/mL).
7. Prepare the TUDCA stock in the same way: 102.5 mg of TUDCA in 1 mL of
   anhydrous DMSO (102.5 mg/mL).

<ProtocolNote>

In the first run, one combined stock was used (102.5 mg of GUDCA and 102.5 mg
of TUDCA in 1 mL of DMSO, 205 mg/mL) at total bile acid concentrations of 0.5%
and 1.0% in 3 mL cultures (73 µL or 146 µL of stock per tube). Only the 0.5%
samples were analysed. When bile acids are combined, their combined
concentration should add up to the target concentration; it should not be the
concentration of each individual bile acid. Some bile acids may also
precipitate when combined with others.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote>

For the second run, the concentrations were lowered to 0.1% and 0.25%, because
the bile salts are expensive and the LC-MS instrument had enough range to allow
lower concentrations. Lower concentrations also give more information, because
the bile salts are then not at saturation.

</ProtocolNote>

8. For each sample, combine the bile salt stock, minimal medium and kanamycin
   in a tube to a volume of 1980 µL (Table 4). For 0.1%: 19.5 µL of stock,
   1958.5 µL of minimal medium and 2 µL of kanamycin. For 0.25%: 48.8 µL of
   stock, 1929.2 µL of minimal medium and 2 µL of kanamycin.

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={4}
  title="Composition of one sample tube."
  workbookTab="Bile salt assay"
  rows={[
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Total culture volume", value: 2000, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "pBile", label: "Target bile salt concentration", value: 0.1, unit: "% (w/v)", input: true },
    { key: "cStock", label: "Bile salt stock concentration", value: 102.5, unit: "mg/mL", input: true },
    { key: "cKanStock", label: "Kanamycin stock concentration", value: 50, unit: "mg/mL", input: true },
    { key: "cKanFinal", label: "Kanamycin final concentration", value: 50, unit: "µg/mL", input: true },
    { key: "dilution", label: "Dilution of the overnight culture (1 in …)", value: 100, unit: "fold", input: true },
    { key: "mBile", label: "Bile salt per tube", unit: "mg", compute: (v) => (v.pBile * 10 * v.vTotal) / 1000 },
    { key: "vBile", label: "Bile salt stock to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.mBile / v.cStock) * 1000 },
    { key: "vKan", label: "Kanamycin stock to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.cKanFinal * v.vTotal) / (v.cKanStock * 1000) },
    { key: "vCulture", label: "Overnight culture to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vTotal / v.dilution },
    { key: "vMedium", label: "Minimal medium to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vTotal - v.vBile - v.vKan - v.vCulture },
  ]}
/>

9. Add 20 µL of the overnight culture to each tube (1:100 subculture).

<ProtocolNote>

After addition of the culture, each tube contains 2 mg of bile salt (1 mg/mL,
0.1% w/v) or 5 mg of bile salt (2.5 mg/mL, 0.25% w/v).

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote>

Ideally, the experiment is performed in triplicate. If this results in too many
samples, it should be performed at least in duplicate.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote>

The control strain that expresses only sfGFP (H2) is one of the 16 strains and
is therefore already included.

</ProtocolNote>

10. Prepare the negative controls: 2 mL of minimal medium with 2 µL of
    kanamycin and bile salt, without culture, in duplicate for each bile salt
    and each concentration (8 tubes), and 2 mL of minimal medium only.

<ProtocolNote>

A DMSO vehicle control could be included to assess potential toxicity of the
vehicle. It was left out, because these samples would not be analysed for bile
acid quantification and would add a considerable number of tubes and additional
work.

</ProtocolNote>

11. Incubate the cultures under aerobic conditions with good shaking at 37 °C
    for 24 hours.

**Day 4: protein precipitation**

12. Centrifuge the samples at 6000 × g for 5 minutes to remove all cell debris.

<ProtocolNote>

In the first run, the cultures were first centrifuged at 4500 rpm for
15 minutes to separate the cells from the spent medium, with a second
centrifugation at maximum speed for 10 minutes for samples that did not form a
pellet directly. The remaining spent medium was stored at −20 °C, properly
labelled, in case the samples needed to be analysed again.

</ProtocolNote>

13. Transfer 100 µL of supernatant to a new 1.5 mL tube and add 400 µL of
    ice-cold methanol (4 °C).
14. Leave the samples at −20 °C for at least 1 hour.

<ProtocolNote>

The proteins present in the sample precipitate during this step.

</ProtocolNote>

15. Centrifuge at 14,000 × g for 15 minutes.

<ProtocolNote>

The precipitated proteins form a pellet.

</ProtocolNote>

16. Transfer 200 µL of the clear supernatant to a new tube and evaporate it in
    the SpeedVac for 1 hour.
17. Add 50% methanol in water according to the bile salt concentration of the
    sample (Table 5): 100 µL for 0.1% and 250 µL for 0.25%.

<ProtocolNote>

In the first run, 50 µL of 50% methanol was added to all samples.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={5}
  title="Reconstitution volume after evaporation."
  workbookTab="Bile salt assay"
  rows={[
    { key: "pBile", label: "Bile salt concentration of the sample", value: 0.1, unit: "% (w/v)", input: true },
    { key: "factor", label: "Reconstitution volume per 1% bile salt", value: 1000, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vMeOH", label: "50% methanol to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.pBile * v.factor },
  ]}
/>

18. Centrifuge at 14,000 × g for 15 minutes.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The tubes should not be shaken or mixed after this centrifugation.

</ProtocolNote>

19. Analyse the samples by LC-MS.

**Sample overview**

**Table 6.** Sample numbers per strain, bile salt and concentration.

| Strain | GUDCA 0.1% | GUDCA 0.25% | TUDCA 0.1% | TUDCA 0.25% |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 |
| B1 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 |
| C1 | 9 | 10 | 11 | 12 |
| D1 | 13 | 14 | 15 | 16 |
| E1 | 17 | 18 | 19 | 20 |
| F1 | 21 | 22 | 23 | 24 |
| G1 | 25 | 26 | 27 | 28 |
| H1 | 29 | 30 | 31 | 32 |
| A2 | 33 | 34 | 35 | 36 |
| B2 | 37 | 38 | 39 | 40 |
| C2 | 41 | 42 | 43 | 44 |
| D2 | 45 | 46 | 47 | 48 |
| E2 | 49 | 50 | 51 | 52 |
| F2 | 53 | 54 | 55 | 56 |
| G2 | 57 | 58 | 59 | 60 |
| H2 | 61 | 62 | 63 | 64 |

For the full sample list in Excel, go to Protocol_Calculators.xlsx, tab "Bile
salt assay" (Table 6).

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Number of samples and volume of medium**

*N* = *g* × *c* × *b* × *r*  and  *V* = *N* × *V*t

in which:

- *N* = number of sample tubes
- *g* = number of strains
- *c* = number of bile salt concentrations
- *b* = number of bile salts
- *r* = number of replicates
- *V* = volume of medium required (mL)
- *V*t = culture volume per tube (mL)

The overnight cultures (strains × replicates) and the negative controls
(concentrations × bile salts × replicates) are counted separately and added to
the total.

**Worked example.** For 16 strains, 2 concentrations, 2 bile salts and 2
replicates: *N* = 16 × 2 × 2 × 2 = 128 tubes, requiring 128 × 2 = 256 mL, plus
64 mL for the overnight cultures and 16 mL for the negative controls, giving
336 mL in total.

**Mass of the minimal medium components**

*m* = (*c* × *V*) / 1000

in which:

- *m* = mass of powder to weigh (g)
- *c* = concentration specified by the manufacturer (g/L)
- *V* = volume of medium (mL)

The factor 1000 converts millilitres to litres.

**Worked example.** For 400 mL of medium: LB at 10 g/L gives (10 × 400) / 1000
= 4.000 g, and glucose at 0.2 g/L gives (0.2 × 400) / 1000 = 0.080 g.

**Composition of one sample tube**

*m* = (*p* × 10 × *V*t) / 1000  and  *V*b = (*m* / *c*1) × 1000

in which:

- *p* = required bile salt concentration (% w/v)
- *V*t = total culture volume (µL)
- *m* = mass of bile salt per tube (mg)
- *c*1 = concentration of the bile salt stock (mg/mL)
- *V*b = volume of stock to add (µL)

The kanamycin volume follows the dilution equation, and the inoculum is the
total volume divided by the dilution factor. The volume of minimal medium is
the total volume minus the bile salt, kanamycin and culture volumes.

**Worked example.** For 0.1% in a 2000 µL tube from a 102.5 mg/mL stock: *m* =
(0.1 × 10 × 2000) / 1000 = 2.00 mg and *V*b = (2.00 / 102.5) × 1000 = 19.5 µL.
With 2.0 µL of kanamycin and 20.0 µL of culture, the medium volume is
2000 − 19.5 − 2 − 20 = 1958.5 µL.

**Reconstitution volume after evaporation**

*V* = *p* × *f*

in which:

- *V* = volume of 50% methanol to add (µL)
- *p* = bile salt concentration of the sample (% w/v)
- *f* = reconstitution volume per 1% bile salt (µL)

The volume is scaled with the bile salt concentration so that the concentration
in the injected sample stays within the range of the LC-MS method.

**Worked example.** For a 0.1% sample at 1000 µL per 1%: *V* = 0.1 × 1000 =
100 µL. A 0.25% sample receives 250 µL.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-a). *D-(+)-Glucose* (Product No. G7021) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/sigma/g7021
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-b). *Dimethyl sulfoxide* (Product No. D8418) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/d8418
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-c). *Glycoursodeoxycholic acid* (Product No. 06863) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/sigma/06863
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-d). *Kanamycin sulfate from Streptomyces kanamyceticus* (Product No. K1377) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/k1377
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-e). *L-Cysteine* (Product No. 168149) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/aldrich/168149
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-f). *Methanol, suitable for HPLC, ≥99.9%* (Product No. 34860) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/sigald/34860
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-g). *Sodium bicarbonate* (Product No. S5761) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/sigma/s5761
- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-h). *Sodium tauroursodeoxycholate* (Product No. T0266) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/product/sigma/t0266

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="LC-MS Quantification of Bile Acid Deconjugation">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/lc-ms-quantification-of-bile-acid-deconjugation.pdf" workbookTab="LC-MS deconjugation" />

#### Introduction

Bile salt hydrolase cleaves the amide bond that joins a bile acid to its
conjugated amino acid, releasing the free bile acid. In this project the
substrates are glycoursodeoxycholic acid (GUDCA) and tauroursodeoxycholic acid
(TUDCA), and the product of both reactions is ursodeoxycholic acid (UDCA). The
extent of that conversion is what this method measures.

Substrate and product differ in mass and in retention, so they can be
separated on a reversed-phase column and detected individually by mass
spectrometry. The eluate is monitored in negative electrospray ionization at
the deprotonated mass of each of the three compounds, and the result is
reported as a percentage of deconjugation: the signal of UDCA as a fraction of
the signal of UDCA plus the remaining conjugate.

The measurement is relative rather than absolute. No calibration curve and no
internal standard are used, and the calculation assumes that the three
compounds give a similar response in the source. The output is therefore a
comparison between samples, which is what the ranking of the constructs
requires, and not a concentration.

The samples are the culture supernatants of the liquid bile salt assay.
Proteins are removed by precipitation with cold methanol before injection,
because protein carried onto the column fouls it and suppresses ionization
(Want et al., 2011).

#### Attributions

The experimental method described here was supplied by Jef Rozenski, Professor
of Medicinal Chemistry (Rega Institute) and Faculty of Pharmaceutical Sciences
at KU Leuven, who performed the LC-MS analysis for the iGEM KU Leuven 2026
team. The instrument settings, the gradient, the monitored masses and the
calculation of the percentage of deconjugation are his. The rationale for
removing protein before reversed-phase separation of bile acids follows the
published application of the technique to biological fluids (Want et al.,
2011).

#### Materials

**Samples**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Culture supernatant from the liquid bile salt assay | 100 µL per sample; see the protocol "Liquid Bile Salt Assay (LC-MS readout)" |

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Methanol, HPLC grade | 400 µL per sample for the precipitation, kept at 4 °C; and 50% (v/v) in MQ water for the reconstitution |
| Acetonitrile, gradient grade for HPLC | Mobile phase B |
| Ammonium formate, eluent additive for LC-MS | 0.05 M in MQ water; mobile phase A |
| MQ water (Milli-Q) | For the mobile phase and the reconstitution solvent |
| UDCA, GUDCA and TUDCA reference standards | For establishing the retention times before the samples are run |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL | Two per sample |
| HPLC vials with low-volume inserts and caps | One per sample |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | 20–200 µL and 100–1000 µL |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Microcentrifuge | 6000 × g for the cell debris and 14,000 × g for the precipitated protein |
| Freezer | −20 °C, for the precipitation step |
| Vacuum concentrator (SpeedVac) | For evaporating the extract to dryness |
| HPLC system | Waters Alliance, with a Waters XBridge C18 column, 100 × 2.1 mm, 3.5 µm particle size |
| Tandem mass spectrometer | Waters Quattro Premier with an electrospray ionization source |

**Software**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Chromatography data system of the mass spectrometer | For acquisition in selected ion recording and for integrating the ion chromatograms |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

Methanol and acetonitrile are both volatile and toxic. All pipetting of these
solvents, the preparation of the mobile phases and the reconstitution step are
carried out in a fume hood, and the solvents are kept away from ignition
sources. The vacuum concentrator is vented into the fume hood or fitted with a
solvent trap.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Methanol | 67-56-1 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H301 + H311 + H331: toxic if swallowed, in contact with skin or if inhaled. H370: causes damage to organs. | Handle in a fume hood. Wear gloves and safety glasses. Keep away from ignition sources. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-d) Product page; SDS under "Documentation" |
| Acetonitrile | 75-05-8 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H302 + H312 + H332: harmful if swallowed, in contact with skin or if inhaled. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Handle in a fume hood. Wear gloves and safety glasses. Keep away from ignition sources. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) Product page; SDS under "Documentation" |
| Ammonium formate | 540-69-2 | Warning. H315: causes skin irritation. H319: causes serious eye irritation. H335: may cause respiratory irritation. | Avoid dust formation when weighing. Wear gloves and safety glasses. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) Product page; SDS under "Documentation" |
| Ursodeoxycholic acid (UDCA) | 128-13-2 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Avoid dust formation. The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-f) Product page |
| Glycoursodeoxycholic acid (GUDCA) | 64480-66-6 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Avoid dust formation. The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-c) Product page |
| Sodium tauroursodeoxycholate (TUDCA) | 35807-85-3 | No hazard statements listed on the product page. | Avoid dust formation. The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-e) Product page |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures and contaminated consumables are inactivated
by autoclaving before disposal. Methanol and acetonitrile, and any sample
extract containing them, are collected as halogen-free organic solvent waste
and are not poured down the sink. The waste and disposal instructions of the
laboratory in use apply.

#### Procedure

**Sample preparation: removing cells and protein**

1. Centrifuge the culture medium samples at 6000 × g for 5 minutes to remove
   cell debris.

<ProtocolNote>

The samples come from the liquid bile salt assay. Whether they were handled in
any other way before this step has not been recorded; see the notes from the
bench.

</ProtocolNote>

2. Transfer a 100 µL aliquot of the resulting supernatant to a
   microcentrifuge tube.
3. Add 400 µL of ice-cold methanol at 4 °C to precipitate the proteins, and
   mix (Table 2).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Methanol precipitation of protein."
  workbookTab="LC-MS deconjugation"
  rows={[
    { key: "vSample", label: "Culture supernatant taken", value: 100, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vMeOH", label: "Ice-cold methanol added", value: 400, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Extract after mixing", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.vSample + v.vMeOH },
    { key: "pOrganic", label: "Methanol content of the extract", unit: "% (v/v)", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.vMeOH / v.vTotal) * 100 },
  ]}
/>

<ProtocolNote>

Four volumes of methanol to one volume of sample gives a final organic content
of 80%, which is what precipitates the protein. The methanol is used cold
because the precipitation is more complete and the bile acids are more stable
at low temperature.

</ProtocolNote>

4. Incubate the samples at −20 °C for 1 hour.
5. Centrifuge the samples at 14,000 × g for 15 minutes.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The supernatant transferred in the next step must be clear. Any precipitate
carried into the vial reaches the column and shortens its life.

</ProtocolNote>

**Sample preparation: concentrating and reconstituting**

6. Transfer a 200 µL aliquot of the clear supernatant to a separate tube.
7. Evaporate the aliquot to complete dryness in the vacuum concentrator.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The samples are dried to complete dryness, however long that takes. One hour
in the vacuum concentrator is not enough for 200 µL of 80% methanol, and a
residue that still holds solvent gives a reconstitution volume that is not the
volume assumed in the calculation.

</ProtocolNote>

8. Reconstitute the dried residue in 100 µL of 50% (v/v) methanol.

<ProtocolNote>

The reconstitution solvent is weaker than the extract it replaces, which keeps
the sample compatible with the starting conditions of the gradient. A sample
injected in a solvent stronger than the mobile phase elutes as a broadened or
split peak.

</ProtocolNote>

9. Calculate the net dilution of the sample relative to the original culture
   medium, and record it with the data (Table 3).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Net dilution of the sample."
  workbookTab="LC-MS deconjugation"
  rows={[
    { key: "vSample", label: "Culture supernatant taken", value: 100, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Extract after mixing", value: 500, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vAliquot", label: "Clear supernatant taken forward and dried", value: 200, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vRecon", label: "50% methanol used for the reconstitution", value: 100, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "dExtract", label: "Dilution caused by the precipitation", unit: "fold", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.vTotal / v.vSample },
    { key: "cAliquot", label: "Concentration by drying and reconstitution", unit: "fold", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.vAliquot / v.vRecon },
    { key: "dNet", label: "Net dilution relative to the culture medium", unit: "fold", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.dExtract / v.cAliquot },
  ]}
/>

10. Transfer the reconstituted samples to HPLC vials with inserts.

**Chromatographic separation**

11. Install the Waters XBridge C18 column, 100 × 2.1 mm with a 3.5 µm particle
    size, on the Waters Alliance HPLC system.
12. Prepare mobile phase A as 0.05 M ammonium formate in MQ water, and use
    acetonitrile as mobile phase B.

<ProtocolNote>

Ammonium formate is volatile and compatible with electrospray ionization; a
non-volatile buffer such as phosphate would deposit in the source and
suppress the signal.

</ProtocolNote>

13. Set the flow rate to 200 µL/min and the injection volume to 5 µL.
14. Program the linear gradient (Table 4).

**Table 4.** Gradient elution program. Mobile phase A is 0.05 M ammonium
formate in MQ water and mobile phase B is acetonitrile.

| Time (min) | Mobile phase A (%) | Mobile phase B (%) | Profile |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 85 | 15 | Initial |
| 1 | 85 | 15 | Isocratic hold |
| 10 | 10 | 90 | Linear decrease of A from 85% to 10% |
| 15 | 10 | 90 | Isocratic hold |

15. Inject the UDCA, GUDCA and TUDCA reference standards before the samples
    and record the retention time of each.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The three compounds are identified by retention time as well as by mass. The
two conjugates are more polar than the free bile acid and elute earlier;
without the standards run on the same column and the same gradient, a peak
cannot be assigned with confidence.

</ProtocolNote>

**Mass spectrometric detection**

16. Couple the HPLC system to the Waters Quattro Premier tandem mass
    spectrometer and operate the electrospray source in negative ionization
    mode.

<ProtocolNote>

Bile acids carry a carboxylic acid or a sulfonic acid group and deprotonate
readily, so the negative mode gives a far better response than the positive
mode.

</ProtocolNote>

17. Set the capillary voltage to −3 kV, the cone voltage to 30 V and the
    source temperature to 120 °C.
18. Acquire the data in selected ion recording, monitoring the three masses
    with a dwell time of 0.1 s each (Table 5).

**Table 5.** Masses monitored in selected ion recording. The values are those
of the deprotonated molecule in negative ionization mode.

| Compound | m/z | Role in the assay | Dwell time |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tauro-ursodeoxycholic acid (TUDCA) | 498.1 | Conjugated substrate | 0.1 s |
| Glyco-ursodeoxycholic acid (GUDCA) | 448.1 | Conjugated substrate | 0.1 s |
| Ursodeoxycholic acid (UDCA) | 391.1 | Deconjugated product | 0.1 s |

<ProtocolNote>

Selected ion recording measures only these three masses rather than scanning
the whole range, which raises the number of measurements per peak and
therefore the sensitivity. Nothing outside the three masses is recorded, so an
unexpected product would not be seen.

</ProtocolNote>

**Data processing**

19. Integrate the ion chromatogram of each of the three masses for every
    sample.
20. Calculate the percentage of deconjugation for each conjugate from the
    integrated peak areas (Table 6).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={6}
  title="Percentage of deconjugation."
  workbookTab="LC-MS deconjugation"
  rows={[
    { key: "areaUDCA", label: "Peak area of UDCA (m/z 391.1)", value: 142000, unit: "counts", input: true },
    { key: "areaGUDCA", label: "Peak area of GUDCA (m/z 448.1)", value: 331000, unit: "counts", input: true },
    { key: "areaTUDCA", label: "Peak area of TUDCA (m/z 498.1)", value: 414000, unit: "counts", input: true },
    { key: "pGUDCA", label: "Deconjugation of GUDCA", unit: "%", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.areaUDCA / (v.areaUDCA + v.areaGUDCA)) * 100 },
    { key: "pTUDCA", label: "Deconjugation of TUDCA", unit: "%", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.areaUDCA / (v.areaUDCA + v.areaTUDCA)) * 100 },
  ]}
/>

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The calculation assumes that UDCA and the conjugate give a similar response in
the source. That assumption is what allows peak areas to stand in for amounts
without a calibration curve, and it is also what makes the result a comparison
rather than a concentration. Absolute quantification requires calibration
curves of the authentic standards, and ideally isotope-labelled internal
standards.

</ProtocolNote>

21. Report the percentage of deconjugation per sample, alongside the strain,
    the bile salt and the concentration it was grown with.

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Methanol precipitation of protein**

*V*total = *V*sample + *V*MeOH  and  organic % = *V*MeOH / *V*total × 100

in which:

- *V*sample = volume of culture supernatant taken (µL)
- *V*MeOH = volume of ice-cold methanol added (µL)
- *V*total = volume of the extract after mixing (µL)
- organic % = methanol content of the extract, as a percentage by volume

Protein precipitates when the organic content of the solution is high enough
that the solvent no longer keeps it folded and in solution. Four volumes of
methanol to one of sample puts the extract at 80% methanol, which is the usual
working point for this kind of sample. The methanol is used cold, which makes
the precipitation more complete and limits degradation of the bile acids.

**Worked example.** For 100 µL of supernatant and 400 µL of methanol: *V*total
= 100 + 400 = 500 µL and the organic content is 400 / 500 × 100 = 80.0%.

**Net dilution of the sample**

*D*extract = *V*total / *V*sample  ·  *C*aliquot = *V*aliquot / *V*reconstitution  ·  *D*net = *D*extract / *C*aliquot

in which:

- *D*extract = dilution of the sample caused by the precipitation step (fold)
- *V*total = volume of the extract after mixing (µL)
- *V*sample = volume of culture supernatant taken (µL)
- *V*aliquot = volume of clear supernatant taken forward and dried (µL)
- *V*reconstitution = volume of 50% methanol the residue is taken up in (µL)
- *C*aliquot = concentration factor of the drying and reconstitution step (fold)
- *D*net = net dilution of the injected sample relative to the original
  culture medium (fold)

The drying step does not change the amount of bile acid in the aliquot, only
the volume it sits in, so taking 200 µL forward and reconstituting in 100 µL
concentrates it twofold. That twofold concentration partly undoes the fivefold
dilution of the precipitation. The net figure is what any later conversion
back to the culture medium depends on; the percentage of deconjugation itself
is a ratio within one sample and is not affected by it.

**Worked example.** For 100 µL of sample in 500 µL of extract, with 200 µL
dried and reconstituted in 100 µL: *D*extract = 500 / 100 = 5.00, *C*aliquot =
200 / 100 = 2.00, and *D*net = 5.00 / 2.00 = 2.50. The injected sample is
2.5-fold more dilute than the culture medium it came from.

**Percentage of GUDCA deconjugation**

% deconjugation = area UDCA / (area UDCA + area GUDCA) × 100

in which:

- area UDCA = integrated peak area at m/z 391.1, the deconjugated product
- area GUDCA = integrated peak area at m/z 448.1, the glycine conjugate

The denominator is the total of product plus remaining substrate, so the
result is the fraction of the bile acid pool that has been converted. A value
of 0% is no conversion and 100% is complete conversion. The calculation
assumes that both compounds give a similar response in the source; without
that assumption a calibration curve for each compound would be required.

**Worked example.** For a sample grown with GUDCA giving an area of 1.42 × 10⁵
for UDCA and 3.31 × 10⁵ for GUDCA: 1.42 × 10⁵ / (1.42 × 10⁵ + 3.31 × 10⁵) × 100
= 1.42 / 4.73 × 100 = 30.0% deconjugation.

**Percentage of TUDCA deconjugation**

% deconjugation = area UDCA / (area UDCA + area TUDCA) × 100

in which:

- area UDCA = integrated peak area at m/z 391.1, the deconjugated product
- area TUDCA = integrated peak area at m/z 498.1, the taurine conjugate

The equation is the same as for GUDCA, applied to the samples grown with
TUDCA. The two bile salts are run as separate samples, so for any one sample
only one of the two percentages is meaningful; the table holds both so that
either can be worked out from the same UDCA area.

**Worked example.** For a sample grown with TUDCA giving an area of 1.42 × 10⁵
for UDCA and 4.14 × 10⁵ for TUDCA: 1.42 × 10⁵ / (1.42 × 10⁵ + 4.14 × 10⁵) × 100
= 1.42 / 5.56 × 100 = 25.5% deconjugation.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The original method text gives one hour in the vacuum concentrator. One hour
is not enough to dry 200 µL of 80% methanol, and the samples are instead dried
to complete dryness however long that takes. The protocol has been written
with the corrected instruction.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

It has not been recorded whether the culture samples were handled in any way
between the end of the liquid bile salt assay and the first centrifugation
step of this method. This needs to be confirmed and added, because any
additional dilution changes the net dilution factor in Table 3.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

No calibration curve and no internal standard were used, so the results of
different runs can only be compared as percentages of deconjugation and not as
concentrations.

</ProtocolNote>
