<ExpandableText title="Dilution Series Protocol">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/dilution-series-protocol.pdf" workbookTab="Dilution series" />

<Callout title="Not to be uploaded on its own yet">

This protocol was written for the bile assay but was also used for other
purposes, and the team's upload notes say it still has to be merged with
another protocol that is being compiled. It is included here for completeness;
check that the merge has happened before this page is published.

</Callout>

#### Introduction

This is a small-scale pilot run using 2 of the 16 plasmid-containing DH5α
strains, intended to identify the dilution that yields a countable colony
density. Rather than plating all 16 strains at a single assumed dilution, each
of the 2 pilot strains is grown from cryostock, standardized to mid-log phase
and serially diluted 10-fold across seven steps (10⁻¹ to 10⁻⁷), and every
dilution is plated separately.

Once colonies are visible, the dilution or dilutions that give the target
colony density are used as the standard for the full run with 16 strains.

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Cryostocks of 2 plasmid-bearing DH5α strains | Kanamycin resistance marker |
| LB broth, sterile | 2 × 5 mL for the cultures and 14 × 90 µL for the dilutions |
| Kanamycin stock, 50 mg/mL | 5 µL per 5 mL culture, added fresh (working concentration 50 µg/mL) |
| LB agar plates with kanamycin | 14 (2 strains × 7 dilutions) |
| Ice | For transport of the cryostocks |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Culture tubes | 2 |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL, sterile | 14 (7 per strain) |
| Inoculation loops | 2 |
| Sterile glass plating beads | Several per plate |
| Cuvettes | For measurement of the OD₆₀₀ |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | 2–20 µL and 20–200 µL |
| Foam box | For the ice |
| Permanent marker | For labelling tubes and plates |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Freezer | −80 °C |
| Shaking incubator | 37 °C |
| Spectrophotometer | Measurement at 600 nm |
| RESHAPE | For incubation and imaging of the plates |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |
| Insulated gloves | For handling items stored at −80 °C |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kanamycin sulfate | 25389-94-0 | Danger. H360D: may damage the unborn child (Repr. 1B). | Wear gloves, lab coat and safety glasses. Weigh powder in a fume hood. Not to be handled during pregnancy. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.) |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

#### Procedure

**Preparation**

1. Take out 14 LB agar plates with kanamycin (7 per strain) and pre-warm them
   at room temperature or at 37 °C.

<ProtocolNote>

Pre-warming prevents condensation from pooling on the surface of the agar.

</ProtocolNote>

2. Label 7 sterile 1.5 mL tubes per strain as 10⁻¹ to 10⁻⁷.
3. Prepare 2 tubes with 5 mL of LB broth and 5 µL of kanamycin stock
   (50 mg/mL stock, working concentration 50 µg/mL; Table 2).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Kanamycin stock volume (C1 × V1 = C2 × V2)."
  workbookTab="Dilution series"
  rows={[
    { key: "c1", label: "Kanamycin stock concentration (C1)", value: 50, unit: "mg/mL", input: true },
    { key: "c2", label: "Working concentration (C2)", value: 50, unit: "µg/mL", input: true },
    { key: "v2", label: "LB broth volume (V2)", value: 5, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "v1", label: "Stock volume to add (V1)", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.c2 * v.v2) / v.c1 },
  ]}
/>

**Sampling the cryostocks**

4. Fill a foam box with ice and take 2 strains from the −80 °C freezer.

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The cryostocks should not be allowed to thaw completely.

</ProtocolNote>

5. Scrape a small amount of the frozen material with an inoculation loop and
   transfer it into one of the 2 prepared tubes with kanamycin; repeat for the
   second strain.
6. Return the cryostocks to the −80 °C freezer immediately.

**Growing the liquid cultures**

7. Leave the caps of the tubes slightly open to allow airflow and incubate at
   37 °C with shaking.
8. After approximately 1.5 hours, measure the OD₆₀₀, using LB broth as the
   blank. The target OD₆₀₀ is 0.6.

<ProtocolNote>

The most important point is that both cultures have the same OD₆₀₀.

</ProtocolNote>

9. If the cultures do not have the same OD₆₀₀, dilute them using
   OD1 × V1 = OD2 × V2 (Table 3).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Normalization of the OD₆₀₀ (OD1 × V1 = OD2 × V2)."
  workbookTab="Dilution series"
  rows={[
    { key: "od1", label: "Measured OD₆₀₀ of the culture (OD1)", value: 0.8, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "od2", label: "Target OD₆₀₀ (OD2)", value: 0.6, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "v2", label: "Final volume (V2)", value: 1000, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "v1", label: "Culture to add (V1)", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => (v.od2 * v.v2) / v.od1 },
    { key: "vBroth", label: "LB broth to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.v2 - v.v1 },
  ]}
/>

**10-fold dilution series**

10. For each strain, add 90 µL of sterile LB broth to each of the 7 labelled
    tubes.
11. Add 10 µL of the culture at an OD₆₀₀ of 0.6 to the 10⁻¹ tube and mix by
    pipetting up and down.
12. Transfer 10 µL from the 10⁻¹ tube to the 10⁻² tube, mix by pipetting, and
    repeat down the series to 10⁻⁷.

<ProtocolNote>

A fresh tip should be used for each transfer, to avoid carrying extra cells
forward.

</ProtocolNote>

**Plating**

13. Label the plates.
14. Pipette 100 µL from each of the 7 tubes onto its own labelled LB agar plate
    with kanamycin, for both strains (14 plates in total).
15. Spread each sample evenly with sterile beads.

**Incubation in the RESHAPE**

16. Load the plates into the RESHAPE and run for 24 hours.

<ProtocolNote>

At the time of writing, it had not been established whether illumination from
below or from above is required.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Volume of kanamycin stock**

C1 × V1 = C2 × V2 → V1 = (C2 × V2) / C1

in which:

- C1 = concentration of the stock solution
- V1 = volume of stock solution to add
- C2 = required final concentration
- V2 = final volume of the solution

C1 and C2 must be expressed in the same unit before the values are entered;
1 mg/mL equals 1000 µg/mL.

**Worked example.** For 50 µg/mL in 5 mL of LB broth, from a 50 mg/mL
(50,000 µg/mL) stock: V1 = (50 × 5) / 50,000 = 0.005 mL = 5.0 µL.

**Normalization of the optical density**

OD1 × V1 = OD2 × V2 → V1 = (OD2 × V2) / OD1

in which:

- OD1 = measured optical density of the culture
- V1 = volume of culture to use
- OD2 = required optical density
- V2 = final volume after dilution

The volume of medium to add is V2 − V1. The relation holds because the number
of cells is conserved on dilution.

**Worked example.** To obtain 1000 µL at an OD₆₀₀ of 0.6 from a culture at
OD₆₀₀ 0.8: V1 = (0.6 × 1000) / 0.8 = 750.0 µL of culture and 1000 − 750 =
250.0 µL of LB broth.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.). *Kanamycin sulfate from Streptomyces kanamyceticus* (Product No. K1377) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sigma/k1377

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title="ParB Colony PCR">

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/parb-colony-pcr-2.pdf" workbookTab="ParB PCR" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the colony PCR used to confirm that a colony obtained
after conjugation is a *Phocaeicola vulgatus* transconjugant, by amplifying
part of the ParB-like gene BVU_RS10845. This sequence is present in
*P. vulgatus* and absent from *E. coli*, so a product of the expected size
distinguishes a transconjugant from a surviving donor colony. Two primer sets
were designed and tested.

The procedure below uses Q5 High-Fidelity DNA Polymerase, which is what the
reaction mixture and the cycling conditions of the draft correspond to. The
team also used DreamTaq; the conditions for that polymerase are given in a
separate sub-section, because the two enzymes require different denaturation
temperatures and extension rates.

#### Attributions

The annealing temperatures were determined with the NEB Tm calculator for Q5
High-Fidelity DNA Polymerase (New England Biolabs, n.d.-a), which is available
online: NEB Tm Calculator.

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol to verify colonies obtained
from the conjugation of pNBU2 plasmids into *P. vulgatus* (see the protocol
"Conjugation of pNBU2 Plasmids into *P. vulgatus*").

#### Materials

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Colonies on selective agar plates | One colony per reaction |
| MQ water (Milli-Q), sterile | 50 µL per colony, plus the water of the reaction mixture |
| Q5 High-Fidelity DNA Polymerase and 5× Q5 Reaction Buffer | New England Biolabs, cat. no. M0491; 2 µL of buffer per 10 µL reaction |
| dNTP mix, 10 mM | 0.2 µL per 10 µL reaction |
| DreamTaq Green PCR Master Mix (2X) | Thermo Fisher Scientific, cat. no. K1081; for the DreamTaq variant |
| parB primers, set 1 and set 2 | Lyophilised; resuspended to 100 µM and diluted to a 10 µM working solution |
| Agarose, TAE or lithium acetate buffer, DNA stain and DNA ladder | For verification on gel (see the protocol "Agarose Gel Electrophoresis (Lithium Acetate)") |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| PCR tubes or strips | One per colony and one per reaction |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL, sterile | For the primer stocks and working dilutions |
| Micropipettes and sterile tips | 0.5–10 µL and 2–20 µL; small tips for picking colonies |
| Ice bucket | The reaction is assembled on ice |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Thermocycler | 1 |
| Horizontal electrophoresis apparatus, power supply and gel documentation system | For verification of the products |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

The polymerase master mixes, buffers, primers, dNTPs and DNA stains are handled
with gloves; the SDS of each product in use should be consulted. The hazards of
the gel step are covered in the protocol "Agarose Gel Electrophoresis (Lithium
Acetate)".

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q5 High-Fidelity DNA Polymerase and 5× Q5 Reaction Buffer | Mixtures | The SDS of each product in use should be consulted. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. | New England Biolabs (n.d.-b) Product page; SDS under "Documents" |
| DreamTaq Green PCR Master Mix (2X) | Mixture | The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Wear gloves and safety glasses. | Thermo Fisher Scientific (2016) User guide; SDS on the product page |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

#### Procedure

**Preparing the primers**

1. Resuspend each lyophilised primer in MQ water to a stock concentration of
   100 µM, and prepare a 10 µM working dilution (Table 2).

<ProtocolNote>

The volume of MQ water in µL is the amount stated on the tube in nmol
multiplied by ten. For the primers used: 248 µL for ParB forward set 1
(24.8 nmol), 274 µL for ParB reverse set 1 (27.4 nmol), 248 µL for ParB forward
set 2 (24.8 nmol) and 208 µL for ParB reverse set 2 (20.8 nmol).

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={2}
  title="Primer resuspension and working dilution."
  workbookTab="ParB PCR"
  rows={[
    { key: "nmol", label: "Amount of primer stated on the tube", value: 24.8, unit: "nmol", input: true },
    { key: "cStock", label: "Target stock concentration", value: 100, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "cWork", label: "Target working concentration", value: 10, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "vWork", label: "Working dilution volume", value: 100, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vResuspend", label: "MQ water for resuspension", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => (v.nmol / v.cStock) * 1000 },
    { key: "vStockAdd", label: "Stock to add to the working dilution", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vWork * (v.cWork / v.cStock) },
    { key: "vWaterWork", label: "MQ water for the working dilution", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vWork - v.vStockAdd },
  ]}
/>

2. Note the annealing temperature of the primer set in use (Table 3).

**Table 3.** Primer sets amplifying part of the ParB-like gene BVU_RS10845,
which is present in *P. vulgatus* and absent from *E. coli*, with the annealing
temperatures calculated for Q5 High-Fidelity DNA Polymerase (New England
Biolabs, n.d.-a).

| Primer | Sequence (5′ to 3′) | Length | GC | Tm | Annealing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Set 1 forward | TATCCGTCAACCCTAACCAGC | 21 nt | 52% | 67 °C | 68 °C |
| Set 1 reverse | TTCTTCCCACCGCTGTCCAC | 20 nt | 60% | 70 °C | 68 °C |
| Set 2 forward | GCTTGACGCACTGATCTCG | 19 nt | 58% | 66 °C | 66 °C |
| Set 2 reverse | CCACTTTACGGACAGAATAGCC | 22 nt | 50% | 65 °C | 66 °C |

**Preparing the template**

3. Pick a single colony with a small pipette tip and resuspend it in 50 µL of
   sterile MQ water in a PCR tube.

**Setting up the reaction with Q5 polymerase**

4. Assemble the reaction components on ice according to Table 4, preparing a
   master mix without template for several reactions.

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={4}
  title="Q5 master mix for several reactions."
  workbookTab="ParB PCR"
  rows={[
    { key: "n", label: "Number of reactions", value: 20, unit: "reactions", input: true },
    { key: "excess", label: "Excess for pipetting loss", value: 10, unit: "%", input: true },
    { key: "vR", label: "Reaction volume", value: 10, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "bufferFactor", label: "Q5 reaction buffer concentration", value: 5, unit: "×", input: true },
    { key: "cdNTPStock", label: "dNTP stock concentration", value: 10, unit: "mM", input: true },
    { key: "cdNTPFinal", label: "dNTP final concentration", value: 0.2, unit: "mM", input: true },
    { key: "cPrimerStock", label: "Primer stock concentration", value: 10, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "cPrimerFinal", label: "Primer final concentration", value: 0.5, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "vTemplate", label: "Template per reaction", value: 1, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vPolymerase", label: "Q5 polymerase per reaction", value: 0.1, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vBuffer", label: "Q5 reaction buffer per reaction", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vR / v.bufferFactor },
    { key: "vdNTP", label: "dNTPs per reaction", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vR * (v.cdNTPFinal / v.cdNTPStock) },
    { key: "vPrimer", label: "Each primer per reaction", unit: "µL", compute: (v) => v.vR * (v.cPrimerFinal / v.cPrimerStock) },
    { key: "vWater", label: "MQ water per reaction", unit: "µL", decimals: 2, compute: (v) => v.vR - v.vBuffer - v.vdNTP - 2 * v.vPrimer - v.vTemplate - v.vPolymerase },
    { key: "vBufferTotal", label: "Q5 reaction buffer, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vBuffer * v.n * (1 + v.excess / 100) },
    { key: "vdNTPTotal", label: "dNTPs, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vdNTP * v.n * (1 + v.excess / 100) },
    { key: "vPrimerTotal", label: "Each primer, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vPrimer * v.n * (1 + v.excess / 100) },
    { key: "vPolymeraseTotal", label: "Q5 polymerase, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vPolymerase * v.n * (1 + v.excess / 100) },
    { key: "vWaterTotal", label: "MQ water, total", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vWater * v.n * (1 + v.excess / 100) },
    { key: "vPerTube", label: "Master mix per tube (before template)", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vR - v.vTemplate },
  ]}
/>

5. Add 1 µL of the colony suspension as template to each tube.
6. Run the program given in Table 5, using the annealing temperature of the
   primer set in use and the extension time from Table 6.

**Table 5.** Cycling program for Q5 High-Fidelity DNA Polymerase.

| Step | Temperature (°C) | Time | Cycles |
|---|---|---|---|
| Initial denaturation | 98 | 30 s | 1 |
| Denaturation | 98 | 10 s | 30 |
| Annealing | 66–68 | 30 s | 30 |
| Extension | 68 | 20–30 s per kb | 30 |
| Final extension | 68 | 10 min | 1 |
| Cooling | 16 | Hold | – |

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={6}
  title="Extension time."
  workbookTab="ParB PCR"
  rows={[
    { key: "length", label: "Expected amplicon length", value: 0.6, unit: "kb", input: true },
    { key: "rate", label: "Extension rate (Q5: 0.33–0.5 min/kb; DreamTaq: 1 min/kb)", value: 0.5, unit: "min/kb", input: true },
    { key: "tMin", label: "Extension time", unit: "min", compute: (v) => v.length * v.rate },
    { key: "tSec", label: "Extension time", unit: "s", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.tMin * 60 },
  ]}
/>

**Setting up the reaction with DreamTaq polymerase**

7. For the DreamTaq variant, assemble the reaction with DreamTaq Green PCR
   Master Mix (2X) according to Table 7, on ice.

**Table 7.** Composition of a DreamTaq reaction, scaled from the manufacturer's
50 µL reaction (Thermo Fisher Scientific, 2016).

| Reagent | Stock concentration | Volume per 25 µL reaction (µL) | Final concentration |
|---|---|---|---|
| DreamTaq Green PCR Master Mix | 2× | 12.5 | 1× |
| Forward primer | 10 µM | 0.5 | 0.2 µM |
| Reverse primer | 10 µM | 0.5 | 0.2 µM |
| Template (colony suspension) | – | 2.5 | – |
| Nuclease-free water | – | To 25 µL (9.0 µL) | – |

8. Run the DreamTaq program given in Table 8.

**Table 8.** Cycling program for DreamTaq Green PCR Master Mix (Thermo Fisher
Scientific, 2016).

| Step | Temperature (°C) | Time | Cycles |
|---|---|---|---|
| Initial denaturation | 95 | 1–3 min | 1 |
| Denaturation | 95 | 30 s | 25–40 |
| Annealing | Tm − 5 | 30 s | 25–40 |
| Extension | 72 | 1 min per kb | 25–40 |
| Final extension | 72 | 5–15 min | 1 |
| Cooling | 16 | Hold | – |

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

The two polymerases are not interchangeable within one program. DreamTaq
denatures at 95 °C and extends at 72 °C at 1 min per kb, whereas Q5 denatures
at 98 °C and extends at 68 °C at 20 to 30 seconds per kb (Thermo Fisher
Scientific, 2016; New England Biolabs, n.d.-b). The annealing temperatures in
Table 3 were calculated for Q5 and do not apply to DreamTaq, for which the
manufacturer recommends the primer melting temperature minus 5 °C.

</ProtocolNote>

**Verification on gel**

9. Analyse the products by agarose gel electrophoresis and check that the
   fragment sizes are correct (see the protocol "Agarose Gel Electrophoresis
   (Lithium Acetate)").

<ProtocolNote>

Products amplified with the DreamTaq Green master mix can be loaded directly on
the gel, because the master mix already contains loading dye and a density
reagent (Thermo Fisher Scientific, 2016). Products amplified with Q5 require
loading dye to be added.

</ProtocolNote>

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The draft of this protocol was titled "ParB PCR Protocol using DreamTaq", but
the reaction mixture, the cycling conditions and the Tm calculations in it are
those of Q5 High-Fidelity DNA Polymerase. Both enzymes were used by the team.
Which run used which enzyme still needs to be recorded here.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="October 2026">

The draft contained a table of melting temperatures (54.2 to 57.8 °C) and
product sizes (263 to 616 bp) that could not be matched to the two primer sets
above. The same table appears in the conjugation draft, where six primer
combinations were tested at an annealing temperature of 55 °C. It has been left
out until its origin is clear.

</ProtocolNote>

<Callout title="Still to be added">

The catalogue and lot numbers of the polymerases and the primer order numbers
still need to be added.

</Callout>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Resuspension of lyophilised primers**

*V* = (*n* / *c*) × 1000

in which:

- *V* = volume of MQ water for resuspension (µL)
- *n* = amount of primer stated on the tube (nmol)
- *c* = required stock concentration (µM)

A concentration of 100 µM equals 100 nmol/mL, so the amount in nmol multiplied
by 10 gives the volume in µL directly. The working dilution follows
C1 × V1 = C2 × V2.

**Worked example.** For a tube containing 24.8 nmol at 100 µM: *V* =
(24.8 / 100) × 1000 = 248 µL. For 100 µL of a 10 µM working dilution:
100 × (10 / 100) = 10.0 µL of stock and 90.0 µL of MQ water.

**Q5 master mix for several reactions**

*V*buffer = *V*r / *F* · *V*dNTP = *V*r × (*c*f / *c*s) · *V*primer = *V*r × (*p*f / *p*s) · *V*total = *V*component × *n* × (1 + *e* / 100)

in which:

- *V*r = reaction volume (µL)
- *F* = concentration factor of the reaction buffer (×)
- *c*f, *c*s = final and stock dNTP concentration (mM)
- *p*f, *p*s = final and stock primer concentration (µM)
- *n* = number of reactions
- *e* = excess for pipetting loss (%)

The water volume per reaction is the reaction volume minus every other
component. The master mix holds everything except the template, so each tube
receives the reaction volume minus the template volume.

**Worked example.** For a 10 µL reaction: 10 / 5 = 2.00 µL of 5× buffer,
10 × (0.2 / 10) = 0.20 µL of dNTPs and 10 × (0.5 / 10) = 0.50 µL of each
primer, leaving 10 − 2 − 0.2 − 1.0 − 1.0 − 0.1 = 5.70 µL of MQ water. For 20
reactions with 10% excess the mix needs 2.00 × 20 × 1.1 = 44.0 µL of buffer,
and each tube receives 10 − 1 = 9.0 µL.

**Extension time**

*t* = *L* × *r*

in which:

- *t* = extension time (min)
- *L* = expected length of the amplicon (kb)
- *r* = extension rate of the polymerase (min/kb)

Q5 extends at roughly 20 to 30 seconds per kb (0.33 to 0.5 min/kb); DreamTaq
extends at 1 min/kb.

**Worked example.** For a 0.6 kb amplicon with Q5 at 0.5 min/kb: *t* =
0.6 × 0.5 = 0.30 min = 18 s. The same amplicon with DreamTaq needs 0.6 × 1 =
0.6 min, rounded up to 1 min.

</ProtocolCalculations>

#### References

- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-a). *NEB Tm calculator* (Version 1.17.0). Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://tmcalculator.neb.com/
- New England Biolabs. (n.d.-b). *Q5 high-fidelity DNA polymerase* (NEB #M0491) [Product information]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.neb.com/en/products/m0491-q5-high-fidelity-dna-polymerase
- Thermo Fisher Scientific. (2016). *DreamTaq Green PCR Master Mix (2X)* (Publication No. MAN0012704, Rev. B.00). https://documents.thermofisher.com/TFS-Assets/LSG/manuals/MAN0012704_DreamTaq_Green_PCR_MasterMix_K1081_UG.pdf

</ExpandableText>

<ExpandableText title={<>Conjugation of pNBU2 Plasmids into <em>P. vulgatus</em></>}>

<ProtocolDownloads pdf="https://static.igem.wiki/teams/6299/wiki/wet-lab/protocols/conjugation-of-pnbu2-plasmids-into-p-vulgatus-2.pdf" workbookTab="Conjugation" />

#### Introduction

This protocol describes the conjugative transfer of pNBU2 plasmids from an
*Escherichia coli* S17-1 λ pir donor strain (Simon et al., 1983) into
*Phocaeicola vulgatus*.

The CRISPR construct of the project was not yet developed when the conjugation
experiments started, so a different plasmid was needed to assess the
conjugation efficiency. pNBU2 was selected for this (Wang et al., 2000). The
plasmid used carries the reporter gene KOFP-7 downstream of a
rhamnose-inducible promoter, a resistance cassette against erythromycin (ermG),
and the IntN2 integrase that integrates the plasmid into the *P. vulgatus*
genome.

Selection works in two directions. Erythromycin selects for cells carrying the
plasmid, through ermG, while gentamicin counterselects against the *E. coli*
donor (El Khoury et al., 2023). pNBU2 is a suicide plasmid in *Bacteroides* and
*Phocaeicola*, so it cannot replicate on its own in the recipient (Wang et al.,
2000); a recipient colony growing on gentamicin and erythromycin must therefore
carry the plasmid integrated in its genome. For that reason the integration
itself is not verified by PCR.

The protocol was originally written for *Bacteroides thetaiotaomicron* and can
be adapted for other *Bacteroides* and *Phocaeicola* species, donor strains and
plasmids, although additional optimization may be required. The original
protocol uses Wilkins-Chalgren (WC) or supplemented brain heart infusion (BHIS)
broth, which can be used interchangeably; the team used mGAM broth and plates
throughout.

The team ran this protocol five times between August and September 2026. The
procedure below is the version of the most recent run; the differences between
runs, and the experience gained from them, are given as dated notes and in the
section "Notes from the bench".

<Callout title="Figure 1 — not yet uploaded">

A map of the pNBU2 plasmid, showing KOFP-7 under the rhamnose-inducible
promoter, the ermG cassette and the IntN2 integrase, belongs here. The image
was not supplied with the protocol and has to be uploaded through the iGEM
uploads tool before it can be shown.

</Callout>

#### Attributions

This protocol is a rewritten version of a protocol provided by Mathew Herdman,
KU Leuven iGEM 2026 Advisor, and adapted by the iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team.

The original protocol was adapted from the work of Leonor García-Bayona and
Laurie E. Comstock on the pLGB13 vector suite for *Bacteroides* and
*Parabacteroides* (García-Bayona & Comstock, 2019), which is available online:
Streamlined Genetic Manipulation of Diverse *Bacteroides* and *Parabacteroides*
Isolates from the Human Gut Microbiota (mBio, 2019).

The iGEM KU Leuven 2026 team used this protocol to transfer pNBU2 plasmids
carrying the promoter-reporter constructs of the promoter library into
*P. vulgatus* DSM1447, in order to test different combinations of promoters and
reporter genes in the chassis organism.

#### Materials

**Strains**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| *E. coli* S17-1 λ pir donor strain carrying the pNBU2 plasmid | One strain per mating (Simon et al., 1983) |
| *P. vulgatus* DSM1447 recipient strain | Type strain, from human faeces; one culture, divided over the matings (Leibniz Institute DSMZ, n.d.-a) |
| *P. vulgatus* RCC1806 | Clinical isolate; obtained from the Raes lab of Bioinformatics and (Eco-)Systems Biology by Ruben Vazquez-Uribe |
| *P. vulgatus* DSM 108222, DSM 108234 and DSM 108228 | Clinical isolates, all from human faeces (Leibniz Institute DSMZ, n.d.-b; Leibniz Institute DSMZ, n.d.-d; Leibniz Institute DSMZ, n.d.-c) |
| *P. vulgatus* DSM 3289 | Clinical isolate, from a retroperitoneal abscess (Leibniz Institute DSMZ, n.d.-e) |

**Reagents**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lysogeny broth (LB), sterile | 2 mL per donor overnight culture and 25 mL per mating |
| mGAM broth, sterile | 3 mL for the recipient overnight culture and 2.5 mL per mating |
| LB agar plates with ampicillin (100 µg/mL) | One per donor strain |
| mGAM agar plates without antibiotic | One per four matings for the mating spots, plus plates for the recipient |
| mGAM agar plates with gentamicin (200 µg/mL) and erythromycin (25 µg/mL) | One per dilution per mating, plus plates for the negative controls |
| Ampicillin stock, 100 mg/mL | Retains the pNBU2 plasmid in the donor strain; 100 µg/mL working concentration |
| Gentamicin stock, 200 mg/mL | Selects the recipient; 200 µg/mL working concentration |
| Erythromycin stock, 10 mg/mL | Selects merodiploid recipients; 25 µg/mL working concentration |
| Phosphate-buffered saline (PBS), 10× | 57 mL per 570 mL of 1× PBS |
| Distilled water | For dilution of the PBS |
| MQ water (Milli-Q), sterile | For resuspension of the primers and for the colony PCR |
| OneTaq 2X Master Mix with Standard Buffer | New England Biolabs, cat. no. M0482; 12.5 µL per 25 µL reaction |
| ParB forward and reverse primers | Lyophilised; resuspended to 100 µM and diluted to a 10 µM working solution |
| Primers for the inserted region | 10 µM working solution |

**Consumables and tools**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Culture tubes and conical tubes, 50 mL | For the overnight cultures, subcultures and washes |
| Microcentrifuge tubes, 1.5 mL, sterile | For the dilution series and the primer dilutions |
| PCR tubes | One per colony PCR reaction |
| Sterile pipette tips, inoculation loops and spreading beads | As required |
| Micropipettes | 2–20 µL, 20–200 µL and 100–1000 µL |
| Cuvettes | For measurement of the OD₆₀₀ |
| Permanent marker | For labelling the plates and the master plate grid |

**Instruments**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Anaerobic chamber | 37 °C, for the recipient strain and all steps from day three onwards |
| Shaking incubator | 37 °C, 180–200 rpm, for the aerobic donor cultures |
| Centrifuge for 50 mL tubes | 3500 rpm, 15 minutes |
| Spectrophotometer | Measurement at 600 nm |
| Thermocycler | For the colony PCR |
| Horizontal electrophoresis apparatus, power supply and gel documentation system | For verification of the colony PCR |
| Laminar airflow cabinet | For the mating spots |

**Personal protective equipment**

| Item | Specification and quantity |
|---|---|
| Lab coat | Worn closed at all times |
| Nitrile gloves | Changed after contact with reagents or cultures |
| Safety glasses | Required when handling hot liquids and irritant powders |

#### Safety and safety data sheets

Hazard classifications were taken from the supplier safety data sheets (SDS)
and product pages cited in the table below. The SDS of the exact product and
lot in use should be consulted before starting, because classifications can
differ between suppliers and product grades.

The donor and recipient strains and the transconjugants are genetically
modified micro-organisms and are handled as contained use. The work takes place
in an L2 laboratory, so laboratory coats are worn closed, gloves are worn at
all times, and no door is opened while wearing gloves.

**Table 1.** Hazard classification and safety data sheets (SDS) of the reagents used.

| Substance | CAS no. | Classification (GHS/CLP) | Precautions | SDS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ampicillin sodium salt | 69-52-3 | Danger. H317: may cause an allergic skin reaction. H334: may cause allergy or asthma symptoms if inhaled. | Avoid dust formation and inhalation. Not to be handled by persons with a penicillin allergy. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-a) |
| Gentamicin sulfate | 1405-41-0 | Danger. Aminoglycoside antibiotic; the SDS of the product in use should be consulted for the hazard statements. | Avoid dust formation and inhalation. Wear gloves and safety glasses. | Supplier SDS |
| Erythromycin | 114-07-8 | The SDS of the product in use should be consulted. | Avoid dust formation. Stock solutions are prepared in ethanol in a fume hood. | Supplier SDS |
| Ethanol, 70% (v/v) | 64-17-5 | Danger. H225: highly flammable liquid and vapour. H319: causes serious eye irritation. | Keep away from open flames and hot surfaces. | Sigma-Aldrich (n.d.-b) SDS of absolute ethanol |
| Bacterial strains and transformants | n/a | Biological agents. Transformants are genetically modified micro-organisms (contained use). | Work under the containment level approved for the laboratory. Inactivate all cultures, plates and contaminated consumables by autoclaving. | No SDS: see the institutional biosafety guidelines |

**Waste disposal.** All cultures, plates and contaminated consumables are
inactivated by autoclaving before disposal. The waste and disposal instructions
of the laboratory in use apply.

<Callout title="Still to be added">

The catalogue numbers of the antibiotics, the primer sequences and the lot
numbers of the reagents still need to be added, together with the full
reference for El Khoury et al. (2023), cited for the gentamicin
counterselection.

</Callout>

#### Procedure

**Preparing the antibiotic solutions**

1. Calculate the volume of antibiotic stock for each culture and each plate
   (Table 2). The working concentrations are 100 µg/mL ampicillin for the
   donor, 200 µg/mL gentamicin for the recipient and 25 µg/mL erythromycin for
   the selection plates.

<ProtocolCalcGrid
  number={2}
  title="Antibiotic volumes per culture or plate (C1 × V1 = C2 × V2)."
  workbookTab="Conjugation"
  columns={[
    { key: "name", label: "Antibiotic", type: "text" },
    { key: "stock", label: "Stock (µg/mL)", type: "input" },
    { key: "working", label: "Working (µg/mL)", type: "input" },
    { key: "volume", label: "Volume of medium (mL)", type: "input" },
    { key: "add", label: "Stock to add (µL)", type: "computed", decimals: 1, compute: (r) => ((r.working * r.volume) / r.stock) * 1000 },
  ]}
  rows={[
    { name: "Ampicillin (donor cultures)", stock: 100000, working: 100, volume: 25 },
    { name: "Gentamicin (recipient cultures)", stock: 200000, working: 200, volume: 2.5 },
    { name: "Gentamicin (plates)", stock: 200000, working: 200, volume: 12 },
    { name: "Erythromycin (plates)", stock: 10000, working: 25, volume: 12 },
  ]}
/>

**Day zero: single colonies**

2. Streak each *E. coli* S17 donor strain on an LB agar plate with ampicillin
   and grow overnight at 37 °C.
3. Streak the *P. vulgatus* DSM1447 recipient strain on an mGAM agar plate
   without antibiotic and grow for 24 to 48 hours at 37 °C in the anaerobic
   chamber.

<ProtocolNote>

The original protocol grows the recipient on plates with gentamicin; the team
used mGAM plates without antibiotic.

</ProtocolNote>

**Day one: overnight cultures**

4. Inoculate 2 mL of LB with 2 µL of ampicillin stock with a single colony of
   each donor strain.

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

The first runs used 3 mL of LB with 3 µL of ampicillin stock. From 15 September
2026 onwards, 2 mL was used.

</ProtocolNote>

5. Inoculate 3 mL of mGAM without antibiotic with a single colony of the
   recipient strain.
6. Grow the donor aerobically and the recipient anaerobically overnight at
   37 °C with thorough shaking (180–200 rpm).

**Day two: subculturing and mating**

7. Subculture the donor: inoculate 25 mL of fresh LB with ampicillin with
   250 µL of the overnight donor culture (1/100 dilution), once per mating
   (Table 3).

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

The first runs used a 1/1000 dilution (25 µL). Because the *E. coli* strains
grew too slowly, a lower dilution of 1/100 was used from 10 September 2026
onwards.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={3}
  title="Subculture volumes."
  workbookTab="Conjugation"
  rows={[
    { key: "vDonor", label: "Donor subculture volume (LB)", value: 25, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "dDonor", label: "Donor dilution of the overnight culture (1 in …)", value: 100, unit: "fold", input: true },
    { key: "vRecipient", label: "Recipient subculture volume (mGAM)", value: 2.5, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "dRecipient", label: "Recipient dilution of the overnight culture (1 in …)", value: 50, unit: "fold", input: true },
    { key: "nMatings", label: "Number of matings", value: 8, unit: "matings", input: true },
    { key: "vDonorAdd", label: "Overnight donor culture to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => (v.vDonor / v.dDonor) * 1000 },
    { key: "vRecipientAdd", label: "Overnight recipient culture to add", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => (v.vRecipient / v.dRecipient) * 1000 },
    { key: "vRecipientAll", label: "Recipient medium for all matings (prepared in one tube)", unit: "mL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vRecipient * v.nMatings },
    { key: "vRecipientAllAdd", label: "Overnight recipient culture for that tube", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => (v.vRecipientAll / v.dRecipient) * 1000 },
  ]}
/>

8. Subculture the recipient: inoculate fresh mGAM with gentamicin with the
   overnight recipient culture at a 1/50 dilution, 2.5 mL per mating.

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

It is recommended to prepare the recipient subculture as a single volume for
all matings in one conical tube and to divide it over the separate tubes once
the required optical density is reached. Volumes of 15 mL and 30 mL were
prepared in this way.

</ProtocolNote>

9. Grow both strains to early log phase: OD₆₀₀ of approximately 0.2 for the
   donor and 0.2 to 0.5 for the recipient.

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

The original protocol states roughly 2 hours for the donor and 4 to 6 hours for
the recipient. In the run of 21 September 2026 the donor took approximately
4 hours and the recipient approximately 3 hours.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

While waiting for the cultures to grow, the 1× PBS can be prepared from the 10×
stock (Table 4).

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={4}
  title="Diluting 10× PBS to 1×."
  workbookTab="Conjugation"
  rows={[
    { key: "vTotal", label: "Total volume of 1× PBS required", value: 570, unit: "mL", input: true },
    { key: "factor", label: "Concentration factor of the stock", value: 10, unit: "×", input: true },
    { key: "vStock", label: "10× PBS to use", unit: "mL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vTotal / v.factor },
    { key: "vWater", label: "Distilled water to add", unit: "mL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vTotal - v.vStock },
  ]}
/>

10. Pellet both cultures by centrifugation at 3500 rpm for 15 minutes and wash
    three times with 10 mL of PBS.

<ProtocolNote>

From this point onwards the recipient can be handled aerobically.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

The recipient gave a large pellet and the donor a much smaller one. A small
black spot, indicating contamination, was visible in the donor pellet in the
first run.

</ProtocolNote>

11. Resuspend both cultures in 5 mL of PBS and combine the donor and recipient
    suspensions for each mating.
12. Pellet the mixed culture by centrifugation at 3500 rpm for 15 minutes and
    resuspend it in 100 µL of mGAM without antibiotic.
13. Spot the 100 µL of mixed culture onto the centre of an mGAM agar plate
    without antibiotic. Up to four matings can be spotted on one plate, with a
    clear distance between the spots.
14. Allow the spot to dry thoroughly, so that the culture does not run.

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

Drying takes a considerable amount of time; the laminar airflow cabinet can be
cleaned in the meantime.

</ProtocolNote>

15. Incubate the plate overnight at 37 °C with the agar side down, until a lawn
    has formed.

<ProtocolNote>

The dense mixed lawn is thought to provide a microaerobic environment that
protects the recipient. Prolonged aerobic incubation nevertheless kills the
recipient and lowers the yield.

</ProtocolNote>

**Day three: selection**

<ProtocolNote kind="critical">

All steps from here onwards are performed anaerobically.

</ProtocolNote>

16. Scrape the lawn from the plate and resuspend it in 1 to 5 mL of mGAM
    without antibiotic.
17. Pellet the suspension by centrifugation and resuspend it in 100 µL of mGAM.
18. Prepare a 10-fold dilution series of the suspension: 10⁻¹, 10⁻², 10⁻³ and
    further. For a first test of the conjugation efficiency, plate 10⁻¹ to
    10⁻⁸.
19. Plate 50 µL of each dilution on mGAM plates with gentamicin and
    erythromycin to select for merodiploid recipients. The dilutions can be
    spotted or spread; spreading makes colony picking easier.
20. Plate the unconjugated recipient and the unconjugated donor on the same
    selective medium as negative controls.

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

The negative controls were not plated in the first run, because it was not
clear whether overnight or diluted cultures should be used. Diluted early log
phase cultures, treated in the same way as the mating, are the appropriate
control.

</ProtocolNote>

21. Grow the plates for 24 to 48 hours and check for colonies.

**Colony counting and conjugation efficiency**

22. Count the colonies manually on the plates of the dilutions that give a
    countable density.

<ProtocolNote>

A plate with roughly 30 to 300 colonies is countable; below that the count is
dominated by chance, and above it the colonies merge. The dilution that gives a
countable density is established once with the protocol "Dilution Series
Protocol" and then used as the standard.

</ProtocolNote>

23. Calculate the number of transconjugants per millilitre and the conjugation
    efficiency from the counts (Table 5).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={5}
  title="Colony counting and conjugation efficiency."
  workbookTab="Conjugation"
  rows={[
    { key: "colonies", label: "Colonies counted on the plate", value: 48, unit: "CFU", input: true },
    { key: "dilution", label: "Dilution of the plated suspension (10⁻ˣ; enter x)", value: 4, unit: "–", input: true },
    { key: "vPlated", label: "Volume plated", value: 50, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vMating", label: "Volume of the resuspended mating", value: 100, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "recipients", label: "Recipient cells in the mating", value: 100000000, unit: "CFU", input: true },
    { key: "perMl", label: "Transconjugants per mL of the resuspended mating", unit: "CFU/mL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => (v.colonies / (v.vPlated / 1000)) * Math.pow(10, v.dilution) },
    { key: "total", label: "Transconjugants in the whole mating", unit: "CFU", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => v.perMl * (v.vMating / 1000) },
    { key: "efficiency", label: "Conjugation efficiency per recipient", unit: "–", decimals: 6, compute: (v) => v.total / v.recipients },
  ]}
/>

**Verification: confirming the identity of the recipient**

Growth on medium with gentamicin and erythromycin is already a strong
indication that a colony is a conjugated *P. vulgatus* and not surviving
*E. coli*, but the identity of the recipient is confirmed by PCR as well. Which
target is used depends on the strain.

24. For *P. vulgatus* DSM1447, amplify part of the ParB-like gene BVU_RS10845,
    which is present in *P. vulgatus* and absent from *E. coli*, and run the
    product on a gel (see the protocol "ParB Colony PCR" for the primer sets
    and the cycling conditions).
25. For the clinical isolates, amplify the 16S rDNA gene, submit the product
    for sequencing and identify the strain by comparing the sequence against
    the nucleotide database (National Center for Biotechnology Information,
    n.d.); see the protocols "PCR Clean-Up" and "Sanger Sequencing Sample
    Preparation".

<ProtocolNote>

The integration of pNBU2 is not verified by PCR. Because pNBU2 is a suicide
plasmid in these organisms, it cannot be maintained without integrating, so a
recipient colony that grows under erythromycin selection must already carry it
in the genome (Wang et al., 2000).

</ProtocolNote>

**Verification: colony PCR of the ParB region**

26. Resuspend the lyophilised primers to a stock concentration of 100 µM and
    prepare a 10 µM working dilution (Table 6).

<ProtocolCalculator
  number={6}
  title="Primer resuspension and working dilution."
  workbookTab="Conjugation"
  rows={[
    { key: "nmol", label: "Amount of primer stated on the tube", value: 38.2, unit: "nmol", input: true },
    { key: "cStock", label: "Target stock concentration", value: 100, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "cWork", label: "Target working concentration", value: 10, unit: "µM", input: true },
    { key: "vWork", label: "Working dilution volume", value: 100, unit: "µL", input: true },
    { key: "vResuspend", label: "MQ water for resuspension", unit: "µL", decimals: 0, compute: (v) => (v.nmol / v.cStock) * 1000 },
    { key: "vStockAdd", label: "Stock to add to the working dilution", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vWork * (v.cWork / v.cStock) },
    { key: "vWaterWork", label: "MQ water for the working dilution", unit: "µL", decimals: 1, compute: (v) => v.vWork - v.vStockAdd },
  ]}
/>

27. Pick a single colony with a small pipette tip and dissolve it in 50 µL of
    sterile MQ water in a PCR tube.
28. Set up the colony PCR on ice with the OneTaq master mix, using the ParB
    primer pair and a primer pair for the inserted region. Prepare the master
    mix for the number of reactions required, with an excess for pipetting loss
    (see the protocol "Colony PCR").

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

Six ParB primer combinations (three forward and two reverse primers) were
tested in triplicate at an annealing temperature of 55 °C, with expected
product sizes between 263 and 616 bp. The combination of ParB forward 1 and
ParB reverse 1 was used from 21 September 2026 onwards.

</ProtocolNote>

29. Analyse the products by agarose gel electrophoresis (see the protocol
    "Agarose Gel Electrophoresis (Lithium Acetate)") and check that the
    fragment sizes are correct.

**Final day: liquid culture method**

30. Select at least four colonies per mating.
31. Pick a single colony with a sterile pipette tip into a PCR reaction with
    primers for the inserted region and for ParB.
32. Transfer the same tip into a tube containing 5 mL of mGAM with gentamicin
    and erythromycin, and incubate overnight at 37 °C with thorough shaking (at
    least 200 rpm).
33. Retain the cultures that give a positive PCR result for sequencing and
    downstream work.

**Final day: spot-plate method (alternative)**

34. Pick at least four colonies per mating and spot each onto its assigned
    position on a fresh selective master plate, marked with a numbered grid.
35. Using the same tip, set up two colony PCR reactions per colony: one with
    primers for the inserted region, and one with primers for a
    recipient-specific region such as ParB.

<ProtocolNote>

The second reaction confirms that the colony is a transconjugant of the
recipient and not a donor colony that survived the selection. This check is
rarely necessary, but it is a useful safeguard.

</ProtocolNote>

36. Incubate the master plate overnight at 37 °C while the PCR products are
    analysed on a gel.
37. Inoculate 5 mL of mGAM with gentamicin and erythromycin from the grown
    spots of the colonies that gave the correct band in both reactions, and
    incubate overnight at 37 °C with thorough shaking.

<ProtocolNote>

The spot-plate method avoids the preparation of liquid cultures for colonies
that turn out to be negative, which saves a considerable volume of selective
medium.

</ProtocolNote>

38. Discard the spots that failed either check and retain the confirmed
    cultures for sequencing and downstream work.

**Confirming the plasmid by reporter expression**

39. Induce KOFP-7 expression with rhamnose in the confirmed transconjugants
    (see the protocol "Rhamnose Induction and Medium Optimization").
40. Confirm the presence of the plasmid by epifluorescence on an inverted
    microscope (see the protocol "Fluorescence Microscopy").

<ProtocolNote>

KOFP-7 is a flavin-binding fluorescent protein and does not need oxygen to
fluoresce, so the transconjugants can be imaged directly without an aerobic
maturation step.

</ProtocolNote>

#### Notes from the bench

This section collects the experience of the team with this protocol: what was
changed between runs, what went wrong and what is worth knowing before
starting. Members of the team are encouraged to add their own entries, each
starting with the month and year.

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

The first run (3 August 2026) used one recipient strain (*P. vulgatus*
DSM1447) and one donor strain carrying pNBU2, to test the conjugation itself
with a flavin-binding fluorescent protein. Later runs used the donor strains
carrying the pNBU2 constructs assembled by Gibson assembly: ten strains on
10 September 2026, four strains (12, 14, 17 and 19) on 15 September 2026 and
eight strains (3.2, 5, 6.1, 9.1, 14, 15.1, 16.1 and 18) on 21 September 2026.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

The donor strains grew too slowly at the 1/1000 dilution given in the original
protocol. A 1/100 dilution was used from 10 September 2026 onwards and gave an
OD₆₀₀ of approximately 0.2 within the working day.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

Preparing the recipient subculture as one volume and dividing it over the
mating tubes once the required optical density is reached is more practical
than growing a separate tube per mating.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

The amount of plates needed grows quickly with the number of matings. The run
of 21 September 2026 used 5 mGAM plates and 18 selective plates for eight
matings.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

A black spot, indicating contamination, was seen in the donor pellet of the
first run. It is worth inspecting both pellets after the washes.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="August 2026">

Adding kanamycin to the selection was considered but not tested.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolNote date="September 2026">

The spot-plate method on the final day was introduced on 7 September 2026 and
avoids preparing liquid cultures for colonies that are later found to be
negative.

</ProtocolNote>

<ProtocolCalculations>

This section explains the calculations used in this protocol. The symbols are
defined for each equation, and the worked example uses the values printed in
the corresponding table.

**Volume of antibiotic stock for cultures and plates**

C1 × V1 = C2 × V2 → V1 = (C2 × V2) / C1

in which:

- C1 = concentration of the stock solution
- V1 = volume of stock solution to add
- C2 = required final concentration
- V2 = final volume of the solution

C1 and C2 must be expressed in the same unit before the values are entered;
1 mg/mL equals 1000 µg/mL.

**Worked example.** For gentamicin at 200 µg/mL in 2.5 mL of medium, from a
200 mg/mL (200,000 µg/mL) stock: V1 = (200 × 2.5) / 200,000 = 0.0025 mL =
2.5 µL. For erythromycin at 25 µg/mL in a 12 mL plate, from a 10 mg/mL
(10,000 µg/mL) stock: V1 = (25 × 12) / 10,000 = 0.030 mL = 30 µL.

**Inoculum for the subcultures**

*V*i = *V* / *D*

in which:

- *V*i = volume of overnight culture to add
- *V* = volume of fresh medium
- *D* = dilution factor (1 in *D*)

When one tube of recipient medium is prepared for several matings, *V* is the
volume for all matings together and the tube is divided once the required
optical density is reached.

**Worked example.** For a 1/100 dilution into 25 mL of LB: *V*i = 25 / 100 =
0.25 mL = 250 µL. For 8 matings of 2.5 mL recipient culture: 8 × 2.5 = 20.0 mL
of medium, inoculated with 20 / 50 = 0.40 mL = 400 µL of overnight culture.

**Diluting a concentrated buffer**

*V*s = *V*t / *F*  and  *V*w = *V*t − *V*s

in which:

- *V*s = volume of concentrated stock (mL)
- *V*t = total volume required (mL)
- *F* = concentration factor of the stock (×)
- *V*w = volume of distilled water to add (mL)

**Worked example.** For 570 mL of 1× PBS from a 10× stock: *V*s = 570 / 10 =
57.0 mL of 10× PBS and *V*w = 570 − 57 = 513.0 mL of distilled water.

**Colony counting and conjugation efficiency**

*C* = (*N* / *V*plated) × 10^*x* · *T* = *C* × *V*mating · *E* = *T* / *R*

in which:

- *N* = number of colonies counted (CFU)
- *V*plated = volume plated (mL)
- *x* = the dilution plated, as the exponent of 10⁻ˣ
- *C* = transconjugants per mL of the resuspended mating (CFU/mL)
- *V*mating = volume in which the mating was resuspended (mL)
- *T* = transconjugants in the whole mating (CFU)
- *R* = recipient cells in the mating (CFU)
- *E* = conjugation efficiency per recipient

Only a plate with roughly 30 to 300 colonies gives a reliable count: below that
the count is dominated by chance, above it the colonies merge. The efficiency
is expressed per recipient, so the recipient count must come from a plate of
the same mating on non-selective medium.

**Worked example.** For 48 colonies from 50 µL of the 10⁻⁴ dilution: *C* =
(48 / 0.05) × 10⁴ = 9.60 × 10⁶ CFU/mL. Resuspended in 100 µL this is
9.60 × 10⁵ transconjugants, and against 1 × 10⁸ recipients the efficiency is
9.60 × 10⁻³ per recipient.

**Resuspension of lyophilised primers**

*V* = (*n* / *c*) × 1000

in which:

- *V* = volume of MQ water for resuspension (µL)
- *n* = amount of primer stated on the tube (nmol)
- *c* = required stock concentration (µM)

A concentration of 100 µM equals 100 nmol/mL, so the amount in nmol multiplied
by 10 gives the volume in µL directly. The working dilution is then prepared
with the dilution equation C1 × V1 = C2 × V2.

**Worked example.** For a tube containing 38.2 nmol at a stock concentration of
100 µM: *V* = (38.2 / 100) × 1000 = 382 µL. For 100 µL of a 10 µM working
dilution: 100 × (10 / 100) = 10.0 µL of stock and 90.0 µL of MQ water.

</ProtocolCalculations>

- Leibniz Institute DSMZ. (n.d.-a). *Phocaeicola vulgatus* (Eggerth and Gagnon 1933) García-López et al. 2020, DSM 1447 [Strain record]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.dsmz.de/collection/catalogue/details/culture/DSM-1447
- Leibniz Institute DSMZ. (n.d.-b). *Phocaeicola vulgatus* strain H4_2, DSM 108222 [Strain record]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.dsmz.de/collection/catalogue/details/culture/DSM-108222
- Leibniz Institute DSMZ. (n.d.-c). *Phocaeicola vulgatus* strain H5_1, DSM 108228 [Strain record]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.dsmz.de/collection/catalogue/details/culture/DSM-108228
- Leibniz Institute DSMZ. (n.d.-d). *Phocaeicola vulgatus* strain H6_5, DSM 108234 [Strain record]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.dsmz.de/collection/catalogue/details/culture/DSM-108234

- Sigma-Aldrich. (n.d.-a). *Ampicillin sodium salt* (Product No. A9518) [Safety data sheet]. Retrieved October 11, 2026, from https://www.sigmaaldrich.com/BE/en/sds/sial/a9518
